# The second UI overhaul: build plan

*Jujutsu Kaisen: Cursed Bonds · written 6 Oct 2026 against branch `ui2` at c7a7728 (the game tree of main at 5f9da45, plus docs and the look-test tool). The owner's word is `docs/UI_OVERHAUL_2.md` ("SCOPE"; it wins over everything here). The audit is `docs/UI_AUDIT.md` ("AUDIT", 134 items, 54 questions). Phase 1 and 2's rules: `docs/PHASE1_DESIGN.md` 2.1 to 2.4 and `docs/PHASE2_DESIGN.md` ("PHASE2") sections 2, 6. What phase 2 really built and the lessons: `docs/PHASE2_HANDOFFS.md` ("HANDOFFS"). Paths are relative to `/Users/hunterjolly/Desktop/JJKgame.md/`. Line numbers are today's.*

This plan was built from three competing designs and three judges' verdicts. It takes the places, the schedule and the owner's latest rulings from the "living campus" design, the save safety and the smallest-risk mechanisms from the "smallest safe steps" design, and the separation of art from code and the proof for every picture from the "pictures first" design. Every factual error the judges found is fixed here; where they disagreed the code was read again and the choice is recorded in the Method note at the end. It was then put through an adversarial review (coverage, feasibility, art); each finding was checked against the code and the pictures, and what changed is item 15 of the Method note. A second verification (five lenses: the first review's fixes, the saves, the build, the art, the owner's page) followed, and the owner's answers of 6 Oct to the first three rulings were taken in; item 16. On 6 and 7 Oct the owner commented on the plan page (`docs/plan_pages/ui2-plan.html`); those rulings are owner rows O16 to O27 and are taken in throughout; item 17. That revision was then checked by two readers (the code, the art and the owner's page); what they found and what changed is item 18. On 7 Oct the owner answered the plan's one question, "Yes" (owner row O28): a painted pose for every person in every place they go, 35 more, 67 in all; item 19. On 7 Oct, with wave 0 half built, the owner asked for the picture itself to be what is clicked (owner row O34, D60); item 23. Nothing was run in Ren'Py to write this.

"D12" and the like are the numbered decisions of section 1. "K7" and the like are the ground rules of section 2. "U3" is a work package, "batch 9" an art sitting.

---

## 0. What this overhaul changes, in plain words

**What you will notice when you play**

- **Every place on campus is redone in the look you chose:** a real place, kept clean. Sixteen rooms are redone exactly where they stand, so the layout stays put (a few exits and actions are then moved onto the door or object they belong to); three of those gain one thing each (a laptop on my desk, a desk and photograph for Yaga, the veil beyond the ridge). The Courtyard is the gateway picture you chose, the Library the bright room you chose; the five yards, the Main Gate and all the maps are designed again. (The gate joins them because today's gate picture looks up at the old main hall, which your Courtyard no longer has.)
- **The campus becomes one place, pictures first and the map last** (your ruling). There is no drawing of the school to approve before pictures are made. The main locations are redone first, the way the Courtyard was: two versions, you choose. Each new picture is composed from the ones you have already chosen beside it, and each choice fixes a fact the pictures further out must agree with. When every place has its picture, the campus map and the six area maps are built round them, and you approve the map then. So the building on my left in a yard is the building on the left of the map, and every exit sits on the door or path it leads to (indoors, when the door is behind me, the way out sits at the bottom of the picture).
- **My room is inside the Boys' Dormitory.** From the garden I walk into the dormitory's corridor; my door is the one at the end. "Go to bed" works exactly as before, and your old saves load where they were.
- **Eleven new places:** the Boys' Dormitory corridor with Yuji's room, Megumi's room and the room Panda and Toge share; Nobara's and Maki's rooms off the girls' corridor; the Teachers' Lounge with the Teachers' Dormitory behind it; a mountain onsen with its sauna and cold plunge, up by the mountain path (the plan's "Bathhouse"; it is named Onsen in the game); the Swimming Pool behind the dojo. The two corridors and the teachers' house are painted with spare doors, so rooms can be added when new characters arrive without redoing a picture.
- **Night has people in it.** Everybody but Gojo now sleeps on campus, and at Night, the last hour of the day, everyone is in their own room, except that on two Nights a week someone reads late in the Library, with its lamps on (Megumi on Sundays, Toge on Wednesdays: your yes of 11 Oct). Some are awake and will talk; some are asleep. It changes through the week, so everyone can be found both ways. A friend's room opens once I know them well enough (a Bond level with them), and from then on at any hour, whether they are in, out or asleep. Someone behind a door I can't open yet shows on the map and in the journal as being at home.
- **Friends go into Tokyo at weekends.** On Sunday afternoons some of them are off campus, shopping. The journal and the map say which district; if I ride in with Ijichi I find them standing there, and can talk and spend the afternoon with them. They are back by the Evening.
- **People stand in the room, not on it.** Each place gets its own floor line and size for people, light measured from the picture, a shadow that leans the way the room's light falls, and furniture in front of people where there is furniture. When I look at one person the others stay in the room behind, dimmed.
- **People do things.** Every person has a real line for every place they go, **and a painted pose there too** (your ruling of 7 Oct): **70 in all** (the 67 you were told, and three that the late Nights of 11 Oct add: Megumi and Toge in the lamp-lit Library, and Panda asleep in the dark shared room). Each person in their main place; the students, Shoko and Gojo at Night; the teachers at home; each friend asleep, as a shape under a blanket; and now everywhere else their week takes them (Yuji in the training hall, Megumi in the forest with a Divine Dog, five painted in the cafeteria at lunch, each in a seat of their own, each friend in their room by day, the three in Tokyo on Sundays). Only people at the baths and the pool are never painted: they stand there as their usual sprites, in uniform. A painted person stays painted while I look at them, if a test in the first step of building allows it (otherwise as today).
- **Things to click everywhere.** Every place has at least one thing in the picture besides its exits. I can soak at the onsen, sit out the sauna and take its cold plunge, and swim at the pool. None of that is ever required: the baths only give their benefits. At the card table I can ask someone over.
- **The picture itself is what I click** (your request of 7 Oct). Once a place has its new picture, the square buttons that sit on it are gone. I rest the pointer on the storehouse and the storehouse lights up and says its name; I click and I walk there. The same goes for the things to do in a place (the striking posts, the card table, the vending machine). A place keeps its buttons until its new picture is in, so nothing is out of reach on an old or borrowed picture. Holding N, or switching on "Show names", lights up everything that can be clicked at once. The Map, Journal and menu buttons in the corners stay, and so does the column at the right edge (Wait, and anything that has no object of its own in the picture).
- **Every place is painted for every hour I can be in it:** its own evening and its own night, not a tint over the day picture (a tint leaves daylight in the windows).
- **All the missing backgrounds:** the prologue (alley, diner, home, street, car, city), the Sun Mall, the warehouse and the annexe, the board's job sites, the five Tokyo districts, the club and the Black Market, and a painted campus map.
- **Real people where there are block figures:** Mom, Dad, Kurose and Nade, and painted curses.
- **Screens:** the main menu designed as a screen of its own (its own picture, the game's name set properly, the menu laid out cleanly, a Continue button; you choose between three mock-ups); new saves that say "Day 9 · Tue · Evening · Cafeteria" (saves you already have keep their clock time); a "How to play" page and six small one-time hints; a "Show names" setting (or hold N); choices moved to the side so they stop covering faces.
- **Faces move again** in the character scenes (no new art).

**What isn't in it**

Combat and its screen. New story scenes, the "you" to "I" rewrite, and the storyboard's new cast, which come once the world is complete, as you said (Sukuna first, with his act). Rooms for the characters still to come (Nanami, Mei Mei, Miwa, Todo, Kamo and others): the spare doors are painted, the rooms are added when each arrives. Quests that use the rooms (taking something from one person's room for someone else): the plan leaves the three hooks such a quest needs and builds no quest, no item and no stealing. Tokyo shops to buy from and buildings to walk into. Outfits, music and sound, video, chess and the other table games, item and fish pictures. Section 11 lists each with its phase.

**What you have to do**

- **Nothing to answer before building.** Everything you were asked is answered; the last was the painted poses (7 Oct: "Yes").
- **Forty-seven small art sittings.** You approved twenty-four and the plan then counted twenty-eight. Going for the best quality adds eighteen, and this is where it costs you, in time more than money: nine more for the campus (every place painted for every hour, and two versions of every picture to choose from), seven more for poses (one for the teachers at home and each friend asleep; **six for your yes of 7 Oct,** a pose everywhere else), two more for the pictures off campus (two versions of each). Your ruling of 11 Oct, that a room has its lamps on at Night when someone is up in it, adds one more (batch 47: the friends' rooms dark, and the dojo lit). **If forty-seven is too many, say so:** choosing between two versions of every evening and night is about seven of the eighteen, and one version of those would make it about forty. The six sittings your yes added are the last six (batches 41 to 46); each is useful alone, so they can also stop after any one of them. Most sittings are: drag one file into ComfyUI, press Run, say which version you like. **Twelve renders or fewer each time,** each with a page of numbered steps. For a room redone where it stands, the tools install your choice at once and you see it in the game after relaunching. For a yard, the gate, a map or a new place, after you choose a version you say so in a session and its layout is done for you (about fifty minutes a picture) before it shows in the game. The tools, not you, check that nothing moved, vanished or was added, and say in plain words if a picture has to be run again ("the floor cushions by the low table are gone").
- **Some sittings are not one file.** In eighteen of them there are two files with a pause: run the first, tell a session which versions you choose (about ten minutes while it installs them; longer for a new place, where the second file can wait for another day), then run the second, which is made from the first (a room's evening and night from its new day picture, for example). Twelve more are two files with no pause (twelve renders are split so that no single run is over ten); five of those are among the six new pose sittings, and the sixth of them is one file. **None of the six new sittings has a pause.** Blink and talk (two sittings) is five short files each, one per person. Four sittings (Dad first, then Mom, Kurose and Nade; batches 37 to 40) are a different kind: a base picture made on Comfy Cloud, then two or three short repair runs in your own ComfyUI, choosing between versions after each. Allow an evening for Dad, less for the others.
- Four checkpoints played from the Ren'Py launcher. At checkpoint C you approve the maps and choose a main menu.

**What it costs**

About **446 renders, and about 25 small repair runs for the four new people, for about 240 installed pictures: about $76**; allow **$92** for pictures run a third time. Outside that count are two short runs for Yuji's new sprite (batches 0b and 0c: seven renders, about $1.12; D57) and the look tests you have already run. (You approved about $36, allow $43, before you said "Dont, worry about credits, we're going for best possible quality"; the rise is the three things that ruling takes back, listed under "Your rulings" below, two small ones (the six asleep shapes with Yaga's evening pose, and the Tokyo map), and the 35 poses you said yes to on 7 Oct: 70 renders, about $11.20; and, since 11 Oct, the rooms lit at Night: 12 renders, about $1.90.) Not yet in that count: the three poses the late Nights add (6 renders, about $1), which have no sitting yet. The campus itself (through checkpoint C, with batch 47) is about **$37** of that. The $76 does not include the Comfy Cloud plan itself, which you have said is active; the four people are batches 37 to 40, so it needs to be active then. Nothing in the game waits for any of it: until a picture exists the game shows what it shows today, or for a new place a clearly marked stand-in.

**Nothing is cut for cost.** The list of things that would have waited if credits ran short is gone. If you ever want to stop, everything after checkpoint C can stop at any point with the game whole.

**Your rulings.** Every question asked with the first draft, with the plan and on the plan page is answered, and nothing is waiting for you:

- *Whose rooms* (6 Oct): **all five** friends' rooms are built. *Comfy Cloud:* **active.**
- *The budget and the cut order* (6 Oct, "budget is fine"; then on the plan page, "Dont, worry about credits, we're going for best possible quality"): **quality first, O21.** The cut order is dropped. Wherever the plan had taken the cheaper of two ways to save money, it now takes the better: a painted evening and night for every place I can be in at that hour (36 more pictures); two versions to choose from for every picture, the simple rooms, the evenings and the nights included; painted poses for the teachers at home.
- *Where the new buildings hang* (O16, O17): the baths as a mountain onsen off the Mountain Path, with a sauna and a cold plunge; the pool off the Training Yard behind the dojo, built into the school so that it looks as if it belongs (you judge that when you choose its picture, and again on the map); the teachers' house off the staff steps; boys left, girls right.
- *When a friend's door opens* (O18, O19): at Night everyone is in their own room, some awake and some asleep (since 11 Oct, O47: except whoever is out late in the Library that Night). **The Bond level stays on doors:** a friend's room opens once Bond with its owner reaches a set level (15 for now, down from 30; the final numbers are decided when storyboarding starts), and then at any hour. The girls' corridor after dark asks the same of Nobara or Maki. A story quest can open any door whatever the Bond. Each friend is still at home for one daytime hour at weekends.
- *The campus from above* (O20): **pictures first, the map last.** No drawing is approved before pictures are made.
- *More rooms later* (O22), *friends in Tokyo at weekends* (O23), *the baths are optional* (O24), *the main menu is a screen to design* (O25): all in the plan as you said them.
- *Confirmed as shown* (O26): the four pictures already made ("way better"); "Living on campus"; the baths, the pool and the card invite; the picture defaults ("We can always add more. This is a good start"). *The story and the storyboard's cast* wait until the world is complete (O27).
- *A painted pose for every person in every place they go?* (7 Oct, "Yes"; O28): **all 35 are added, 67 in all.** What your yes buys and costs:
  - **35 more poses,** each in two versions for you to choose from: 70 renders, about **$11.20**.
  - **Six more sittings, forty-six in all.** They are the last six, batches 41 to 46, after the four new people. Five are two files with no pause and one is a single file; none has a pause. They are run in order, 41 first.
  - **The number you were given, 67, stands.** None of the 35 is at the Onsen, the Sauna or the Pool, so the rule that nobody is ever painted there removes none of them.
  - **What is still never painted,** by rules you have already approved: anyone at the Onsen or the Pool (six hours of the week: Nobara, Maki and Utahime in the onsen's rest hall on weekend Evenings; Yuji, Nobara and Panda on the pool's deck on weekend Afternoons), who stand there as their usual sprites, in uniform; Utahime and Ijichi asleep, who are behind their bedroom doors; Gojo when he wanders (his day is drawn by lot); and someone walking with me, or someone I asked over to the card table.
  - **The pose test still comes first.** Three painted people (Nobara, then Utahime and Megumi) are in the game at checkpoint C before any of the other sixty-four is made. If they do not sit right beside the standing ones, poses stop at three and none of this is bought.
  - **Each pose waits for the picture it is painted on,** so the two in Harajuku and the one in Shibuya come after Tokyo's pictures (batch 32). In Tokyo a friend stands as their usual sprite for the one line that says what they are doing, and is painted while I choose what to do with them.

**The characters (settled 7 Oct).** You looked again at Yuji, Maki, Shoko and Nobara. Only Yuji changes: he wears the school uniform, like Megumi's, with his red hood and red trainers (the picture you chose, F). His other five faces are one short run that you can press before the build starts (`yuji_edit.json`, five pictures, about $0.80); his blink and talking mouth are bought again later from the new face. All of it goes into the game in one step, so you never see half of him changed. Maki, Shoko and Nobara stay exactly as they are; the green speck in Maki's shoelace is gone.

**You will also be asked, later, while it is being built** (questions for you during development; players never see them): at batch 0, on the Courtyard and the Library, whether clicking the picture itself feels right (how strong the light is, whether anything is hard to find) before the other thirty-three places are done the same way; at checkpoint B whether Yuji's talking mouth looks right before ten more are made, and to read the list of new captions; at checkpoint C whether a painted person sits right beside the standing ones (three are made before sixty-four more), which of three main menu mock-ups you want, and whether the maps are right; at checkpoint D the review of titles, captions and numbers.

**What will look unfinished for a while (expected, not faults)**

- **The maps are made last.** Until batches 21 and 22 the area maps and the campus map show the old campus (the new places have pins on it, and every pin still takes me to the right place).
- **Buttons in some places and not in others.** A place loses its buttons once its new picture is in and the shapes I click have been drawn on it: the Courtyard and the Library at batch 0, the three yards at batch 1, each room as it is redone. Until batch 19 some places are clicked on the picture and others still carry buttons.
- From batch 0 until batches 1 and 2 (the first things made) the new Courtyard sits beside old yards that disagree with it (today's Training Yard has the hall on the other side), the doors to the four new buildings float on the old yard pictures, and the gate still looks up at a hall.
- At checkpoint B the eleven new places, the four you named among them, are still borrowed pictures (the Onsen is the infirmary tinted teal, the pool is the river cropped, friends' rooms are my room mirrored). Their own pictures are batches 9 to 11, 13, 16 and 19. If you would rather judge the onsen and the pool as themselves at checkpoint B, batches 16 and 19 can be run before it; they need only batches 1 and 2. (Run that early, the pool's evening picture waits for batch 7; its day picture does not.)
- Until a place's evening and night are painted it keeps today's tint at those hours. Area by area the tint goes.
- An asleep friend is a short "Asleep" tag at the bed and one line when I look, until the six blanket shapes are painted (batch 27).
- **Painted and standing people share rooms for a while.** Until batches 41 to 46, anyone in a place that is not yet on their list stands as their usual sprite, as today. So the cafeteria at lunch has one painted person (Yuji) from batch 23 and gains one more with each of batches 42, 43, 45 and 46; the three in Tokyo stand as sprites until batches 45 and 46.
- Nobara's painted pose on the sofa disappears when batch 0 goes in and comes back at batch 5. Utahime's disappears at batch 12 and comes back at batch 20.
- From batch 0 to batch 3 the Common Room's evening and night are its new day picture tinted, and one memory ("Eleven Tuesdays") still shows the old night picture.
- Between checkpoints A and B, Yuji, Nobara and Panda are at the pool on Saturday afternoons, but there is nothing to do there yet. In Tokyo the friends stand on today's flat district pictures until batch 32.
- In the eleven new places people stand on general spots, not on spots measured for the room, until each place has its own picture.
- The main menu looks as it does today, on today's gate picture (with a Continue button from checkpoint B), until you choose a mock-up at checkpoint C. From then it is the layout you chose, on the campus picture it was mocked up over, until its own picture is painted in batch 34.
- The Common Room and the Cafeteria, which you have approved, are run once more (batches 3 and 4) so their windows show the campus as the yards you chose have it. They may come back looking a little different, and you choose between two versions.
- Saves you already have keep their clock-time line; only new saves read "Day 9 · Tue · Evening · Cafeteria".

**Chosen for you; say the word and any of these changes** (each is one table entry or one prompt line; all 54 of the audit's questions are answered one by one in section 12.2)

- *Living on campus* (approved as shown, O26). Panda and Toge share a room; Maki is in the Girls' Dormitory. Yaga, Utahime and Ijichi live in the teachers' house; Shoko spends her nights in the infirmary on purpose; Gojo is never on campus at Night except on his own night. At Night everyone is in their own room, except Megumi on Sunday Nights and Toge on Wednesday Nights, who read late in the Library (your yes of 11 Oct, O47), and the garden and the Common Room are empty. On weekday Nights Yuji, Nobara and Toge are awake and Megumi, Maki and Panda asleep; at weekends it is the other way round. Yaga is up at his workbench every night; Utahime and Ijichi are asleep behind their bedroom doors; Shoko is awake. Someone asleep cannot be talked to and offers nothing; nothing more than that they are asleep is ever shown or said. (Yuji's weekend hour by the garden pond and Maki's at the onsen, which the plan had at Night, move to their weekend Evenings.)
- *Doors.* A friend's room asks Bond 15 with its owner (with either of them for the room Panda and Toge share), then it is open at any hour. The girls' corridor after dark asks Bond 15 with Nobara or Maki (today nothing ever opens it). A story quest can open any door. The teachers' sitting room is open at any hour; their bedrooms are doors. The pool's gate is chained at Night. I can always walk out of a room I am in. **In the first days, before any door is open, only Shoko and Yaga can be talked to at Night** (today it is three people: Yuji and Panda in the garden, and Shoko), and whoever is reading late in the Library, which has no door: Megumi on Sunday Nights, Toge on Wednesday Nights (O47). Everyone else shows on the map and in the journal as at home. That is what keeping the Bond level on doors costs at the start, and why the level is low; say the word and it is lower.
- *Weekends.* On Saturdays the river crowd splits: Yuji, Nobara and Panda swim at the pool. **On Sunday afternoons Nobara and Maki are in Harajuku and Yuji is in Shibuya** (the proposal; one table entry each), and they are back by the Evening. Spending the afternoon with one of them there gives Bond, as time together does on campus (a new number, on checkpoint D's list). Nobara, Maki and Utahime spend weekend Evenings at the onsen; Yuji is by the garden pond. The teachers use their lounge off duty, and Yaga is on campus at weekends except the afternoon.
- *The baths and the pool* (approved, O24, O26). **The baths are never required by anything and skipping them costs nothing; they only give their benefits.** Nobody is ever drawn or described in the bath, the sauna, the cold plunge or the pool's water, and nothing romantic is offered in those three places: people stand in the rest hall or on the deck as their usual sprites, and my soak or swim is a few lines about heat, cold and tiredness over the picture. One bath a day: a soak brings stamina back to full, the sauna cursed energy; each takes an hour, so both are closed at Night. (The two refills are new numbers; they are on checkpoint D's list.)
- *The card invite* (approved, O26). From the table: any student I have met who is on campus this hour, no Bond level, up to three friends at the table, free, not at Night. They walk over and sit down for that hour. In the Evening, when the three first years already fill the table, I can still ask one more over, and the one I asked sits first.
- *Pictures* ("a good start", O26). The veil is a small dark dome on the very top of the ridge, about the size of a hut: it holds one body, which is never shown. I see it from the Mountain Path, and from the ridge I can look at it close by (your ruling of 7 Oct, O36). Yaga's office keeps its doll hall and gains a low desk with the photograph. No car at the gate (it has a garden path, no road): Ijichi is "on the phone by the gate". A painted evening and night for every place I can be in at that hour (the three offices and the pool are shut at Night, and the sauna, a windowless box lit by its stove, is one picture). The dead "Later build" pins in Tokyo are hidden, and the Tokyo map is redone in the look.
- *People.* Poses are single figures, never groups. Faces go onto the campus character scenes now; the "you" lines are not touched. Lines are proposed for every "what they're doing" caption and you approve them at checkpoint B.
- *Screens.* The main menu is designed as a whole screen (O25). At checkpoint C you are shown three mock-ups, each a real screenshot with the name set and the menu laid out, over three pictures the campus already has by then: the Main Gate at blue hour; the gateway Courtyard at night with its lanterns lit; the ridge at night with the veil on its peak. The one you choose gets a picture painted for it. A small card when the day turns; the hour and yen stay off the HUD. The red seal stays as the game's mark; buttons become icons; one Close everywhere. Notes about later builds are hidden where they are only notes (the journal's extra rows, the "combat update" lines, the dead Tokyo pins); the cards that mark where the story really stops for now stay.
- *Clicking the picture* (your request, O34). Nothing is marked until the pointer is on it. The light is a soft warm white with a brighter edge, and the name opens beside it as a button's name does today. A locked door still lights, more faintly, and says what it asks. A thing that is used up for the day (the vending machine after one can) stops lighting until tomorrow, as its button disappears today. **A thing stops answering while a person's picture covers its point** (the picture is wider than the person: about 395 by 728 px). A way out or a thing to do is then in the column at the right edge, as a hidden button is today; a thing to look at waits until they move. One or two people standing in a place never hide anything; a person painted into the room hides the thing they are painted at. You first see it at batch 0, on the Courtyard and the Library, in a played game (in explore mode only the ways out and the things to look at answer); say then if you want a stronger or weaker light. A shimmer over everything when I walk into a place, or a thing staying clickable with someone standing in front of it, are yours to ask for at checkpoint B, when the yards have people in them; either would be built late, with the integration. The maps keep their pins: clicking a building on a map is not in this overhaul.
- *Left as they are, though more money would buy more* (each is time, not money; say the word): a blink and a talking mouth on every face, not only the calm one (about 110 more renders, ten sittings); a picture of its own for each of the board's eight jobs, where five kinds are shared (6 renders, one sitting); the maps painted for the evening and the night, where they are tinted by the hour (28 renders, three sittings). (A second Night picture of each friend's room, dark for when they are asleep, was on this list; it is taken now, by your ruling of 11 Oct: batch 47.)
- *Not now:* chess, memory and tic-tac-toe; outfits; music; video; item pictures; a paper or phone view; one-off moment pictures; the storyboard's cast; the lake, the basement, Yaga's workshop.

---

## 1. Decisions

"From" says where the idea came from: C, S, P are the three designs (living campus, smallest safe steps, pictures first); J-owner, J-code, J-art are the judges; "code" means it was settled by reading the game. "Pkg" is the package or batch that builds it.

### 1.2 Places and the map

## 2. Ground rules

### 2.1 Carried over, unchanged

PHASE1 2.1 to 2.4 and PHASE2 2.1 to 2.4 stand as written:

- Protected statements stay textually identical (PHASE2 2.3's list, with `room_paths.rpy`'s `label path_walk` and all 24 `label sb_do_path_<to>`). New branches are new lines; new labels are **appended** to the end of their file.
- No label is deleted. No store variable is renamed. New state is `default` and holds only plain values. Nothing un-picklable is saved or shown (R10).
- The owner's `game/*.rpyc` are never deleted, moved or force-recompiled. Worktrees get `cp -p` copies and never copy back.
- Screens keep their names, parameters and return tuples. An action id is never renamed (the loop calls `"sb_do_" + id`).
- First person; no literal protagonist name; Kaito never on screen. New text passes `check_content.py --narration-person`.
- R1 prefixes, R2 checks, R3 frozen return values, R4 screen actions, R5 fixtures from constructors, R6 lanes, R8 drop-in art, R9 the motion budget, R10 picklability. PHASE2's D43 (never edit a `call` and the statement after it in one phase) and D51 (a failed package gets one retry with its log, then the owner is asked; a cut of something the owner decided is the owner's call).
- **Frozen for the whole overhaul** (only integration, U10, may touch them, and it expects to change none): `sb_loop.rpy`, `sb_tokyo.rpy`, `sb_missions.rpy`, `sb_events.rpy`, `sb_hooks.rpy`, `explore.rpy`, `script.rpy`, `sb_combat.rpy`, `jjk_screens.rpy`, `sb_threads.rpy`, and every `ch*`, `story_*`, `missions_*` and `events_*` file except the lines U9 lists (8.1).

### 2.2 New rules, each designing out a known way to fail

Lettered K so they are not mistaken for packages (U) or phase 2's rules (R).

**K1. Home is the key `dorm`, for ever.** The place key `dorm`, its image name `bg your_room`, its files `your_room*.png`, its label `sb_do_path_dorm` and `default here = "dorm"` never change. No HOME constant is introduced.

**K2. A picture carries its numbers.** Every number measured on a picture or judged from one (exit points, hotspot points, the shapes that are clicked, hover-label sides, standing slots, the floor, a door for scale, pose boxes, foreground outlines, light rows, map pins, the Mountain marker's box) lives in `game/layout/` or `tools/fg/`, in a file named for the picture. Tables in `.rpy` files hold only schematic defaults that lint clean. **Installing a picture changes files under `game/images/`, `game/gui/`, `game/layout/`, `tools/fg/`, `tools/bgs/` and nothing else:** no `.rpy` file, no harness. (The one exception is as old as the pipeline: installing a new person's sprite regenerates `game/jjk_sprites.rpy`.)

**K3. Harnesses pin rules, not coordinates.** A check on an exit, slot, pin or polygon asks "inside the frame, clear of the lanes, on the floor, apart from its neighbours", never "equals (1640, 850)". A count of art-dependent rows is read from its table, never written as a literal. U0a makes this change once, on the untouched tree: each rewritten check must give the verdict it gave before, **and must fail when its sidecar is deliberately broken** (one negative test per rule). A literal coordinate added to a harness later is a review finding.

**K4. Pictures first, the map last.** No drawing of the campus is approved before pictures are made (O20). No outdoor prompt is written by hand; every one is built by a tool from the campus record, `tools/bgs/campus.json` (5.2): the place's own words, the facts fixed so far, and the chosen pictures of its neighbours as references. **A fact is fixed only by a picture the owner has chosen.** A candidate that contradicts a fixed fact is marked before the owner chooses and is not installable, except by a waiver the owner accepts at the last rung of 5.2's ladder, and the tool says which fact in plain words; a chosen picture that differs from a mere proposal changes the proposal, and the status list marks every unmade picture that proposal touches. The six area maps and the campus map are made last: the tool refuses a map while a place on it has no chosen picture.

**K5. One look.** `tools/bgs/look.txt` is the only place the look is written; every maker reads it. A redo in place puts the keep text in front (`keep.txt` indoors, `keep_outdoor.txt` outdoors; `keep_light.txt` after it for a day redo only; 5.1). `tools/check_prompts.py` fails a prompt whose **per-picture lines** contain a banned word or phrase (5.1 says exactly which lines it reads; the frozen texts are reviewed once, as wholes). The look text and `keep.txt` are frozen after batch 3, `keep_outdoor.txt` after batch 8.

**K6. A picture is installed only by the tool that proves it.** `tools/install_redo.py`: for a redo in place, nothing moved, nothing vanished, nothing was added, sharper than before; for a new composition, a layout that passes the screen's rules. A picture refused twice in place is made as a new composition and laid out again, and the checkpoint note says so. `install_bg.py` is never used for a redo (its fit drifts 7 px at the edges), nor `install_pose.py`'s colour rule (it reads 44 to 56% "changed" on a good redo).

**K7. Nothing stale is shown.** A foreground, an evening or night picture, a pose or a light row made for a room's old picture is not used once that room's picture is replaced; the game falls back to what it does when the file is missing (the day picture tinted, the standing sprite, the general light). Foregrounds are recut by the installer in the same run. An hour picture in a room with a foreground is not used until its foreground exists. Written scenes and memory rows that name an hour picture themselves bypass this (`scene bg your_room night`, five times today and nowhere once U9 has pointed all five at `world_lit_bg("dorm")`, which is gated: 8.1, 5.7a; `scene bg rooftop evening`, twice; and `sb_memories.rpy:182-253`, which stage memories on `bg your_room night`, `bg rooftop evening`, `bg rooftop night`, `bg infirmary night` and `bg dorm_common night`, before U9 and after it), so **for those four rooms (My Room, the rooftop, the infirmary, the Common Room) the day picture and its hour pictures are always made in one sitting** (and 5.7 says what happens if both versions of one of those five relights are refused). Everywhere else a place's evening and night may follow in a later sitting, and until they do the gates show its day picture tinted, as today. The one known gap is the Common Room between batch 0 and batch 3 (its day picture is already made): until then the memory "Eleven Tuesdays" shows the old night picture; the checkpoint A note says so and `art_status.py` lists it under "half-made room sets".

**K8. Order is enforced by the tools.** An hour picture cannot be made before its day picture is installed in the new look; a pose cannot be made before the picture it is painted on; **a place's picture cannot be made before the chosen picture of the yard it stands off, working outwards from the Courtyard** (a yard before the buildings and rooms that stand off it; the first ring of yards needs only the Courtyard, which is chosen. **A room inside a house waits for the chosen picture of the yard its house stands on, not for the corridor's.** Inside a house the order is the batch table's "needs first", which `art_batch.py` holds, not a rule of the maker's: My Room is redone in place in batch 3 and is the Boys' Dormitory's reference, and a house and one room inside it may be made in one file, as in 9a, 13a and 19a); a map cannot be made before every place on it has its picture (K4). The maker refuses and says what is missing. (There is no rule about lenders: nothing lends any more, D22.)

**K9. Nothing new in a saved sprite.** `lit_sprite`'s displayable tree, `_sprite_light`, `_shadow_light`, `view_blink_fn`, `view_talk_fn`, `view_breathe_fn`, `hot_pulse_fn`, `view_enter_ease`, `char_focus` and every `pos_*` transform keep their names, signatures and shapes. Blending is done by data those functions already read, or in `view_sprite` and the stage screens, which never reach a save. Files that sit inside saves keep their paths: every `images/char/...` file, `images/fx/contact_shadow.png`, and **every `images/fg/fg_*.png` that has ever been installed** (a save made on a dialogue line in a room that keeps its foreground up holds that file's path). The installer never deletes one; a room that loses its foreground gets a fully transparent file of the same name, and `art_status.py --check` reports a missing one.

**K10. Story files: one package, alone.** Only U9 opens a story file. It may change the image name on the twelve listed `scene` lines (on My Room's five the name gives way to `expression world_lit_bg("dorm")`, each line keeping its own `with`: O47, 5.7a), change the picture name that is the fourth argument of the two listed `call combat` lines (8.1; the one stated exception to PHASE2's D43, safe because a call's identity is its label and ignores its arguments), add the one listed `scene` line, and add say attributes; no quoted text changes, no statement is removed or moved, no menu, flag or reward line and no other call is touched. Each of its two commits is right the first time (a line reworded, compiled and put back has lost its name for good), and is proved by the names check (8.1) in a tree whose `.rpyc` are copies of the owner's.

**K11. Budgets are raised once, on purpose, in the contracts file:**

| budget | today | this overhaul | pinned at |
|---|---|---|---|
| places | 24 | **35** | `p2_map.rpy:350`, `p2_paths.rpy:393` (both become `len(LOC_ORDER)`) |
| markers on level 1 | 6 | 6 | unchanged (`p2_map.rpy:272, 282`; `wp1_world.rpy:241-243`) |
| pins on an area view | 5 | **6** (Dormitories only) | `p2_map.rpy:352` (buttons ≤ 10 becomes ≤ 11) |
| words on an area view | ≤ 16 | **≤ 18** (Dormitories and Staff are 17: the plates, the title, Back's "Jujutsu High" and the date chip all count) | `p2_map.rpy:351` |
| exits from a yard | 7 (the garden) | 7 (the garden); the staff steps and the Training Yard 6, the Mountain Path 5 | `p2_paths.rpy:316, 483, 1089` become "at most 7 and equal to its table" |
| exits from a house (a corridor, the lounge, the Onsen) | – | at most 7 (the boys' corridor 5, the girls' corridor 3, the lounge and the Onsen 2); the room for the rooms still to come (4.1, D50) | `path_validate`; a new line in `u2_home` |
| tiles in the icon column with people in the place | ≤ 8 | ≤ 8, **not loosened** (`p2_paths.rpy:739-740`) | 4.6 says how each yard is laid out to keep it |
| icons | 88 | **114** | `p2_contracts.rpy:1052` becomes "the count the sheets hold" |
| faces on the map | 3 a pin, 18 in all | unchanged | `p2_map.rpy:299-301` |

**K12. A new place is walkable on a stand-in before its picture exists,** and its stand-in is a borrowed, mirrored, tinted picture that cannot be mistaken for the real room. Until a yard is repainted, an exit to a new building sits at a clear point on the old yard picture, on the proposed side where the old picture has that side and otherwise wherever it is clear; the old exits stay on today's sides. The checkpoint note says so, and `check_campus.py` reports such a yard as waiting for its batch, not as wrong (5.2).

**K13. Phase 2's four lessons, as rules.**
- *A failing check that is a hand-off is not a failure.* U0a writes `docs/UI2_PINS.md`, the ledger of every harness line this overhaul is known to change (10.3), each with the package that changes the behaviour and the package that owns the harness that wave. A ledger line that fails for its listed reason is logged with the exact edit, proven in a scratch copy, and passed to its owner.
- *Pointer harnesses run alone:* `p2_contracts`, `p2_hud`, `p2_map`, `p2_paths`, `p2_view` and the new `u2_map`, one at a time, no game window open, never from two worktrees at once. This is enforced, not asked: `tools/smoke.sh` takes a machine-wide lock (a directory under `~/Library/Caches/`) before a harness on the pointer list, waits for it, and refuses with one line while a window of this game is open. A hover failure is rerun alone before it is believed.
- *Review rounds are capped.* A package gets at most two review rounds; a third happens only for a finding about a save, a crash, a contract or the owner's word. Wording goes on the owner's reading list.
- *Everything a reviewer needs is in the repository:* the plan, the look, the manifest, the baselines, the keep lists; the old pictures come from git (`git show <commit>:game/images/bg/<file>`); the nineteen made saves are committed under `tools/smoke/assets/ui2_saves/`, and the snapshot of the owner's eleven is named, with a checksum for each, in `docs/load_report_ui2_wave0.txt`. `tools/review_pack.sh <package>` regenerates that package's layout sheets, stage sheets and screenshots. Nothing lives only in a scratch folder.

**K14. Two Pythons; paid runs are the owner's.** A tool that needs numpy or Pillow starts through `tools/py_art.sh`, which finds ComfyUI's Python (`~/ComfyUI-Installs/ComfyUI/standalone-env/bin/python`) or prints one clear line and exits. A harness that needs such a tool prints `SKIPPED (no ComfyUI python)`: a pass for a code package, a failure for an art sitting. A tool writes the workflow and the note; the owner presses Run. No tool queues a paid node.

**K15. An art sitting never writes a file an open worktree owns.** Light rows are in their own file (`light_<place>.json`) and pose boxes in theirs (`pose_<place>.json`), so that installing a room or a pose during a wave touches nothing a package is editing. The `hot_` and `view_` files of rooms redone in place are written by L's in-place layout sittings (10.2), which no code package shares, and carry no `for_rev` (3.2), so an install never has to rewrite them. Code packages test with sidecars of their own under `tools/smoke/assets/`.

**K16. The baths, the pool and the bedrooms.** No picture, pose, caption, look or line added by this overhaul shows or describes anyone undressed, in a towel, in swimwear or bathing, or dwells on anyone's body. My own soak, sauna and swim are told as heat, cold, water and tiredness. Anyone else in those places is in the rest hall or on the deck, dressed as their sprite. No pose is ever made at the Bathhouse, the Sauna or the Pool (`make_pose_workflow.py` refuses such a row). The pose prompt's sentence that keeps a person's "face, hair, clothes and colours exactly as in the second picture" (`make_pose_workflow.py:122`) is frozen. The romance, confession and intimate activities are not offered while I stand in the Bathhouse, the Sauna or the Pool. `check_content.py --decency` (U0a) runs a word list (towel, naked, undress, strip, swimsuit, bikini, skin, bare, changing) over `room_bath.rpy`, `room_pool.rpy` `room_tokyo.rpy`, and every `AMBIENT`, `SPOT_LINES` and `POSES` row whose place is one of those three or a friend's room; a hit is a failure for the package that wrote the line.

**The asleep (O18).** Nothing shows or describes an asleep person beyond that they are asleep: a short tag at the bed ("Asleep"), one plain line when I look ("Megumi is asleep. I leave him be."), and, once painted, a shape under a blanket, turned away, with no face, limb or skin drawn (the pose tool writes that sentence itself for a row whose `state` is `asleep`, and the sentence is frozen). Someone asleep is not on the stage, so nothing at all is offered with them, the romance kinds included. A mission of theirs, which the place view's column would offer from the room and not from their view, is left out of the column and off the map while they are asleep (D48). **Nothing is started by walking into a room at Night, or into a room whose owner is out or asleep:** no scene, beat, activity or line is keyed to entering a friend's room in this overhaul, and `u2_world` checks that entering each of the five rooms at Night calls nothing. Teachers asleep are behind their bedroom doors and are never shown. A caption in Tokyo says what a friend is doing in the street, dressed as their sprite.

**K17. The baths are optional (O24).** Nothing in the game may require a soak, a sauna or a swim, or take anything away for skipping one: no objective, mission, story beat, grade, lock, schedule cell, daily need, stat decay or penalty reads whether I bathed. The key `"bath"` in `actions_today` is read by the two bath actions and by nothing else. They only give their benefits (4.7). `check_content.py --baths-optional` (U0a) greps the `.rpy` files under `game/` for `"bath"`, `bath_soak`, `bath_sauna`, `bath_ok` and `pool_swim` outside `room_bath.rpy` and `room_pool.rpy`. A hit is a failure unless it is one of exactly these, which the plan itself puts there: a row of `ICONS` or `ICON_FOR` in `ui_icons.rpy`; a row of `SPOT_PLANNED` in `sb_hotspots.rpy`; a `register_hotspot` row for one of those ids; the tuple `NIGHT_REST_BLOCKS` in `sb_night.rpy`. It has one negative test: a line reading `"bath" in actions_today` in a scratch `sb_missions.rpy` must fail. `u2_rooms` plays thirty days twice **on one script of actions**: one run bathes once a day, and the other presses Wait in the same hour (a bath spends an hour, so without that the two clocks part and everything after differs for a reason that has nothing to do with the rule). Afterwards the flags, `done_events`, every mission's status, the grade, every `place_access` answer, the objective's text and `who_is_here` for every place and hour are equal; only stamina, cursed energy and the string `"bath"` in `actions_today` may differ. A later phase that wants a reason to bathe adds a benefit, never a need.

### 2.3 Statements that become protected in this overhaul

From the commit each lands in, these stay textually identical, each with the line after it.

| file | statements |
|---|---|
| `room_paths.rpy` | the eleven appended labels `sb_do_path_boys_dorm`, `_yuji_room`, `_megumi_room`, `_senpai_room`, `_nobara_room`, `_maki_room`, `_teachers_lounge`, `_teachers_dorm`, `_bathhouse`, `_sauna`, `_pool`, each with its line `call path_walk("<to>")` |
| `room_cards.rpy` | `call screen cards_invite_pick` / `$ _cards_i = _return` |
| `room_bath.rpy` | `label sb_do_bath_soak`, `label sb_do_bath_sauna` (their names; no menu and no called screen inside) |
| `room_pool.rpy` | `label sb_do_pool_swim`, `label act_swim_pool(who)` (their names) |
| `room_tokyo.rpy` | `label district_harajuku`, `label district_shibuya` (the frozen `label sb_trip` calls them by name), `label tky_people(key)`, and inside it `call screen tky_meet(key)` / `$ _tky = _return` |
| `sb_night.rpy` | the one new line `scene expression night_club_bg(key)` (U9), once it lands |

Action ids that never change once shipped: `path_<key>` for the eleven keys, `bath_soak`, `bath_sauna`, `pool_swim`, `cards_invite`; and the activity id `swim_pool` (the loop calls `"act_" + id`). Labels that never change once shipped: `district_harajuku`, `district_shibuya` (the frozen `sb_trip` calls `"district_" + key`) and `tky_people`.

## 3. Data model

### 3.1 Tables changed (init data, never saved)

| table | file | change |
|---|---|---|
| `LOCATIONS`, `LOC_ORDER` | `sb_world.rpy` | +11 rows, appended, each written `"<key>": dict(indoor=..., name="...", bg="bg <key>", ...)` in exactly that order (three tools find a place's picture by that pattern). The Library's `desc` is rewritten for a bright room. |
| `AREAS[…]["places"]`, `["spots"]`, `["blurb"]` | `sb_world.rpy` | new keys appended to `dorms`, `staff`, `grounds`, `mountain`; `spots` holds only places that are not in `WORLD_PINLESS`; the `dorms` blurb loses "where everyone ends up by evening". The six `anchor`s do not change. |
| `SCHEDULE`, `WEEKEND` | `sb_world.rpy` | 4.3. Every Night cell of `SCHEDULE` for someone who lives on campus becomes their home; `WEEKEND` gains **no** Night cell (the Night cells are the same on all seven days; the two late stays of 5.7a are a table of their own, `WORLD_LATE`, read after the away table, and no cell) and gains the Evening and daytime cells of 4.3 |
| `TIME_VARIANT` | `sb_world.rpy` | the static rows stay as the fallback; at init a `WORLD_VARIANTS` row whose file exists and is fresh sets its cell, **replacing a static cell where there is one** (My Room's Evening is `night` today, `sb_world.rpy:326`, and becomes `evening` when that picture lands) |
| `place_access`, `char_location`, `person_now` | `sb_world.rpy` | 4.2 (return shapes unchanged) |
| `stage_people`, `ambient_of`, `rail_missions` | `sb_screens.rpy` | `stage_people` leaves out anyone for whom `world_asleep` is true (D48), so every screen that draws, lists or offers people takes the rule from one place. `ambient_of` gives a sleeper the caption "asleep": the test is the function's first statement, ahead of the story caption (`rail_story_caption` is returned first today, `:167-169`), and it returns the pair `("asleep", None)`. `rail_missions` leaves out a mission whose giver (`m["with_who"] or m["who"]`, as `_ms_giver` reads it) is `world_asleep`, because the frozen `_ms_startable` finds the giver in `who_is_here` (`sb_missions.rpy:689`), which still counts a sleeper. Three lines, laid by U0b with the table they read |

Laid by U0b, which has the file in wave 0 |

So from checkpoint A to batch 5 nothing changes on any exit and no column drops to six tiles. `path_validate` keeps its test on the fallback name. |
