### 3.2 New tables, functions and files

| name | where | what |
|---|---|---|
| `WORLD_INSIDE` | `sb_world.rpy` | `{room: the room it opens off}`, one level deep: `dorm`, `yuji_room`, `megumi_room`, `senpai_room` → `boys_dorm`; `nobara_room`, `maki_room` → `girls_dorm`; `teachers_dorm` → `teachers_lounge`; `sauna` → `bathhouse`. `world_inside(loc)` returns the house's key or None. |
| `WORLD_PINLESS` | `sb_world.rpy` | `("yuji_room", "megumi_room", "senpai_room", "nobara_room", "maki_room", "teachers_dorm", "sauna")`: reached on foot or by "Go there"; no pin. `dorm` is **not** in it. `world_pinned(loc)` → bool. |
| `WORLD_HOMES` | `sb_world.rpy` | `{who: place}`: Yuji, Megumi, Nobara, Maki to their rooms; Panda and Toge to `senpai_room`; Yaga, Utahime, Ijichi to `teachers_dorm`; Shoko to `infirmary`. Gojo has no row. `world_home(who)`. The table and the function are U0b's, with the rows for the four boys and Shoko (the asleep function reads them); U0c1 adds the other five rows. |
| `WORLD_VARIANTS` | `sb_world.rpy` | every planned new hour picture as `(place, period, attribute, file)`. At init a row whose file is loadable and fresh registers its image with `renpy.image` and sets its `TIME_VARIANT` cell (over a static cell too); a row whose file is missing does nothing. A new check reports any `TIME_VARIANT` cell with no image. |
| `SPOT_PLANNED` | `sb_hotspots.rpy` (a `prefixes.json` exception: the file's prefixes are `hot_` and `mg_`) | every hotspot this overhaul will register, as `(id, place, kind)`, for all 35 places (7.3's looks and moved actions, the new places' actions). Frozen in the contracts file. `hot_validate` and `check_layout.py` accept a sidecar point for a planned id that is not registered yet, so a layout sitting before wave 1 places every point once. `u2_things` checks that wave 1 registered exactly these. |
| `hot_shapes_for(loc)`, `hot_tip_rect(key)`, `HOT_SHAPE` | `sb_hotspots.rpy` | D60. `hot_shapes_for` returns `{hotspot id: [polygon, ...]}` for a shaped place and `{}` for any other (no `shapes` key, a sidecar whose `for_rev` does not match, or a picture still at revision 1: `art_rev` of the file stem of `LOCATIONS[loc]["bg"]`, which is `your_room` for `dorm`, K1); a polygon is a list of `[x, y]`. `hot_tip_rect` returns the 72 px square at a shaped hotspot's point for either of its tooltip keys, `hot_<id>` (the square) and `hotshape_<id>` (the shape), and None for any other key: the hover label opens against that square whichever of the two has the focus. `HOT_SHAPE` holds the limits both validators read (points, area, overlap) and the light's default colour and strengths, which `game/layout/hot_look.json` may replace |
| `WORLD_DOOR_BOND`, `world_door_bond(loc)`, `world_door_owners(loc)`, `world_welcome_check()` | `sb_world.rpy` | the door table (4.2): `{None: 15}`, the one entry every door reads today; a later row `{"maki_room": 25}` would set one door apart (the storyboard's numbers). `world_door_owners` returns whose Bond a door asks (`girls_dorm` → Nobara, Maki; `senpai_room` → Panda, Toge). The check runs from the new-day hook and, the first morning a door's level is reached, shows that door's toast and sets its flag (`invited_girls_dorm` for the corridor, `welcome_<room>` for a room) |
| `WORLD_ASLEEP`, `world_asleep(who, per=None)` | `sb_world.rpy` | `{"weekday": ("megumi", "maki", "panda", "utahime", "ijichi"), "weekend": ("yuji", "nobara", "toge", "utahime", "ijichi")}`. The function is the only reader: True when it is Night, the person is on the list for today, they are in their `WORLD_HOMES` place by routine (no story pin, no flag schedule) and they are not my companion. The lists above are the finished ones: U0b lays the table with the four boys' names, and U0c1 adds Nobara, Maki, Utahime and Ijichi as it adds their homes, so the table is whole at every commit. **`_validate_world` gains these rules** (U0b for the tables it lays, U0c1 for `WORLD_AWAY`): every name on an asleep list has a `WORLD_HOMES` row; every `WORLD_HOMES` place exists; for each `WORLD_HOMES` person `SCHEDULE`'s Night cell is their home, and `WEEKEND` has no Night cell for anyone; every `WORLD_DOOR_BOND` key is None or a place; every `WORLD_AWAY` district is in `DISTRICTS`. So a row appended later (4.1) cannot be half made |
| `world_home_state(who, per=None)`, `world_room_state(loc)` | `sb_world.rpy` | a hook for later quests (D49): `"awake"`, `"asleep"` or `"out"` for a person against their home; `{owner: state}` for a room. Read by the bed tags, the name plates' lines and the map's pins now |
| `WORLD_AWAY`, `world_away(who, per=None)` | `sb_world.rpy` | who is in Tokyo (4.3, 7.6): `{who: {(weekday, period): district}}`, today `nobara` and `maki` → `("Sun", "Afternoon"): "harajuku"`, `yuji` → `("Sun", "Afternoon"): "shibuya"`. `char_location` reads it in its routine branch and answers None (off campus), so story pins and flag schedules still win; `world_away` returns the district key, or None when the person is not there |
| `tky_here(key)`, `tky_caption(who, key)`, `TKY_LINES`, `TKY_BOND`, `screen tky_meet(key)`, `label tky_people(key)` | `game/room_tokyo.rpy` (new; prefix `tky_`, with a `prefixes.json` exception for the `district_` labels) | 7.6 |
| `map_district_people(key)` | `ui_map.rpy` | the friends who are in a district this hour (7.6: the Afternoon only); the district's marker and its view draw their faces. The "will be here this afternoon" line, in the Morning and at Noon, names the friends the table sends there later that day and draws no face |
| `art_rev(stem)`, `art_fresh(stem)`, `art_validate()` | `game/art_manifest.rpy` (new; prefix `art_`) | the manifest reader (5.5) |
| `path_keep_clear(loc)` | `room_paths.rpy` | list of (x, y) |
| `light_row(loc, variant)`, `light_follow(row, grade)`, `light_validate()` | `lighting.rpy` | 6.2 |
| `view_floor(loc)`, `screen view_others(loc, who)` | `view_stage.rpy` | 6.2 |
| `bath_*` | `game/room_bath.rpy` (new) | Soak and Sauna, their labels and lines |
| `pool_*` | `game/room_pool.rpy` (new) | Swim, its label and lines; `label act_swim_pool(who)`, the shared swim's own scene on `bg pool` (a `prefixes.json` exception for the one `act_` label) |
| `hint_*` | `game/ui_hints.rpy` (new) | the six hints, `screen hint_layer`, `default hint_seen` |
| `cards_invitable()`, `screen cards_invite_pick` | `room_cards.rpy` | 7.4 |
| `standin_*` | `jjk_defs.rpy` (gains a `prefixes.json` row for this prefix only) | the guarded stand-in block (8.2) |
| `sys_*`, `gui.sys_*` | `screens.rpy`, `gui.rpy` | new helper screens and tokens for 8.3 |

**Sidecars** (`game/layout/`, all read through `rule_layout`; shapes frozen in the contracts file and checked by a new rule, `check_content.py --layout-files`):

| file | written by | keys |
|---|---|---|
| `hot_<place>.json` | a layout sitting (L): the picture's own for a composed picture or a new place, the in-place series beside wave 1 for a room redone in place | `spots` (hotspots by id; exits by `go_<from>__<to>`, the reader exists today), `tips` (hover-label sides), `shapes` (D60: for a hotspot or exit id, the one to three polygons that are clicked in its place, each a list of `[x, y]`), `keep_tile` (D60: `{id: reason}`, the few that keep a button in a shaped place), `spare` (the three houses only: the points of painted doors that lead nowhere yet, D50; read by `check_layout.py` and by nothing in the game), `for_rev` (composed pictures and new places only) |
| `view_<place>.json` | a layout sitting (L), the same way | `slots`, `poses` (today's two keys; still read, for the two poses that exist), `floor`, `scale`, `slot_light`, `cast`, `talk`, `for_rev` (composed pictures and new places only) (6.1) |
| `hot_look.json` | L, on the owner's word | D60: the light's colour and its strengths (under the pointer, its edge, dimmed, while names are shown); every key optional, `HOT_SHAPE`'s defaults otherwise. It is how the owner's "stronger" or "weaker" is answered once `sb_hotspots.rpy` is final |
| `pose_<place>.json` | `install_pose.py` only | `poses`: the pose boxes, read by `view_pose` before the `view_` file's own `poses` key |
| `light_<place>.json` | the installer, from the picture | `{"": row, "evening": row, "night": row}`, each row four or six values; a room with a lit picture gains a row under the key `"lit"` (U0n, 5.7a) |
| `light.json` | the installer | rows for story pictures, keyed by image name (`"bg alley"`) |
| `area_<key>.json` | a layout sitting | `spots` (as today), `for_rev` |
| `map_campus.json` | a layout sitting | `anchors` (optional nudges), `mountain_box` |
| `faces.json` | `install_face.py` only | `{"blink_also": {who: [faces]}}`, from its measurement of the eye box's rim; read by `view_face_layers`, with `VIEW_BLINK_ALSO` as the fallback |
| `pictures.json` | the installer only | the manifest (5.5) |

A sidecar that carries `for_rev` is ignored, with a lint line, when the installed picture is at another revision. **Only a layout written by `install_redo.py --compose` carries `for_rev`.** The sidecars of the sixteen rooms redone in place carry none: the "nothing moved" proof is what keeps them valid from revision to revision, and with a `for_rev` every later in-place install of the room (its window rerun, an "again") would silently drop its slots, floor and points back to the code's defaults. `check_content.py --layout-files` rejects `for_rev` in a `hot_` or `view_` file whose place is not `composed` in the manifest. **`shapes` are read only on the new picture** (D60): for a place whose picture is at revision 2 or later. A room redone in place has its shapes traced at its in-place layout sitting, on the picture it shows that day, and they wait in its `hot_` file until its new picture is in, so no room loses its buttons while it still shows its old picture.

### 3.3 What is saved, and what a save can hold

| new store variable | default | holds |
|---|---|---|
| `hint_seen` | `set()` | hint ids already shown (strings) |
| `persistent.show_names` | `False` | the "Show names" setting (persistent, not saved; a `prefixes.json` exception, as `persistent.motion` is; U0a step 5 lists it) |

Nothing else. Written into existing saved variables in their existing shapes: `actions_today` gains the strings `"bath"` and `"cards_inv:<who>"` (reset each day); `schedule_overrides` gains invite pins (the shape story pins already use); `scene_light` may hold six values where it held four (the code accepts either, so an old save's four-value tuple still works); the flag `invited_girls_dorm` gets a setter, and the existing flag set gains `welcome_<room>` for each friend's door (set once, with its toast) and, when a later quest sets one, `door_<place>`; `spent_today` gains whoever I spent the afternoon with in Tokyo, in the meaning it has today. A new save's slot line is written into the save's own header by a `config.save_json_callbacks` entry, not into the store (8.3 says what it writes before the campus opens and in the middle of a scene). `hint_seen` is changed from a screen, so the function that changes it calls `renpy.retain_after_load()` (7.5).

- **No migration. `SAVE_VERSION` stays 1.**
- A save may now hold `here` equal to a new key. A key that reaches a save is never removed (if one ever were, `_migrate_15` already sends such a save to the Courtyard, and its `sb_do_path_` label must stay).
- Nothing is added to what `lit_sprite` builds (K9).
- A sprite on screen in an old save is saved by image name, so Mom shows as the new sprite after loading once her files exist.

---

## 4. The places and the map

### 4.1 The new places (eleven; 24 becomes 35)

The owner's four (the lounge, the teachers' dormitory, the recovery rooms as a Bathhouse with a Sauna, the pool) and the dormitory the owner asked for as seven places (a corridor, my room re-hung, five friends' rooms). Considered and not added: a main hall interior, a lake, a kitchen, the basement, Yaga's workshop, an armory room, a staff corridor (section 11).

| key | name | area · opens off | indoor | what it is | what there is to do | door (`place_access`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `boys_dorm` | Boys' Dormitory | dorms · the garden | yes | the first-year corridor upstairs in the house on the west side of the garden: a shoe rack at the head of the stairs, three sliding doors on each side, a window seat, and one door at the far end (mine). Three of the six side doors are the friends'; **three are spare**, plain and shut (D50) | exits; looks: the shoe rack, the notice board | always open |
| `yuji_room` | Yuji's Room | dorms · `boys_dorm` | yes | film posters, a floor cushion, a small television; the window faces the garden | talk; "Study together"; look: the film shelf; at Night, when he is asleep, the bed's tag | Bond 15 with Yuji, or Yuji walking with me, or the flag `door_yuji_room`; then at any hour, whether he is in, out or asleep. Until then: "I don't know Yuji well enough to let myself in.", tag "Bond 15" |
| `megumi_room` | Megumi's Room | dorms · `boys_dorm` | yes | spare and tidy, a full bookshelf, shoes in a line | talk; "Study together"; look: the bookshelf; the bed's tag | the same, for Megumi |
| `senpai_room` | Panda and Toge's Room | dorms · `boys_dorm` | yes | the big corner room: one very large futon, one neat one, a shogi board between them | talk; look: the shogi board; a tag at each futon | the same, with Panda **or** Toge |
| `nobara_room` | Nobara's Room | dorms · `girls_dorm` | yes | a dressing mirror, shopping bags in a row, a tool roll on the desk | talk; "Study together"; look: the mirror; the bed's tag | the same, for Nobara ("I don't know her well enough to knock."); the corridor's own after-dark rule comes first (4.2) |
| `maki_room` | Maki's Room | dorms · `girls_dorm` | yes | bare, a weapon case, a pull-up bar in the door frame | talk; look: the weapon case; the bed's tag | the same, for Maki; the corridor's rule first |
| `teachers_lounge` | Teachers' Lounge | staff · the steps | yes | the ground floor of the teachers' house: sofas round a low table, a kitchenette, a television, the staff rota on the wall, stairs up | talk to teachers off duty; look: **the rota** (it says where each teacher is this hour and whether Gojo is in today); look: the kettle | always open |
| `teachers_dorm` | Teachers' Dormitory | staff · `teachers_lounge` | yes | upstairs: a sitting room round a sunken hearth, Yaga's workbench under the end window, **four** sliding bedroom doors, three with name plates and **one with a blank one** (D50; O69: the plan had six and three) | talk; looks: the workbench, the name plates (a line each, by who is home and, at Night, who is asleep) | always open; the bedrooms are doors, never places |
| `bathhouse` | Onsen | mountain · the Mountain Path | yes | the rest hall of a timber mountain onsen at the edge of the trees: benches, a fan, a fridge of cold milk, a cedar door to the sauna, and through open screens the stone hot spring, steaming and empty, with the forest behind it | **Soak** (4.7); look: the milk fridge; talk | always open (the soak itself is closed at Night) |
| `sauna` | Sauna | mountain · `bathhouse` | yes | a cedar box, a stove, a bucket and a bench, and beside the door a stone cold-plunge tub fed by a bamboo spout; no window | **Sit it out** (4.7): the heat, then the cold plunge | always open |
| `pool` | Swimming Pool | grounds · the Training Yard | no | an open-air 25 m pool set into a stone terrace behind the dojo, with a timber deck, a changing hut under the school's grey tiled roof, cedars round it and the mountains beyond | **Swim** (4.7); "Swim together" (`swim_pool`, 4.7); look: the lane board | closed at Night: "The gate's chained after dark.", tag "Night" |

**The names (O16).** The place with key `bathhouse` is shown to the player as **Onsen**: one word, as "Bathhouse" was, so no area view's word count changes (K11). This plan's prose still says "the Bathhouse" for it. The cold plunge is a tub inside the sauna room, so the room still has no window and still needs one picture; the tub is empty in the picture, like the spring.

**The pool has to belong (O17: "we just have to make sure that it looks like it would and should be there").** It is built into the school, not set down beside it: cut into a stone terrace on the slope behind the dojo, edged in the same grey stone as the yards, its deck, fence and changing hut in the dojo's timber with the school's grey tiled roof, cedars close round it. The Training Yard's new picture shows the way to it (a roofed timber gate beside the dojo, which is where its exit sits), and the Training Grounds area map, made at the end, shows it there. The owner judges this twice (there is no campus drawing to judge it on, O20): when its picture is chosen from two versions in batch 16 (whether it looks as if it belongs), with the chosen Training Yard and the dojo's picture as its references; and again on the map (where it sits). The first sight of it is earlier still: the pool's gate beside the dojo is in the Training Yard's two versions in batch 1. If neither version of the pool belongs, it is run again before anything else in that batch is installed.

**Nobody is ever drawn in the bath, the sauna, the cold plunge or the pool's water** (K16). The Bathhouse's picture is its rest hall; the pool's picture, its evening picture and both stand-ins show no people. A soak, a sauna or a swim is told in the first person over the place's own picture with no sprite shown; people who are there stand in the rest hall or on the deck in uniform, and no pose is ever painted in these three places.

**What each needs painted** (5.13 has the batches). Until a picture exists the place shows its stand-in (K12), defined with `bg_placeholder` so the real file replaces it at the next launch with no script change; while `config.developer` is on, a small "stand-in" tag sits in the corner.

| place | day | after dark | foreground | stand-in until then |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `boys_dorm` | yes | evening, night | – | the girls' corridor, mirrored, cool grey |
| `yuji_room`, `megumi_room`, `senpai_room` | yes | evening each, and two Night pictures each, `lit` and `night` (5.7a) | – | My Room mirrored, in three tints (warm, blue, green) |
| `nobara_room`, `maki_room` | yes | evening each, and two Night pictures each, `lit` and `night` (5.7a) | – | My Room, rose and slate |
| `teachers_lounge` | yes | evening, night | the sofa's back | the Common Room, violet-grey |
| `teachers_dorm` | yes | evening, night | – | the Principal's Office cropped, warm |
| `bathhouse` | yes | evening, night | – | the infirmary, pale teal |
| `sauna` | yes (no window, lit by its stove: one picture and a light row for every hour) | – | – | the storehouse, amber |
| `pool` | yes | evening (Night is closed) | – | the riverside cropped to the water, cyan |

Eleven day pictures and twenty-four hour pictures, **every one in two versions for the owner to choose from** (O21). A friend's room at Night is painted twice (O42, D61, 5.7a): `lit`, with one small lamp on and the rest dim, shown while its owner is up, and `night`, dark, shown while its owner is asleep or out. That is the five more that make nineteen twenty-four, and it ends the one shared picture this paragraph had (it was on 5.13's list of compromises left, and is marked there as taken).

**Which friends' rooms are built was the owner's to say, and the owner said "All five rooms"** (SCOPE 3), so all five keys are laid in wave 0. After wave 0 a key has reached saves and can never be removed (3.3), so:

- **A room dropped later** (by the owner) becomes a shut door, not a missing place. Its `LOCATIONS` row, its `sb_do_path_` label and its exit row stay (so `path_validate` still finds it and a save standing in it still loads and can walk out); `place_access` returns a fixed line for it ("Her door's shut.", tag "Closed"); its owner's schedule cells go back to the corridor (the girls') or to where they were before; its `WORLD_HOMES` row points at the corridor; no picture is bought and its stand-in stays. One small commit in `sb_world.rpy`, with `u2_world`'s Night column updated in the same commit.
- **A room added later** (O22: "We can add dorms, as new we add more characters. Like Nanami, mei mei, miwa, todo, kamo, etc.") never needs a corridor repainted, because the doors are already there (D50). Each of the three houses is painted with spare doors, and at its layout sitting each spare door's point is measured and written to the house's `hot_` file under `spare`, 110 px from its neighbours and inside the frame (`check_layout.py` fails a house whose `spare` list is short of the record's count or holds a point that breaks either limit: U0d3), so it is a legal exit point the day it is needed. Adding a room is then its own small package, after the wave that is running and never inside one: (1) the key appended to `LOCATIONS`, `LOC_ORDER`, its area's `places`, `WORLD_INSIDE`, `WORLD_PINLESS` and `WORLD_HOMES`; (2) `label sb_do_path_<key>` appended and one exit row each way, the corridor's point taken from `spare`; (3) a `WORLD_DOOR_BOND` row if its door should ask a different level; (4) the newcomer's schedule and `WORLD_ASLEEP` rows (lint refuses a name on an asleep list with no home row, and a resident whose Night cell is not their home: 3.2); (5) the room's own pictures (day, evening, night; two versions each) and its layout sitting, with a stand-in until then (K12); (6) the contracts file's place count and `u2_contracts` updated in that package. **The limits:** a house holds at most seven exits (K11), so the boys' corridor (five today) takes two more rooms and the girls' (three today) as many as its far doors carry; a third boy, or a house of their own for visitors, is a second corridor (a new house key, one more picture) off a staircase the corridor's picture already shows. A room inside a house has no pin, so no map and no word budget changes; its icon takes one of the four empty cells of sheet 4, or its word until then. A teacher needs none of this: the bedrooms are doors, so a new teacher is a name-plate line, a schedule row and a `WORLD_HOMES` row. **Where each kind of newcomer lives is decided when they arrive** (a student in a dormitory; the Kyoto students, who visit, perhaps in guest rooms; Nanami and Mei Mei in the teachers' house or off campus, as Gojo is): none is built now.

### 4.2 Doors, homes and "at home"

- **A friend's room asks a Bond level, and then it is open at any hour** (O18, O19, D10). `place_access` refuses a friend's room until one of these is true: Bond with one of its owners (`world_door_owners`: either Panda or Toge for the shared room) has reached `world_door_bond(room)`, which is **15** for now; or that owner is the companion walking with me; or the story flag `door_<room>` is set. From then on the door is open at every hour, whether its owner is in, out or asleep. Until then it says, for example, "I don't know Megumi well enough to let myself in.", tag "Bond 15" (the number is read from the table). `place_access` reads Bond, flags and the companion only; it does not call `char_location`, so there is no loop. The level is one table entry, and the storyboard decides the final numbers, door by door if it wants (3.2).
- **What I find inside.** Its owner in and awake: they stand in the room, to talk to and do things with, as anywhere. Its owner out: the room, its looks, nobody; nothing is offered. Its owner asleep (at Night, 4.3): a short tag at the bed and one line when I look; they are not on the stage and nothing is offered with them. **Walking in starts nothing,** at any hour (K16).
- **Once inside I am never locked in.** The protected `label path_walk` refuses a locked destination (`room_paths.rpy:420-424`), and a room's only exit is its house, so `place_access` starts with one clause: **a house is always enterable from one of its own rooms** (`if world_inside(store.here) == loc: return True, "", ""`, before the girls' corridor's after-dark clause). The case it exists for: I walk into Nobara's room in the Afternoon with her as my companion, or on a quest's flag, without the Bond the corridor asks after dark, and stay until Evening; without the clause the only exit would be locked. "Go to bed" teleports as before.
- **A room inside a house asks its house first.** `place_access("nobara_room")` applies the girls' corridor's rule before its own, because the journal's "Go there" walks straight to a place after `can_enter` (`sb_rules.rpy:68-78`) and must not step round the corridor. Two exceptions, both stated in the code: when `store.here` is the house itself **or a room inside it** (`world_inside(store.here)` is that house: from Nobara's room, "Go there" to Maki's), the house's rule is skipped (I am already inside the dormitory, so the corridor's sentence, "I am not walking into the girls' dormitory after dark uninvited", would be said to someone standing in it; the room then answers with its own line); and a room opened by its story flag is not stopped by its house.
- **The girls' corridor after dark:** `place_access` lets me in with the flag `invited_girls_dorm` **or** Bond 15 with Nobara or Maki (the same table entry). The lock's own sentence does not change, and the harness line that sets the flag still passes. Being welcome in the corridor opens neither bedroom by itself: each girl's door asks its own Bond with her, at every hour, as the boys' doors do.
- **One toast when a door first opens.** `world_welcome_check()` runs from the new-day hook: the first morning a door's level is reached it shows that door's line and sets its flag, so it is said once. The corridor's is "Nobara: 'You can come up, you know. Knock first.'"; each room has one of its own ("Yuji: 'My door doesn't lock. Never has.'"), proposed by U1 and on the owner's reading list.
- **A story quest can open any door** (O19). The flag `door_<place>` opens that place whatever the Bond, the hour or its other locks: one clause at the top of `place_access`, for every place and not only the five rooms. No quest sets one in this overhaul; a later one sets it and clears it (D49, section 11).
- **Someone at home is no longer "nowhere".** `person_now` keeps its six keys. When the person is in a place I cannot enter and that place is their `WORLD_HOMES` place or the house it is inside, `name` reads "In the Girls' Dormitory" (the house's name) where today it reads "Somewhere I can't go right now"; `hidden` stays True and `place` stays None, so no caller can walk there. `map_pin_people` and `map_area_people` return such people, and the pin draws their faces at half strength with a small door mark (`at_home`), where today it shows a lock and nobody. The journal's People card follows: `jr_card_place` (`ui_journal.rpy:480-490`) returns "Elsewhere" for anyone `hidden` today, and returns the clipped house name instead when `name` is not the old "Somewhere I can't go right now" sentence (one line, U6), so the card and the person's page both read "In the Girls' Dormitory".
- **Someone asleep is counted where they are.** In a room I can enter, `person_now` gives the room as usual and `doing` reads "asleep" (`ambient_of`, 3.1), so the journal says "Megumi's Room — asleep" and "Go there" walks to the door. On the map a sleeper's face is drawn at half strength on the house's pin, with "asleep" in the hover line. `who_is_here` returns them; `stage_people` does not (D48).
- **Someone in Tokyo is named there.** For a friend `world_away` puts in a district, `person_now`'s `name` reads "In Harajuku" where it would read "Off campus", and `doing` is their caption there (`tky_caption(who, district)`, 9.2: in `room_tokyo.rpy`, a stub returning `""` until U5 fills it; an empty caption leaves `doing` as it is today for someone off campus); `place` stays None, so nothing offers to walk there (the way is Ijichi's car, 7.6). The People card prints the name as it is.
- **A house's pin and its exit carry everyone inside it.** `map_pin_people("boys_dorm")` includes the people in the rooms `WORLD_INSIDE` hangs off it (my room keeps its own pin and its own count), and the exit to a house shows up to three faces of who is home: on its tile where the place has buttons, so that "who is in" is visible from the garden; in a shaped place beside the exit's name, under the pointer and while names are shown (D60).
- **Gojo** is off campus at Night whatever his daily draw says: one rule in `char_location`, read before story pins, so the Gojo night's pin to the ridge still wins.
- **For later quests, three hooks and nothing else** (D49): the door flag above; a hotspot that shows while a story flag says so, which a registration can already carry (`register_hotspot(..., when="<function>")`, with a sidecar point; such an id is appended to `SPOT_PLANNED` by the package that adds the quest); and `world_home_state(who)` / `world_room_state(room)`, which answer "in and awake", "asleep" or "out". No item sits in any room, nothing can be taken, nobody notices or wakes, and there is no stealth: all of that is the quest's own design, later.

The split is the owner's (approved as shown, O26) and is two lists in `WORLD_ASLEEP` (3.2): everyone is awake on five Nights or two, so everyone can be found both ways in any week; the two who stay out late are up in their own room on one Night fewer (Megumi on Saturday Nights only, Toge on four weekday Nights), and awake in the Library on the other. With every door open, five people can be talked to on any Night (three friends, Shoko, Yaga). **Before any door is open, two can** (Shoko and Yaga; today it is three: Yuji and Panda in the garden, and Shoko), **and three on a Sunday or a Wednesday Night,** when Megumi or Toge is in the Library, which has no door. That is what keeping the Bond level on doors costs in the first days, and why the level is low; the map and the journal show everyone else at home.

**Someone asleep** (D48, K16): is in their room and counted there by `who_is_here`, the map and the journal; is not returned by `stage_people`, so they are not drawn as a standing sprite, cannot be clicked or talked to, and offer nothing, a mission of theirs included (the column and the map leave it out until they are awake; the consequences below); shows as a short "Asleep" tag at the bed and one line when I look, and later as a painted shape under a blanket (7.2 rows 27 to 32). A companion is never asleep, and neither is anyone a story scene has pinned somewhere. `world_asleep` is false at every hour but Night.

**In Tokyo on Sunday afternoons** (O23; the proposal, one `WORLD_AWAY` entry each): Nobara and Maki in **Harajuku**, Yuji in **Shibuya**. Both districts are open from Ijichi's ride-along, with no grade asked (`DISTRICTS` in `sb_tokyo.rpy`); before that the friends still go, and the journal says so, which gives the ride a reason. Megumi keeps the Library, Toge the river, and Panda, who cannot walk down Takeshita Street, the pool. They leave after lunch and are back for the Evening: at Noon each is still where their weekend routine puts them, and from Noon on none of the three walks with me (7.6). 7.6 has how I find them there.

Consequences, each checked against the code:

- The Common Room is still empty at Night by routine on all seven days (`wp1_world.rpy:329-334`, unedited), and now **the garden is too**, on all seven (it held Yuji and Panda; ledger, 10.3).
- On Saturdays the river holds two (Maki, Toge) and the pool three. On Sundays the river holds Toge, the pool Panda, Harajuku two and Shibuya one. Only the weekday cafeteria at Noon still holds five: Yuji's starred cell takes him home at weekend Noon, so the weekend cafeteria drops to four. The line in `wp1_world.rpy:335` is updated twice, each time in the same commit by the package that owns the schedule: by U0b for the weekend cafeteria, by U0c1 for the riverside.
- "Nobody who moved lost an activity" (`p2_paths.rpy:996-1002`) asks whether the activity is still offered anywhere in the person's week. Yuji, Nobara and Panda still stand in the Courtyard, a walk place; for the three who left the weekend river, "Swim together" is still offered, as `swim_pool` at the pool on Saturdays (and for Panda on Sundays too); Maki still swims at the river on Saturdays, and still drills in the dojo at weekend Noon. The check is told that `swim_pool` continues `swim` (one mapping, a ledger line, 10.3); no list of exceptions is written. The new people are added to its `_zz_moved` list: in wave 0 everyone but the three who left the river, and those three in wave 1, in the commit that brings `swim_pool` (until then the pool, like every new place, offers nothing to do).
- No written scene is tied to a person and a place (`p2_paths.rpy:987`), so no scene breaks. The four "stay the night" scenes still play wherever I stand; setting them in the new rooms is the story phase's.
- Utahime and Yaga keep their office hours, so the board's host and the missions' `where="office"` are untouched. Ijichi keeps the gate from Morning to Afternoon on all seven days (`trip_ok_now`), which is what makes a Sunday ride possible.
- The Courtyard on a weekday afternoon is still Yuji and Nobara (`p2_paths.rpy:1028`).
- The Daytime and Evening moves in the table (the pool on weekend Afternoons, the baths and the pond on weekend Evenings, the lounge off duty, Yaga on campus at weekends, Nobara's weekend Morning in her room) were shown to the owner under "Chosen for you" and approved as shown (O26); each is one table cell. A caption for a cell that no longer happens (Megumi at the Mountain Path and Toge on the rooftop on weekend Mornings, Yuji in the cafeteria and Maki in the dojo on weekend Evenings) is removed with the others that can never show (7.1), and U0a's baseline of who is where is what `u2_world` compares the new week against.
- At Night every timed activity is closed by the night rule, except the four ids in `NIGHT_OK` (`sb_night.rpy:34`): the Gojo night, "Sit out with them", "Stay a while longer" (`rom_close`) and "Stay the night" (`int_night`). All are offered from a person's view, so **none is ever offered with someone asleep**; with a friend who is awake in their room they are offered exactly as they are today, by my choosing them. "Sit out with them" gains the garden, so Yuji by the pond on a weekend Evening offers it. `rom_close` and `int_night` have no place of their own (`sb_activities.rpy:80-91, 106`), so with Nobara, Maki and Utahime at the baths on weekend Evenings they would be offered there. **They are not offered in the Bathhouse, the Sauna or the Pool** (K16; one test in `activity_state`, laid by U0c so it holds from the first day anyone is sent there; `u2_things` checks it).
- **Every reader of `who_is_here`, and how each is closed for a sleeper** (D48; `who_is_here` still returns them, so each reader was read). (1) `stage_people` (`sb_screens.rpy:189`) is the filter itself. (2) **Missions.** `_ms_startable` (`sb_missions.rpy:677-693`, frozen) asks only that the giver is in `who_is_here(loc)`, and its results are offered as a tile in the place view's column, not from a person's view. A character mission has no place and no cost (`sb_missions.rpy:358-365`), so the night rule lets it through (`sb_night.rpy:147`): left alone, on a weekday Night in Maki's room the column would offer "The Armory" (`cm_maki_1`, Bond 10) and play a scene with her awake. It is closed where the results are read, by the same test: `rail_missions` (`sb_screens.rpy:312-322`) leaves out a mission whose giver is `world_asleep`, and on the map `map_has_news`, `map_waiting` and the "mission" rows of `screen map_area` do the same (`ui_map.rpy:115-121, 143-145, 801`; 3.1). On a Night the giver is awake the mission is offered as it is today. (3) The map's lists of people (`ui_map.rpy:126, 168, 334, 343, 376`) count a sleeper on purpose (4.2); the pin's gold badge (`:352`) goes through `map_has_news`. (4) `ambient_of`'s "someone else is here" test (`sb_screens.rpy:172`) picks a caption and offers nothing. (5) The frozen loop's three uses, the river's swim, the infirmary's patch-up and Yaga's mission board (`sb_loop.rpy:204, 228, 382`): none is in a bedroom, and Shoko and Yaga are never asleep. (6) The card table (`room_cards.rpy:208`) reads the Common Room, which is empty at Night. (7) `st_noise` (`story_arc1.rpy:131`) is an Evening beat. The board's list of partners reads `char_location`, not `who_is_here` (`sb_board.rpy:523`), and the board is used at its host's office (`:240`), which is shut at Night. An invite and a night out start from a person's view, so never with a sleeper.

### 4.4 My room moves into the Boys' Dormitory, case by case

The house rule, added to `path_validate` (`room_paths.rpy:349-364`): a place in `WORLD_INSIDE` must have its house as its **first** exit and is left out of its yard's "must lead to every room" list; a house must be a room of the same area whose own first exit is the yard; a house may lead to the places inside it. `path_hub`, `path_exits`, `path_neighbours` and `path_register` do not change. Every place still belongs to exactly one area, so `area_of("dorm")` is still `"dorms"`.

| step | what | file |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `WORLD_INSIDE["dorm"] = "boys_dorm"`; `dorm` stays in `AREAS["dorms"]["places"]` and keeps its pin | `sb_world.rpy` |
| 2 | `PATH_EXITS["dorm"] = [("boys_dorm", 150, 760)]` (the same point: it is my door, and my room is redone in place); in `PATH_EXITS["dorm_yard"]` the row `("dorm", 248, 728)` becomes `("boys_dorm", 248, 728)` (the same point: the left house's veranda steps) | `room_paths.rpy` |
| 3 | `label sb_do_path_boys_dorm` and the three rooms' labels appended. **`label sb_do_path_dorm` (now used from the corridor) and `label sb_do_path_dorm_yard` are not touched.** | `room_paths.rpy` |
| 4 | `screen map_area` skips places in `WORLD_PINLESS`; `_validate_world` and `map_validate` accept a missing spot for those keys only | `ui_map.rpy`, `sb_world.rpy` |
| 5 | The walk-home line for the area already reads "The dormitory is a row of closed doors and one that isn't. Mine." The boys' rooms take it. The girls' rooms get rows of their own with the corridor's line, "I let myself out before the corridor wakes." ("Stay the night" is allowed at Night and costs an hour, so a day can turn in her room; the table looks up the place before the area, and without a row her room would give the general line.) | `sb_night.rpy` (U0c) |

Nothing else changes, because everything that means "home" says `"dorm"` by name and **teleports**: `night_carry_home`, `sb_do_night_bed`, the Gojo night, the night out, the Sun Mall's end (`events_arc1.rpy:1056`), `default here = "dorm"`, Sleep and Study in `place_actions`, the computer and the bed. The walk home never walks exits, so a lock or a moved door cannot strand me.

| a save made… | after the move |
|---|---|
| standing in my room (`sb_loop.rpy:37`, `here == "dorm"`) | loads on the same statement; the room shows one exit, "To the Boys' Dormitory", at the same point. Nothing a save holds names an exit. |
| on the map with `here == "dorm"` (`sb_loop.rpy:72`; a made save) | loads on level 1 (the open area is a screen variable); "I'M HERE" is on Dormitories, as before; `row_dorm` is still there |
| in the garden | loads the same; the garden's exits are rebuilt from the table; the left house's exit reads "To the Boys' Dormitory" |
| with a rollback log that walked my room → garden by the old exit | rollback restores `here`; the return sites are in `sb_do_path_dorm_yard` and `path_walk`, which still exist, textually identical |
| parked on `sb_do_path_dorm`'s return (walked into my room a moment before the save) | the label and its `call path_walk("dorm")` are untouched; loads the same |
| on a night menu, on the walk-home line, or anywhere a night ends (made saves) | unaffected: those paths set `here = "dorm"` by name |
| before the campus opens (most of the owner's saves today; default `here == "dorm"`) | unaffected. `arc_one_open` then says "I go out into the courtyard", a narrated walk, still true |
| a new game | wakes in `dorm` as before |

**No row of this table is tested on a slot the owner happens to have.** Autosaves rotate: on the day this was checked the owner's auto-1 was on the map in the cafeteria and auto-3 on a menu in `ch2_tour.rpy`, all eleven were on day 1, and none held a Night, the map opened from my room, or a person view. Every case above is one of U0a's made saves (10.2), named for what it holds.

Three ways home, each tested by name in `u2_home.rpy`: "Go to bed" from anywhere (a teleport, unchanged); the map (Dormitories, then my room's pin: two clicks, as before); on foot (garden, corridor, the door at the end: one click more than today, which is what the owner asked for).

The spare doors of D50 are painted and measured but are not exits: nothing is registered for them, and no label exists, until a room is added (4.1). Eleven labels are appended; all 24 old ones stay. `PATH_LINKS` keeps today's six: there is still no path from the Courtyard to the staff steps (the plan has none; AUDIT M5 stays as it is, now for a reason the plan shows).

### 4.6 The icon column: how each yard keeps to one column

`p2_paths.rpy:739-740` wants at most eight tiles in the column with four people in the place. An exit, or an action on an object, drops into the column whenever a person's rectangle covers its square, and that rectangle is the whole sprite canvas (about 395 by 728 px), so four people can cover most of the lower picture. Hold the hour and Wait are always two tiles. So:

| yard | things that can drop into the column | at least this many must stay clear of a four-person row |
|---|---|---|
| Dormitory Garden | 7 exits | 1 |
| Training Yard | 6 exits and Train at the posts | 1 |
| Staff Building Steps | 6 exits | 0 |
| School Courtyard, Mountain Path | 5 exits | 0 |
| Courtyard | 4 exits, Practice; the vending machine is never hidden | 0 |

The rule for a layout sitting: in each yard the four-person row stands on the near ground and at least the number above of exits sit where no person's rectangle reaches (above the heads, as the garden's tower exit does today, or outside the row's left and right ends). `tools/check_layout.py` counts it from the sidecars before a yard is installed; the unedited harness line counts it again in the game. With a companion a second column is allowed, as today. **If the Training Yard cannot pass, "Train at the posts" becomes a look and training stays in the hall;** nothing else depends on it.

**In a shaped place (D60) the count is the same count.** A hotspot there is hidden, and its action back in the column, exactly when a person's rectangle covers the 72 px square at its point, as today; the shape adds somewhere to click and takes no part in the rule. So the layout sitting puts a shaped hotspot's point on the part of its shape that people cover least (beside the standing rows; above them only left of x 364, since under the banner an exit starts at y 270, `p2_paths.rpy:714`, and a person's picture reaches up to y 312), and the table above is met the way it is met with buttons.

## 5. The pictures

### 5.1 The settled look: one paragraph, in every prompt

`tools/bgs/look.txt`, read by every maker (redo, new composition, relight, pose room, map). It is `LOOK3` from `tools/make_look_workflow.py`, the wording the third test proved, with these small changes: "the room" becomes "the place" and "the floor" becomes "the floor or the ground"; "daylight" becomes "light" (the hour comes from its own sentence); its last window clause ("of trees, tiled roofs and sky") is dropped, because what a window shows now comes from the campus record, room by room; and "water marks" becomes "damp patches" (the project's record: the service once blocked a room clean-up as watermark removal when "remove" met "water mark", and one blocked box aborts the whole run; `KEEP` says "Do not remove" in every in-place prompt). Batches 1 and 2 use these words first, on composed pictures; batch 3 is their test on a room redone in place, before they are frozen.

> THE LOOK: a real place you could walk into, drawn as a clean modern anime background. Believable materials (wood grain, glass with soft reflections, stone, plaster, fabric), physically believable light with soft shadows and bounced light, real depth. Clean and well kept: walls are fresh and even, with no stains, cracks, peeling, damp patches or dirt, and there is no dust, litter or fallen leaves on the floor or the ground; everything that belongs to the place is still there. Crisp precise edges, clean clear colour, no film grain, no noise, no canvas texture, no visible brush strokes, no haze or glow washing out detail. Through every window a clear, in-focus view. No people, no animals, no text, no logos. Output a 16:9 picture.

Rules round it:

- **A redo in place puts the keep text in front.** It is `KEEP` from the same file, split into data files the maker reads, because `KEEP` as tested is wording for an indoor room by day and ends "Keep the time of day and the direction of the light", which a relight must not say:
  - `keep.txt`: `KEEP` word for word, less its last sentence (keep the camera, every wall and object, "every object that is in the picture now, however small, each in its place: the books and boxes on the shelves ... Do not remove, tidy away or add anything."). Indoor places.
  - `keep_outdoor.txt`: the same sentences with outdoor nouns ("every building, roof, wall, path, step, rock and tree exactly where it is"; "the benches, the posts, the signs, the stones") in place of the furniture and the shelf list. The forest, the riverside, the ridge, the rooftop and every outdoor relight. **Untried. Its first use is batch 7a, the Courtyard's evening and night (the first outdoor relight); its first use on a day picture is batch 8a, the rooftop.** Nothing else outdoors is relit before 7a is judged, or redone in place by day before 8a is.
  - `keep_light.txt`: "Keep the time of day and the direction of the light." Added after the keep text for a **day** redo only; a relight leaves it out and says its hour instead.
  - **A named addition** (the laptop, Yaga's desk and photograph, the veil) replaces the keep text's last sentence with "Do not remove or tidy away anything, and add nothing except this:" followed by the room's `allow` line. **Untried; batch 3a (the laptop) is its test.**
  - **A named change** (used only in 5.2's fallback, to tell two existing buildings apart) is a different thing from an addition: it re-skins something that is there. Its sentence is "Change only this, and keep its outline exactly:" followed by the keep list's `change` line; the installer leaves that box out of "nothing vanished" and "nothing was added" and checks its outline alone (5.4). **Untried; it is bought only if a yard of batch 1 or 2 fails twice.**
  - The keep lists of 5.4 are installer data for the "nothing vanished" check; they add no words to the prompt.
  - Every picture is made in two versions (D52), so a picture is "refused" when both versions are, and "refused twice" when a second pair is too. "Refused twice" is counted per picture: that picture keeps its old state (the old picture, the tint for that hour, or the room without its addition) and the owner is told what and why. A *wording* is judged dead, and no more pictures are bought with it, only when two different pictures have each been refused twice with it.
- **Never "nothing on the floor", "empty", "tidy away the", or a list of things to take off a seat or a table.** The second test's "nothing scattered on them" emptied the Common Room.
- **What a window shows is one more sentence, written by the tool from the record** ("Through the windows: the garden pond and the tower's foot"), never invented per prompt.
- **A new composition of a place people stand in** (the yards, the gate, the eleven new places) puts `tools/bgs/stage.txt` in front instead (5.6): what the screen needs from the picture, in a painter's words. `stage.txt` speaks of eye height and open ground at the bottom, so it is **not** put in front of anything else. **The seven maps** get `tools/bgs/map.txt` ("seen from high above, from the south, north at the top; every building named above is clearly separate; open ground beside each building where a marker can sit"). The main menu's picture and the pictures of 5.8 get the one line of framing their own row gives ("from below", "seen from the back seat").
- `tools/check_prompts.py` fails a prompt that contains: worn, old, weathered, aged, moss, mossy, lived-in, dusty, scattered, "nothing on", "leaves on", jacket, umbrella, haze, bloom, painterly, brushwork, "soft painted", "water mark", watermark, a character's or the series' name (the editor is a closed service), a count of movable objects in a room ("four desks"), or "lantern" unless the record puts one there. **What it reads, exactly:** the per-picture lines only (the hour sentence, the window line, an `allow` or `change` line, a pose's `spot`, the place line a new composition's prompt is built from), with `look.txt`, the keep texts, `stage.txt` and `map.txt` taken out first: those are frozen and reviewed once, as wholes, and the look itself has to say "no haze" and "fallen leaves on the floor". Banned words match whole words. The count rule is about movable things, not about a record look line's storeys, rows of windows or single named features ("five storeys", "two stone lanterns", "one heavy door"). Sprite base prompts (`tools/chars/prompts/`), which must name the house style, are outside it. It runs on every background, map and pose prompt before a workflow is written; `test_art_tools.sh` has the two cases that prove the rule is passable (the plain Common Room prompt and the School Courtyard prompt both pass) and one that proves it bites (a window line with "old tiled roofs" fails).
- The four phase-1 style files (`style.txt`, `style_interior.txt`, `style_dim.txt`, `style_aerial.txt`), `STYLE.md` and `make_relight_workflow.py` ask for the old look. They get a first line saying which file replaced them, and no maker of this overhaul reads them.
- Every render is 2K (2752×1536, Nano Banana Pro, $0.16). Nothing is enlarged from 1K again. Every picture is rendered twice, from two seeds, and the result files carry a letter for the version (`_a_`, `_b_`), never a finish-order number.

### 5.2 The campus record (the bible that grows)

**There is no drawing first** (O20: "Lets redo all the main locations first like we did with the Courtyard and work outwards. Once we have all the mini locations. We can construct a accurate main map around them."). Nothing is approved from above before pictures are made, and checkpoint A has no drawing. The campus is kept one place a different way: every picture the owner chooses fixes facts, and every later picture is composed from words plus the chosen pictures of its neighbours and must agree with the facts so far. The map is the last thing made, from the finished places.

- **`tools/bgs/campus.json` is the record.** It is the only place an outdoor fact is written, and it holds two kinds of row. A **fixed** row is a fact a chosen picture shows; it names that picture and its revision. A **proposed** row is the plan's words for something not painted yet: it goes into prompts, and it changes without ceremony when a chosen picture says otherwise. A third kind exists for one case only: a **waived** row names a fixed fact, the chosen picture that stands against it and the date the owner accepted that (the last rung of the ladder below); nothing else may write one. A building's look is held in two fields: `look` (materials, storeys, roof, windows: it goes into every prompt that shows the building) and, where one side differs, `court_side` (what stands on that side only: it goes only into prompts whose camera sees that side).
- **What is fixed today,** by the four pictures already chosen (U0d1 reads them off the pictures; D14): from the gateway Courtyard, the School Building on the left with its porch and shoe lockers, the gateway ahead with the steps uphill to the dormitories, the Training Hall on the right with its weapon rack, straw posts and vending machine, a matching pair of timber gate lodges either side of the gateway, the pagoda behind the schoolhouse, and the flagstones at the bottom of the frame leading back to the gate; from the bright Library, its layout and the low grey roof among treetops outside its far windows; from the Common Room and the Cafeteria, each room's layout (their window views are redone, D16).
- **How a fact is fixed.** When the owner chooses a version, its layout sitting (5.6) reads off the picture, and `install_redo.py --compose` writes into the record in the same commit as the picture: on which third of the frame each exit's door, steps or path stands, and their order from left to right; each building the picture shows, with a crop of it cut to `tools/bgs/refs/<building>.png` (the first chosen picture that shows a building is that building's reference from then on); and what is seen in the distance. The manifest stamps the picture with the record's hash.
- **Two pagodas (O35).** The garden's chosen picture (version A, run early) shows a five-storey pagoda beyond the girls' dormitory. The Courtyard fixed its pagoda behind the schoolhouse's north end, which from the garden's camera is behind me, so this is a second one, and the owner keeps it. At the garden's layout sitting it is written into the record as a landmark of its own (`pagoda_east`, a building that holds no place, north of the Girls' Dormitory and east of the tower, at about (1110, 350) on the ground plan, fixed by the garden's picture, with its crop as its reference), and is not reported as standing against the first. **The record is not edited for it before that sitting:** batch 1's early prompts are compared with the prompts the record gives on the day of batch 0's sitting (10.2), and a row written from one of batch 1's own pictures would change the garden's prompt and throw away the very render it came from. From then on a picture whose camera looks that way shows it, and the map, made last, shows both.
- **How the next picture is composed.** `tools/make_scene_workflow.py` builds every outdoor and map prompt from the record: the place's proposed words; the fixed facts that bear on it, in plain words ("the long brown-timber hall with the deep veranda, the building in the second picture, stands on the left"); and, as reference pictures, the chosen picture of each neighbour and the crop of each building it shares with them (the editor takes up to fourteen). No outdoor prompt is written by hand. The order is enforced (K8): working outwards from the Courtyard, a place is not made before the chosen picture of the yard it stands off (a room inside a house waits for that yard, and for whatever its batch's "needs first" names, not for its corridor: My Room comes six batches before the corridor it opens off, and is its reference).
- **`tools/check_campus.py`** (plain Python, part of every package's acceptance once it lands) is the automatic test of "the map flows", now against the record. Every place has a row and every building a look line; every `PATH_LINKS` pair is a path in the record, and nothing else is. For each layout, a candidate's first guess or an installed one: each exit's point lies in the third of the screen its side says, and exits to two buildings keep their left-to-right order (two exits within 60 px of each other across are not ordered); a place **behind the camera** has its exit in the bottom band of the picture; **and nothing contradicts a fixed fact.** The fixed facts are relations seen from a known camera ("from the Courtyard, facing north, the Training Hall is right of the gateway"), and together they must fit one ground plan: a layout whose sides cannot be placed on it fails, and the tool names the fact ("in this version the training hall is on the far side of the yard from the Courtyard you chose"). On each area picture the pins keep the ground plan's relations (if building A is west of B, A's pin is left of B's, and north is higher; a tolerance of 60 px), and the same for the six markers on the campus map (the Mountain's marker is checked only as north of the other five: 4.8). Every building a room's window line names lies in the half of the ground plan that wall faces; and for a room entered from a yard with the camera in the doorway, the far wall faces away from that yard.

  **What counts as a failure, and when.** Only a contradiction of a **fixed** fact is a failure, and it is found before the owner chooses: such a version is shown marked, and is not installable. A version that differs from a **proposal** is not a fault: if the owner chooses it, the proposal is rewritten to match and `art_status.py` lists every unmade picture whose prompt it touches. Today's pictures disagree with the proposals on purpose (the Training Yard's hall and dojo are on the other sides, `room_paths.rpy:128-130`; the two offices are swapped on the Staff map, `sb_world.rpy:139-140`), and U0c leaves the old yards' code points on today's sides. So: the record itself and every test sidecar are checked in full, always; a yard or map whose picture is still at **revision 1** is reported as "waits for batch N" and is not a failure; from **revision 2** every side, order and pin relation is checked against the fixed facts and is a hard failure. The one exception is a fact with a `waived` row (the ladder's last rung): it is reported on every run and is not a failure. The tool has two negative tests: a composed test sidecar with two exits swapped against a fixed fact must fail; and the same sidecar, which passes with a report once a `waived` row names that fact, must fail again when the row is taken out.
- **`tools/campus_plan.py`** draws the record as a working sketch: `tools/bgs/campus_plan.png` (labelled, fixed facts solid and proposals dashed; for reviewers) and one crop per area. **It is never put to the owner for approval.** At the end, when every place is fixed, it also draws `campus_plan_ref.png` (colour blocks, no words) as the layout reference for the map prompts (5.9). It writes `tools/bgs/CAMPUS2.md` from the same data, so the prose can never disagree with the record; `CAMPUS.md` gets a first line pointing at it.

**The record's ground plan** (top-down, north and uphill at the top, on a 1920×1080 sheet, laid so that each quarter sits where its map marker already is; the Mountain is the exception, 4.8: it runs across the whole top, and its marker sits on the high ground east of the stairs). **Rows marked † are fixed** by the pictures already chosen. Every other row is a proposal: its look line goes into the prompts word for word, a "court side" is added only where the camera sees that side, and its position is settled by the pictures as they are chosen and, for the map, at the end.

| building or ground | holds | at (x, y) | its look, the same in every picture that shows it |
|---|---|---|---|

| † Courtyard | `courtyard` | (960, 800) | a wide flagstone court |

| † Gate Lodges (two) | landmarks, not places | about (890, 690) and (1040, 690), clear of the pagoda | a matching pair, one either side of the gateway steps behind the low stone wall: brown timber, a railed veranda, a grey tiled roof (read off the chosen Courtyard, which shows both). **Fixed as lodges:** they stand at the foot of the steps and are not the dormitories, which stand further up round the garden and keep their own two looks; the owner sees that the day the Dormitory Garden's two versions are in front of them |

| † Training Hall | `training` | (1200, 760), long, north to south | a broad brown-timber hall with a deep roofed veranda and paper-screened sliding doors (read off the chosen Courtyard). *Court side:* a weapon rack on the veranda and straw posts by the steps; the vending machine at the court end of the veranda |

**What I see in each yard** (the camera, and what each exit sits on; the Courtyard's row is fixed, the others are proposals that the owner meets as two pictures to choose from; this also settles AUDIT N14, where the map and the steps disagreed about the two offices: Gojo's is on the left and Utahime's on the right, on the steps and on the Staff map alike):

The garden and the Mountain Path keep today's camera and today's sides. The staff steps keep today's camera, but their way to the Training Yard moves from the right edge (today's dirt path, at (1640, 850)) to the bottom of the frame, and the Teachers' House stands where that path leaves. The Training Yard's sides swap (the hall must be on the Courtyard's side) and the School Courtyard is laid out afresh. On the Mountain Path the order from left to right is the Forest Trail, the Ridge's stairs, the Bathhouse, the Riverside, in the record, in the picture (today's exits already are: 180, 862, 1640 across) and on the Mountain map alike. **From the Main Gate** (composed again, 5.3; a proposal like the others) I stand on the garden path before the gatehouse, facing north: water on either side, the plank bridge, the gatehouse over me, and through its open doors the open Courtyard, with the schoolhouse's roof above the garden wall on the left and the training hall's on the right; no hall, and no torii (O36).

**Behind the camera means the bottom of the picture.** A place the camera has its back to (the Cafeteria from the School Courtyard; the door I came in by, in a room that shows no door) has its exit in the bottom band, the lowest row the lanes allow, as the yards' ways back already are. It is a stated rule of the record, `check_layout.py` and `check_campus.py` know it, and it is what "an exit sits on the thing it leads to" means when the thing cannot be in the picture.

**What the windows show** is a line per window wall in the record, each with the way that wall faces, so the tool can check it (a building named in a line must lie on that side of the room on the ground plan). Common Room (I stand in the glass doors on the deck, the pond behind me, looking north; read off the installed picture): the back window shows the tower's foot and cedars, the left window the Boys' Dormitory's dark timber and gallery; the pond is not in this view. Cafeteria: the School Courtyard and the pine. Library (I stand in its doorway, having come in from the School Courtyard, so I look north and the far wall faces north; the Cafeteria is behind me): the archive wing's grey tiled roof and treetops, which is what the chosen picture already shows (fixed). Classroom: the School Courtyard below, the Library's roof, cedars. Infirmary: cedar trunks at the forest edge. My Room: the mountain slope and the ridge, no building. Yuji's Room: the garden and the pond. Megumi's Room: the forest. Panda and Toge's Room: the tower. Girls' corridor (the end window) and both girls' rooms: the garden and the Common Room's roof. Lounge: the staff steps. Teachers' Dormitory: a small inner garden. Training Hall (high windows): sky and cedar tops. Dojo: the Training Yard's posts. Bathhouse: the spring and the cedar slope. Utahime's office: her gravel garden. Gojo's office: cedars. The Principal's office, the storehouse and the sauna have no windows. The Rooftop's far view is a redo in place and stays a loose cluster of roofs. A window line names a neighbour only once that neighbour's picture is chosen, which is why a room is made after its yard (K8); the Common Room's and the Cafeteria's are written after batch 1.

**How a building keeps its look from picture to picture,** in order of trust: (1) the look line above, identical in every prompt; (2) each later picture is given the chosen picture of each neighbour and the crop of each building it shares (`tools/bgs/refs/<building>.png`, cut by the installer from the first chosen picture that shows it); (3) a person looks, with a five-line checklist per picture ("the schoolhouse is on the left: yes / no"; for the Training Yard, "no vending machine: yes / no"; for the gate, "beyond the torii is the open Courtyard, no hall: yes / no"). **The order of making is the owner's** (O20): the Courtyard (done) fixes the schoolhouse, the gateway, the two gate lodges, the pagoda and the training hall; then the first ring, the School Courtyard, the Dormitory Garden and the Training Yard (batch 1); then the second ring, the staff steps, the mountain path and the gate (batch 2); then the rooms off each and the new places, each with its yard's picture as a reference; and last the six area maps and the campus map (5.9).

**The first test is batch 1.** The old first test, whether the editor follows a plan drawing, is gone with the drawing. The new one: **whether a yard composed from words and the chosen picture beside it agrees with that picture.** The Courtyard itself was composed from words alone and worked (the second look test); the editor has been handed a room's own picture before (every look test), never a neighbour's to agree with. What happens if it does not is decided here, not on the day:

| step | what | cost |
|---|---|---|
| the pass rule for batch 1 | for each of its three yards, at least one of the two versions on which a first-guess layout passes `check_layout.py` and `check_campus.py` (no fixed fact contradicted: for the Training Yard, the training hall is on the Courtyard's side and is the chosen Courtyard's hall), and the five-line checklist is all yes | – |
| if a yard fails | one more pair for it, with the shared building's crop given larger and its side said twice in words | $0.32 a yard, at most $0.96 |
| if it fails again: a yard that keeps today's camera (the garden; in batch 2 the staff steps and the Mountain Path) | redone in place with the outdoor keep text and one named thing, by 5.4's procedure: for the garden a named *change* (the two dormitories told apart, each inside its own outline); for the staff steps and the Mountain Path a named *addition* (the Teachers' House beyond Utahime's office, left of today's dirt path; the Onsen among the trees on the right). These wordings are untried at that point: an outdoor redo in place is first run in batch 8, a named addition in batch 3, and a named change nowhere | two renders each |
| and if both versions of that are refused | the yard is redone in place with **no** addition or change (plain outdoor keep text). The new building's exit sits on the nearest path or gap in today's composition; for the staff steps the Training Yard exit stays on the right, on the dirt path. **The record takes its facts from the picture** (for the garden: the two dormitories keep one dark-timber look and are told apart on the map and by their door curtains only), and the owner chooses it knowing that | two more renders, from the allowance |
| if it fails again: the School Courtyard and the Training Yard, which cannot be redone in place to match | today's picture is redone in place, and **the record takes its facts from the picture** (the map, made last, then agrees with what is seen, which is what "accurate" asks). For the Training Yard that leaves the hall on the far side from the Courtyard, which stands against a fact the chosen Courtyard has fixed (the Training Hall on its right). **This is the one case where a chosen picture may stand against a fixed fact.** The owner is shown the contradiction in plain words before choosing ("in this picture the training hall is on the far side of the yard from the Courtyard you chose") and accepts it; the installer then writes a `waived` row in `campus.json` naming the fact, the picture and the date (`install_redo.py --waive`, refused without the owner's word in the session). `check_campus.py` reports a waived fact and does not fail on it, and the Training Grounds map is composed to the Courtyard's fact, with the waiver printed on its checklist. The owner sees it again at checkpoint B and may ask for the swap to be tried again later. The pool's gate is then a named addition | two renders each |
| batch 2 (the staff steps, the Mountain Path, the gate) | the same pass rule and the same ladder; the gate has no in-place rung (today's picture shows a hall the Courtyard does not have, D46), so a gate that fails twice is made from words alone, as the Courtyard was | as above |
| the maps (batch 21a, the Courtyard & Gate area map alone) | whether the editor paints a map that agrees with finished places. Pass: on one of the two versions the pins' relations pass `check_campus.py` and the checklist is all yes. If a second pair fails too, all six area maps and the campus map are drawn by `campus_plan.py` from the record in the new palette | $0.32, then free |

Because facts are taken from what the owner chooses and not laid down beforehand, less can go wrong than under a drawing: a picture can only fail by contradicting something already chosen, or by putting a door where the screen cannot use it.

**The editor may change under us.** It is a preview model (`gemini-3-pro-image-preview`) used across forty-seven sittings. At the start of any sitting more than a week after the last, from batch 3 on, the art-install role runs `test_art_tools.sh`'s real cases and one fixed "canary" ($0.16): **batch 3a's own Common Room workflow, one version of it, run again unchanged** (the same prompt, the same seed, the same input: the Common Room's revision-1 picture from git). Batch 3a makes that render anyway (5.4). **The canary is always measured against the Common Room's revision-1 picture** (the manifest's `rev1_commit`), by `install_redo.py --dry --against-rev1`, never against whatever is installed that day; batch 3a's baseline figures (shift, vanished-object, sharpness) are taken the same way and recorded in `tools/art/batches.json` beside its ordinary install figures (which 5.4 takes against the installed picture), each with a stated margin. Measured any other way the two sets would not be comparable: the baseline against the third-test render and every later run against batch 3a's own result. If the canary's figures fall outside the margins, the sitting stops and the owner is told, before a batch is bought in a look that no longer matches the rooms already made. (Batches 1 and 2 come before the canary has a baseline; they are composed pictures chosen by eye, and are run within days of each other.)
