### 5.4 Redone in place: the procedure, and the proof that nothing moved

**What the tests proved** (measured on the nine first-test renders and the two third-test renders): fitted the right way, every one of 24 tiles of every picture lines up with the game's picture to 0 px; a picture shifted 12 px or zoomed 2% by hand reads as moved everywhere; and with `KEEP` every object stays.

**The maker: `tools/make_redo_workflow.py`.** Input: the game's own file `game/images/bg/<stem>.png` (never the stale `backgrounds_chosen/` folder). **One exception, the reruns of the Common Room and the Cafeteria in batches 3a and 4a:** their input is each room's revision-1 picture, taken from git by the manifest's `rev1_commit`, because the installed files are themselves redos and a redo of a redo has never been tried; the third test proved the route from revision 1. Prompt: the keep text for the place (5.1: indoor or outdoor; `keep_light.txt` for a day redo; the addition sentence where the room has one), then at most two lines from data (the hour, for a relight; "Through the windows: …" from the record), then `look.txt`. Every workflow uses fixed seeds, so pressing Run again after an error in the same session pays only for the boxes that did not finish. **Two versions** ($0.32), from two fixed seeds (D52): the installer runs its proof on both, and the owner chooses between those that pass. A version that fails is shown with its reason and cannot be chosen; if both fail, or the owner says "again", one more pair is run. It writes the workflow and a page of numbered steps (`art_<n>.md`) into `~/Desktop/JJK Game images/ui2/`, and refuses a picture whose order is wrong (K8).

**The keep list: `tools/bgs/keep/<stem>.json`,** one per room, written once before the room's batch by the art-install role (about ten minutes a room, looking at the picture with `layout_sheet.py`):

```
{"keep":  [{"name": "the floor cushions by the low table", "box": [1330, 820, 240, 110]}, ...],
 "empty": [{"name": "the sofa seat", "box": [150, 300, 230, 60]}, {"name": "the floor", "poly": [[...]]}],
 "allow": [{"name": "a closed laptop on the desk", "box": [400, 540, 120, 70]}],
 "change": [],
 "doors": [{"name": "the sliding door, right wall", "box": [1600, 380, 150, 420]}],
 "scale": [1640, 380, 800]}
```

Every hotspot's object, every object a caption or a pose names and every foreground object is in `keep` automatically (a 110 px box round each registered point, the bounding box of each foreground polygon); the hand-written rows add what the eye would miss. `empty` is what must not gain anything; `allow` is the named addition or the window. `change` (5.2's fallback only) is a box that is meant to change inside a fixed outline: it is left out of steps 3 and 4, and its outline (the roof line and the foot of the wall) must still agree within 3 px. `doors` are the room's painted doors, which `check_layout.py`'s indoor-exit rule reads (7.3). `scale` is one door or post taken as 200 cm, the same triple as the `view_` file's key (6.1); it is written here for a room a pose is planned in, so that the pose maker has the number before the room's layout sitting has happened (5.12).

**Every art tool reads the game's numbers from one file, `tools/art/game_layout.json`** (D47): exits in order with their default and applied points, hotspots, `SPOT_PLANNED`, slots, `HOT_LANES`, the arrival caption's box, `PATH_APART` and `PATH_CLEAR`, the hotspot frame, the map pin frame, each person's canvas width, the grade and light tables, each place's picture name and whether it is indoors, `SCHEDULE` and `WEEKEND`, and a hash of the files it was read from: the `.rpy` files and every `game/layout/*.json` (the applied points come from those). It is written by a small harness (`tools/smoke/u2_dump.rpy`, U0a; not pointer-dependent) and committed. A tool that finds the hash out of date refuses with one plain line ("the game's layout has changed since the dump was written: run tools/smoke.sh u2_dump"). **The committed file is regenerated only in the owner's folder:** by U0b, U0c1 and U0c2 as their last step, at each merge, and at the start of each art sitting and each in-place layout sitting (and again in a sitting after an install or a layout has written a file under `game/layout/`), so the installer never proves "nothing moved" round an out-of-date set of points, and `check_layout.py` has the same numbers the game's four validators use. **In a worktree the dump is never committed.** Every wave-1 package edits files the dump is read from, so there `tools/smoke.sh u2_dump --out shots/art/game_layout.json` writes a local copy (`shots/` is ignored by git) and `check_layout.py`, `check_campus.py` and `stage_preview.py` take `--layout-dump <file>`; acceptance (f) of section 10 runs them that way, and six branches never change the one generated, hashed file.

**The installer: `tools/install_redo.py`** (ComfyUI's Python, through `tools/py_art.sh`).

1. **Fit:** refuse static and letterbox bars; scale the 2752×1536 render to 1920×1072, lay it 4 px down, fill the 4 px above and below with the render's own first and last rows.
2. **Nothing moved.** Edge maps of old and new. The whole-picture shift must be 0 or 1 px. Round every registered point of the place (each exit, each hotspot, the feet of every slot for one to four people, each pose box, a 12 px band along each foreground outline and along the outline of each traced shape, D60) a 192 px window is checked alone and must agree within 3 px. A moved window refuses the picture and names it: "the door my room's exit sits on moved 9 px left".
3. **Nothing vanished.** For each `keep` box, the edge agreement inside it; below the "gone" threshold the picture is refused: "the floor cushions by the low table are gone".
4. **Nothing was added.** For each `empty` zone, and across the whole floor polygon, the amount of fine edge has not risen above the threshold (this is the number that catches leaves, a cup, a jacket): "something new is on the sofa seat", unless the zone is in `allow`.
5. **Sharper.** Variance of the Laplacian at least **twice** that of **the place's revision-1 picture** passes; between 1.3 and 2 the verdict is LOOK (shown to the owner, not refused); below 1.3 it is refused. (The approved third-test rooms read 3.0 and 7.5 times; the first test's 13 to 19.) The revision-1 picture is taken from git by the manifest's `rev1_commit`, never from the installed file: in batches 3a and 4a the Common Room and the Cafeteria are already in the look, and against the installed picture a good rerun reads about 1.0 times and would be refused. Steps 2 to 4 are measured against the installed picture, which is what must not move.
6. Everything else that changed (a window's view, a tile that is simply different) is shown, not refused: verdict LOOK, with the tiles tinted on the preview.
7. **Preview:** `shots/art/redo_<stem>.jpg`, old beside new with every point and zone drawn on both.
8. **For the version the owner chooses, when it is a PASS or a LOOK they accept:** write `game/images/bg/<stem>.png`, its manifest row, `light_<place>.json` (6.2), recut the room's foregrounds with `make_fg.py` (free; for a relight whose hour has no polygon of its own, the day polygon is first copied to that hour's key in `tools/fg/<place>.json`, which is valid because the relight proved nothing moved; a foreground file is never deleted, K9), cut the reference crops of 5.2, and copy the approved render to `~/Desktop/JJK Game images/ui2/approved/` (a seed does not reproduce a picture on another day). Nothing else.
9. The old picture is not copied anywhere: it is in git. The manifest row records two commits, because a room can be redone more than once: `rev1_commit`, the commit that holds the place's revision-1 picture (written at the first install and copied forward unchanged ever after; step 5 uses it), and `from_commit`, the commit that holds the picture this install replaced (steps 2 to 4 were proved against it). So the proof can be rerun from the repository alone.

The thresholds of steps 3 to 5 are set in U0d against renders whose answers are known: the tool must pass both third-test renders and the first test's A pictures; flag the first test's Common Room B for the jacket and the cup; report the cushions, the manga shelf, the kettle and the bag as gone on the second test's emptied Common Room; and refuse a picture shifted 12 px or enlarged 2% by hand while passing one that is only recoloured. `tools/test_art_tools.sh` runs the synthetic cases anywhere and the real ones when the files are present, and prints which it skipped.

**Verdicts, in the owner's words, one for each of the two versions:** PASS (it can go in), LOOK (here is what changed; choose it or not), REFUSED (what moved, vanished or appeared; it cannot be chosen, and if both are refused, "run it once more").

**What a redo in place costs besides the picture:** no code, no harness line, and no layout number changes at the install (the room's `hot_` and `view_` files carry no `for_rev`, 3.2, so they stay in force). The few exits that sit on no door today are moved once, on today's picture, at the room's in-place layout sitting (L; 7.3, 10.2). Its hour pictures, foregrounds and poses follow in order; until each is remade the game shows the fallback of K7. A room refused twice is made as a new composition (5.6) and laid out again.

### 5.5 The record: which picture is at which revision

`game/layout/pictures.json`, written only by the install tools, read at init by `game/art_manifest.rpy`. Not saved. A picture with no row is today's picture (revision 1), and with no rows at all the game behaves exactly as it does today, so the scaffold changes nothing on screen.

```
{"pictures": {
  "courtyard":           {"rev": 2, "kind": "composed", "batch": 0, "render": "look2_courtyard_gateway_00001_.png", "record": "9f3a…"},
  "dorm_common":         {"rev": 3, "kind": "inplace",  "batch": 3, "rev1_commit": "…", "from_commit": "…", "shift": [0, 0], "sharp": [30, 95]},
  "dorm_common_evening": {"rev": 2, "kind": "relight",  "of": "dorm_common", "of_rev": 3},
  "fg_dorm_common":      {"of": "dorm_common", "of_rev": 3},
  "pose_dorm_common__nobara__evening": {"of": "dorm_common_evening", "of_rev": 2}
}}
```

- `sharp` is `[revision 1's figure, this render's]`; `shift` is against the picture this install replaced; `record` is the hash of `campus.json` as it stood when the picture was installed (5.2).
- `art_rev(stem)`: the picture's revision (1 when there is no row).
- `art_fresh(stem)`: False when the row names a parent (`of`) whose revision is no longer `of_rev`. A dependant with no row is fresh only while its parent is at revision 1.

K7 in code, four small gates laid by the scaffold: `bg_variant` (a stale hour picture is not used: the day picture is tinted; and in a foreground room an hour picture is not used until its foreground exists); `view_fg_path` (a stale foreground is not used); `view_pose` (a stale pose is not used: the standing sprite); `light_for_place` (a stale light row is not used: the general row). A harness that lays test pictures of its own over a real room carries its own `pictures.json` in its overlay (`p2_view`, 10.2 step 6), so its fixtures do not go stale on the day the real room is revised. `tools/art_status.py` prints the manifest as a table (what is in the new look, which room sets are half made, which dependant is stale, which unmade pictures a change to the record touches); it is the first thing in every checkpoint note.

### 5.6 Composed again: the procedure, and what the re-layout costs

1. **The prompt** comes from the record (the place's words, the fixed facts that bear on it, and its chosen neighbours' pictures as references: 5.2), plus `look.txt`, plus, for a place people stand in, `tools/bgs/stage.txt`, the rules such a picture must obey, in a painter's words (a map gets `map.txt` instead, 5.1):

   > The picture is seen from a standing person's eye height, the horizon a little above the middle. Level open ground runs across the whole bottom of the picture, with nothing tall standing on it. Leave the right ninth of the picture and the top-left corner free of doors, steps, signs and machines: put nothing there that a visitor would walk to or use. Every door, flight of steps and path named above is clearly visible, and no two of them are close together.

   **A door has a room behind it (O37).** A prompt never puts a door in a wall that also has a window onto the outdoors, and where a wall has several doors it asks for them evenly spaced; the layout sitting checks both by eye before a picture is offered to the owner.

   The tool also places each exit's "thing to sit on" in words ("the steps are two thirds of the way across") and, for a yard, asks for the clear places 4.6 needs. The last sentence of `stage.txt` is also what a shape needs (D60): a door, a flight of steps or a path that is clearly visible and apart from the next can be traced and clicked. Nothing is added to the prompt for it.
2. **Two versions** (2K, $0.32) of every composed picture, the rooms with one exit and no foreground included (D52), with one more pair if neither layout can pass. References: the chosen picture of each neighbour, the picture being replaced "to know the school and the camera height", and the crop of each building it shares. There is no plan drawing among them (O20).
3. **The layout sitting** (the art-install role, not the owner; no `.rpy` file is touched; the owner starts it by saying which version they chose). It places every hotspot in `SPOT_PLANNED` for the place (3.2), whether or not wave 1 has registered it yet, so each picture is laid out once; for the Boys' Dormitory corridor, the girls' corridor and the Teachers' Dormitory it also measures the spare doors (`spare`, D50); and it reads off the facts the picture fixes, which the installer writes into the record (5.2). It starts from `install_redo.py --compose --dry`, which draws the *old* layout on the new picture: what still sits on its thing is kept, the rest is moved. Per picture:

| laid out again | where it is written |
|---|---|
| every exit and hotspot point; which side a hover label opens | `hot_<place>.json` (`spots`, with exits under `go_<from>__<to>`; `tips`) |
| the shape of every exit and hotspot (D60): the building, door, steps, path or object itself, as one to three polygons | `hot_<place>.json` (`shapes`; `keep_tile` for the few that keep a button) |
| standing slots for one to four people; the floor polygon; a door for scale | `view_<place>.json` (`slots`, `floor`, `scale`) |
| a foreground outline, where the design has one | `tools/fg/<place>.json`, then `make_fg.py` |
| the light rows | `light_<place>.json`, measured by the installer, tuned by eye |
| map pins (area pictures); the Mountain box and any anchor nudge (campus map) | `area_<key>.json`, `map_campus.json` |
| words that describe the picture (`LOCATIONS[…]["desc"]`, a caption, a hotspot line) | collected as a hand-off list for the package that owns the file, never edited by the sitting |

4. **`install_redo.py --compose <stem> <render> --layout <files>`** installs a composed picture only together with its layout, and only if `tools/check_layout.py` (plain Python: the same numbers as `hot_validate`, `path_validate`, `view_validate` and `map_validate`) passes on it: every exit and hotspot inside (40, 180, 1700, 1000) and out of the toast and banner lanes and the arrival caption's box; exits 110 px apart and 120 px from a hotspot; the top-row rule; every slot's rectangle inside x 40 to 1700 and its feet inside the floor polygon; a lockable exit with room for its label; the icon-column count of 4.6; the shape rules of 7.3 (D60); the fixed facts of 5.2 (`check_campus.py`). A picture that cannot pass is not installable, and the reason goes to the owner in plain words. On success one step writes the picture, its layout files (`for_rev` set), the manifest row and the reference crops.

**The budget for the fifteen composed pictures and the eleven new places:**

| pictures | exits | hotspots | slot rows | pins | to follow |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `courtyard` | 4 | 3 | 4 | – | a pose; an evening and a night picture |
| `gate` | 1 | 1 | 4 | – | a pose; an evening and a night picture |
| `library` | 1 | 3 | 4 | – | three poses; an evening and two Night pictures (`night` and `lit`, 5.7a) |
| `school_yard`, `dorm_yard`, `staff_yard`, `training_yard`, `trailhead` | 29 | 7 | 20 | – | a pose; five evening and five night pictures |
| six area maps | – | – | – | 28 | – |
| the campus map | – | – | – | the Mountain box | – |
| eleven new places | 17 | about 20 (six of them bed tags) | 44 | – | twenty-four hour pictures (the five friends' rooms have two Night pictures each, 5.7a); fifteen poses (six of them asleep); one polygon; the spare doors |

52 exits, about 34 hotspots (each of those 86 also traced as a shape, D60), 76 slot rows, 28 pins: a layout sitting of about fifty minutes for each picture, after the owner's choice, in each batch that carries composed pictures (0 to 2, 9 to 11, 13, 16, 19, 21 and 22). (The sixteen rooms redone in place are laid out separately, on today's pictures, in L's in-place series beside wave 1: 10.2.) The two layouts for batch 0 are written by U0d before checkpoint A; as U0d changes no file under `game/`, they are staged under `tools/art/staged/<stem>/` and installed by L with their pictures at batch 0. **No `.rpy` file and no harness line is edited for any of it,** because U0a turned every coordinate a harness pins into the rule it stands for (K3), and proved it as a whole with a test overlay that moves everything (10.2). First estimates for the two pictures already chosen, read off a 200 px grid and agreeing with the judges' measurements (to be set properly at the sitting): Courtyard, School Courtyard exit on the porch about (450, 720); garden exit on the gateway steps about (975, 720); Training Yard exit on the veranda steps about (1515, 860); the gate exit stays (1300, 985); the vending machine about (1365, 750); "Practice" on the straw posts about (1690, 880), at the edge of the frame the screen allows. Library: the stacks about (330, 520) and the locked cabinet's padlock about (1610, 527) (both within 40 px of today's points); the way out at the bottom of the picture, about (1480, 950): the chosen room shows no door, because I stand in it (5.2's rule).

### 5.7 Times of day

**Every place has a painted Evening and a painted Night for every hour it can be visited** (O21, D52). The second draft painted an hour only where people stand after dark and tinted the rest, to save money; a tint leaves daylight in every window and a daytime sky over every roof, so it goes. A painted hour is used when its file exists and is fresh (5.5); until it is made the day picture is tinted, as today.

Each is relit **from the new day picture the owner chose**, never redone from the old night picture (two separate redos would disagree in every detail the editor invents): `make_redo_workflow.py --relight <stem> evening|night`, the installed game file as input, 2K, the keep text **without** `keep_light.txt` (5.1), the hour sentence, `look.txt`, **two versions** for the owner to choose between. `install_redo.py --against <day picture>` runs the fit and "nothing moved" (5.4 steps 1 and 2) and "nothing vanished" with a threshold of its own, on each version; it skips "sharper" and "nothing was added", because a night picture loses edges in its dark corners, gains pools of light on the floor and is less sharp than its day picture, and none of that is a fault. Colour is free to change. The relight thresholds are set in U0d on the eight day and hour pairs the game has today (a pair that was not made as a relight of its day picture is listed and left out). **A relight has never been run with these words; batch 3b is its test** (indoors), and batch 7a the first outdoor one (every other outdoor relight waits for it). A relight refused twice (both versions, then a second pair) is not bought again: that hour keeps the tint and the owner is told. **Five hour pictures cannot simply keep the tint,** because scenes and memory rows name them directly and would go on showing the old-look file beside a new-look day picture (K7): My Room's night, the rooftop's evening and night, the infirmary's night and the Common Room's night. For these, a relight refused twice is made once more with words only (the hour sentence and the look, no "nothing vanished" refusal) and shown to the owner as a LOOK; if the owner turns that down too, the old file stays, `art_status.py` lists the room under "half-made room sets", and the checkpoint note says which scenes show it.

|---|---|---|---|
| `dorm` (My Room), `dorm_common` | painted (My Room's is new; today its Evening shows the night picture warmed, AUDIT T9) | painted (remade), **with every light off, by moonlight** (O38); My Room has a `lit` picture as well, its lamp on, for written scenes (5.7a) | 3 |
| `cafeteria`, `girls_dorm` | painted (the cafeteria's with its own foreground cut) | painted, **with every light off, by moonlight** (O39; the corridor's from its one end window) | 4 |
| `classroom`, `infirmary` | painted | painted: the classroom **with the lights off, by moonlight**; the infirmary remade **with someone working late** (its monitor on, one ceiling light low), for Shoko's two night memories (O41) | 6 |
| `courtyard`, `school_yard`, `library` | painted | painted (the Courtyard's **with every window dark**, only its stone lamps and the vending machine glowing: O40); the School Courtyard's **with every window dark** too; the Library's **with the lights off**, and a second night with its desk lamps on, shown while someone is there (O42, D61) | 7 |
| `dorm_yard`, `rooftop` | painted | painted: the garden **with people at home** (a few dormitory windows lit, the Common Room dark: O43, D61), under a full moon; the rooftop under the same moon, with a few lights among the roofs below and the pagoda dark (O44) | 8 |
| `boys_dorm`, `yuji_room`; `megumi_room`, `senpai_room`; `nobara_room`, `maki_room` | painted | painted: the corridor's with its lights off; each friend's room twice, `lit` (one small lamp on, the rest dim) and `night` (dark, made in batch 47): 4.1, 5.7a | 9; 10; 11 |
| `office`, `utahime_office`, `gojo_office` | painted (lamplight; Yaga is in his on weekday Evenings) | none: all three are locked at Night (`place_access`) | 12 |
| `teachers_lounge`, `teachers_dorm` | painted | painted | 13 |
| `training_yard`, `staff_yard`, `trailhead` | painted | painted | 14 |

**72 relit pictures, each in two versions:** the 8 that exist, remade; 37 new on old places; 19 on new places; and 8 that D61 adds, a second Night picture under the attribute `lit` for each room that is shown both dark and lit (the Library, My Room, the dojo and the five friends' rooms). In the table above "painted" in the Night column is the picture `bg <place> night`; **5.7a says what a `lit` picture is, which rooms have one and when it is shown.** And `ridge_veil`, the story's picture, made from the new ridge. (The second draft had 28.) The rule has no exception for a room without windows other than the sauna: the Principal's Office and the storehouse are painted by lamplight for their evenings, because a tinted daytime room is still a daytime room.

A *new* hour picture needs an image name and a `TIME_VARIANT` cell; both appear when the file does (`WORLD_VARIANTS`, 3.2, 56 rows; My Room's Evening cell exists today and points at the night picture, and the new row replaces it), so nothing is defined for a picture that does not exist and a missing file can never break a place at that hour. Where an hour is still a tint because its picture is not made yet, people take 60% of the room's tint (6.2 row 2), so nobody glows in the meantime.

**A pose shows only with the picture it was painted on** (K7), so painting an hour that used to be a tint takes a day pose off that hour. Two cells are affected and each gets a pose of its own on the new picture: Yaga in his office on weekday Evenings (7.2 row 14) and, by the schedule's change, Yuji by the pond on weekend Evenings (row 15). Everyone else's posed cells are at one hour picture only.

### 5.7a Night, lit and dark (D61)

Written 11 Oct from a reading of the code as it stands on `ui2-u0c2` (Method note 32), then **checked by a second reader, whose seventeen findings are in the text below** (Method note 33). Nothing here is U0c2's. **The owner answered its three questions on 11 Oct: yes to all three (O47).** **Built and merged on 12 Oct as U0n (Method note 40).**

**The rule.** At Night a room shows one of two pictures: **dark** when nobody is up in it, **lit** when someone is. "Someone" is a person whose own place that hour is the room (their routine, a late stay below, a story pin) and who is awake. **I do not count:** My Room at Night is the lights-off picture the owner chose (O38), and I am in it. **Whoever walks with me counts only in the room their own table has them in:** asking Megumi along in the Library does not put its lamps out while we stand there, walking a friend home lights their room, and nowhere else does a companion light anything. Someone asleep lights nothing. A yard has one Night picture (D61).

**The names.** `bg <place> night` stays what it is today: the room on a night nobody is up in it. Six rooms' chosen `night` pictures are exactly that (O38 to O42), and every record made so far stays true. The lit room is a second picture under one new attribute, **`lit`**: image `bg <place> lit`, file `images/bg/<stem>_lit.png`, manifest row `{"rev", "kind": "relight", "of": "<stem>", "of_rev"}`, foreground cut `fg_<place>_lit.png`, poses `pose_<place>__<who>__lit.png`, light row under the key `lit`. It is one token with no underscore, so every gate that builds a file name from the attribute (the foreground, the pose, the light row, a `view_` file's hour tables) works as it is written, and the pose tool's name pattern holds; a painted picture is lit by its own name, whose third word the light reader takes. `ART_HOURS` in `art_manifest.rpy` gains `lit`, so that a `lit` name is read to its parent as an hour's name is. **Two pictures are made only where both states are shown.** A room where someone is up every single Night has one Night picture, its `night`, and it shows them up; a room nobody is ever up in has one too, dark.

| rooms | Night pictures | why |
|---|---|---|
| the Library | `night` (dark) and `lit` | a late stay, below. Both exist (7b); the lit one, kept as `library_night_lit`, is installed as `library_lit.png` |
| the five friends' rooms | `night` (dark: asleep, or out) and `lit` (up, one small lamp) | a friend who is up has a lamp on, and one who is asleep has not (O42). This ends 4.1's one shared picture. The room Panda and Toge share has someone up on every Night but Toge's late one, so its dark picture is shown on Wednesdays only, with Panda asleep in it |
| My Room; the dojo | `night` (dark) and `lit` | the place view shows `night`: nobody but me is up in My Room, and nobody in the dojo. The lit ones are for the script's own scenes, through the helper below: five in My Room, where I am up at my desk or have visitors, and the dojo's ("the dojo lamps are down to one"). My Room's exists (3b, version A, with its lamp on); the dojo's is painted with its other hours, **with every lamp on: the owner refused it with one (O48), and the line's one lamp does not decide the picture** |
| the infirmary; the Teachers' Dormitory | `night` only, lamps on | someone is up there on all seven Nights (Shoko, chosen so in O41; Yaga at his bench). Each keeps words of its own in the batch table |
| the Common Room, the Cafeteria, the classroom, the two corridors, the Teachers' Lounge, the training hall, the storehouse, the Onsen | `night` only, lights off (the storehouse by the moon through its door and its barred window, now that it has them: O45) | nobody is up there at Night by any table. The lit renders that exist for the Common Room (3b) and for the girls' corridor (4b) stay in the early folder |
| the three offices, the pool; the sauna | none; one picture at every hour | shut at Night (4.1); lit by its stove |

**The reader.** `bg_variant(loc, per)` keeps its signature (contract B6). At Night it answers `lit` when the place has a lit picture that is registered, loadable and fresh against its day picture, with its own foreground cut where the room has one, **and** `world_night_up(loc)` is true. Otherwise it answers exactly what it answers today, the `night` cell or None, so K7's fallback is unchanged and with no lit picture installed nothing differs anywhere. `world_night_up(loc)`:

- is false whenever there is no Night to ask about: the hour asked is not Night, or the store holds no hour yet (init, lint, the dump: the test is `getattr(store, "period", None)`, not only an init test). Memory tiles are built at init and so show the plain `night`;
- in a replay, is whether the memory being replayed names a person, read from what the replay already holds (`_mem_label`, `_mem_args`), and false when the memory is not found. A memory's room is lit by its own cast, never by the weekday on which the journal happens to be opened. No memory has a Night in a two-picture room today (the rooftop, the infirmary and the girls' corridor are theirs), so this clause is for rooms to come;
- otherwise, is whether anyone on the roster has `char_location(who) == loc` and is not asleep (`world_asleep`). It does not read `who_is_here`, which adds whoever walks with me to the place I stand in.

Lit pictures are a table of their own beside `WORLD_VARIANTS`, `WORLD_LIT = ((place, file), ...)`, registered the way `world_variants_register` registers: the frozen 56 rows, their lint and the harness count are untouched. `_BG_CACHE`'s key gains the attribute, so the two pictures of one place's Night are two cache rows. **`_light_key` stays `(loc, per)`** (a frozen line pins it, and only a tinted picture carries it), and `lighting.rpy` is not edited. Every new name in `sb_world.rpy` begins `world_` or `WORLD_`, as its row in `tools/prefixes.json` asks. The picture is chosen each time the place is drawn; with the companion rule above, nothing changes it while I stand in a room, and a scene's picture, once shown, does not flip.

**Written scenes.** A scene that names `bg <place> night` shows the room dark. A scene that needs the lit room asks through one gated helper, `world_lit_bg(place)`: the lit picture if it is installed and fresh, with its cut, else `graded_bg(place, "Night")`. It does not ask who is there: the scene says so. **By 8.5 the hour a scene shows waits for the story phase, and this package re-points no scene line;** U9 re-points five, the exception below. For the story phase, the lines that should ask for a lit room: the dojo's late scene (`events_arc1.rpy:913`, which shows the day dojo today); the infirmary's two night scenes and the Common Room's, which show day pictures (`events_arc1.rpy:967`, `:1265`, `:1135`); and the three memories set in the girls' corridor at Night, which move to her room (4.3).

**The exception, My Room.** Five written scenes show `bg your_room night`: my first night (`ch2_tour.rpy:381`), Panda's and Toge's visit "at eight in the evening" (`events_arc1.rpy:381`), and three letters found at Evening (`story_kurose.rpy:119`; `story_oda.rpy:23`, `:95`). Today that picture has the room's lamps on. The night the owner chose has them off (O38), so on the day it is installed all five go dark, in scenes where I am reading at my desk or have company. **Decided (O47):** U9 points the five at `world_lit_bg("dorm")`, each keeping its own `with`; no save and no harness rests on any of them. It makes K10's, 8.1's and D37's seven lines twelve, makes these five 8.5's stated exception, and adds `ch2_tour.rpy` (one line, on which no save rests), `story_kurose.rpy` and `story_oda.rpy` to U9's files; until My Room's pictures are installed the five show what they show today.

**Some nights someone stays out late.** A table `WORLD_LATE = {who: (place, days)}` puts a person in a room at Night on named weekdays. It is read in `char_location`'s routine branch after the away table, so a story pin or a flag schedule still wins, and it is init data: nothing is saved and no save is migrated. Someone out late is not asleep (`world_asleep` is true only at home) and is "out" of their home. Lint: the person is awake on each of those Nights (never on that night's `WORLD_ASLEEP` list); the place is indoors, is nobody's home, has no Bond door, has a row in `WORLD_LIT`, and is not one of the places shut at Night, which are named in a tuple for this (a harness line holds the tuple equal to what `place_access` answers). **Approved by the owner on 11 Oct (O47):**

| who | where | which Nights | from | what it costs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Megumi | the Library | Sunday | the owner's own example, "someone studying late"; his caption there exists ("three books deep") | one pose later, on the lit Library |
| Toge | the Library | Wednesday | he reads there every Evening; his caption exists | the shared room's dark picture, with Panda's asleep shape painted on it, and one pose on the lit Library |

- **This amends O18** ("at the lastest hour. Have everyone in there respective room"): at Night everyone is in their own room except whoever the table has out late that Night. It was put to the owner in those words, and agreed (O47).
- No stay falls on a Tuesday, a Friday or a Saturday, the Nights the harnesses fix (10.3), and Megumi is still up in his own room on Saturdays, so he can still be visited awake.
- **What is offered by someone out late:** Talk and "Ask them along", as in their own room at Night; nothing else, by the night rule as it stands (sitting out has its own places, and telling something true costs a period). Neither of the two gives a mission today. Whoever writes one for them in the story phase gives it hours, or it would open in the Library at Night.
- **Maki in the dojo was considered and left out.** The script has her there past eleven, but the dojo at Night with her in it would offer the Grade 3 promotion fight, her first character mission and, after a confession, "Stay the night", whose scene is a bedroom (`sb_missions.rpy`, `sb_activities.rpy`). Closing each of those is more than a late light is worth; the dojo's lit picture is made for the scene.
- On a late Night the stayer's own room opens as it always does, empty and dark. The journal's "right now" line, the map's pin and the room's caption already say where the person is, and the "usually" line is left alone, because it is not usual.
- **What a late stay cannot show:** a yard's one Night picture has each building's windows as they usually are, so the Library's lamps are seen from inside only.

**Pictures this adds** (5.13). The dark rooms of the five friends (priced there as ten renders) and the dojo's `lit`, as one sitting of their own, **sitting 47**, because sittings 9, 10, 11 and 15 are already at twelve renders; the dojo's was painted early, with `art_15b.json`, and is counted in 47. The Library's and My Room's lit pictures exist. **Which sitting makes which:** sittings 9 to 11 make each friend's room with its lamp on, which is its `lit`; sitting 47 makes its dark `night`; and the asleep poses (sitting 27) wait for 47. In the batch table the friends' `night` rows of sittings 9 to 11 become their `lit` rows and sitting 47 carries their new `night` rows, lights off: **U0n writes this into `tools/art/batches.json` with the tool's counts,** because the table cannot hold a `lit` row before the tools know the hour; **every other room's `night` row is given its own words before its sitting** (lights off, or for the infirmary and the Teachers' Dormitory their lamps), because the tool's general indoor sentence becomes the `lit` one. The tool counts 64 hour pictures in three places; with all of this it is 72 (the five friends' second picture, the dojo's, the Library's and My Room's). With the poses, later: the six awake-at-home rows of the friends (7.2, rows 16 to 21) are painted on `lit` and the asleep rows on `night`, Panda's and Toge's asleep shapes on `lit` beside the other one awake; Shoko's, Gojo's and Yaga's night rows (22 to 24) stay on `night`; and three more for the table above (Megumi and Toge in the lit Library, Panda asleep in the dark).

**Tools.** `lit` joins the two-value hour lists: `install_redo.py --relight <place> lit` (target `<stem>_lit`, with its light row and its foreground cut), `make_redo_workflow.py` (lists and sentences), `sample_light.py`, `game_layout.py` (`periods_showing("lit")` is Night, which the pose installer's "can show together" test reads), `art_status.py` (the list and its words), `art_batch.py` (the three counts and the twelve-render rule), `tools/test_art_tools.py` (its pairs, relight cases and pose tables) and `tools/smoke/make_shape_overlay.py`. `check_content.py --layout-files` learns the light key `lit`, and refuses a `Night` key in a `view_` file's `slot_light` or `cast` for a place that has a lit picture, since that key would serve both. **One thing found that is not this package's:** `game_layout.py`'s `where()`, `asleep()` and `away()` read keys the dump does not write (it writes them under `world`, in another shape), so the pose tools read none of the three tables today. U0d3, which owns the file, points them at the dump's real keys; this package then teaches them the late table.

**The package: U0n, the last of wave 0**, after U0d3 and before checkpoint A (the second draft's U0e is not this; the name is not reused). In `game/`: `sb_world.rpy` (the two tables, the tuple of places shut at Night, the registration, the choice in `bg_variant`, `world_night_up`, `world_lit_bg`, the cache key, `char_location`'s branch, lint) and `art_manifest.rpy` (`ART_HOURS`). Not `lighting.rpy`, and not `sb_memories.rpy`, which has no row in the prefixes table and may gain no name. The dump gains two keys at its top level, beside `variants_planned`, and none under `world`, whose keys a line pins. **The contracts:** section 9's sentence ("final after U0c") and the contracts' own header are amended to name this addition as the second sitting's was (its B15: nothing changes shape, rows are gained); rows are gained in A3 (the dump's two keys), A4 (the light key, what an hour is, the manifest's keys), B2, B3, B6 and B13 (the tools' command lines); `u2_contracts.rpy` gains the lines that pin them and no line of it is edited. **U0n lands before any lit file is installed.** *Acceptance:* the standard set; with no lit picture installed, `bg_variant` and `bg_name` for every place and hour, and a screenshot of each room at Night, are what they were; with a test lit picture of the Library: lit with Megumi there, lit still when he is walking with me in it, dark with nobody, dark with someone walking with me whose table has them elsewhere, dark with only someone asleep, dark when the lit file is stale or its foreground cut is missing, and nothing different at Evening; the two are two cache rows and `_light_key` is unchanged; a replay of a memory that names a person shows the lit room, one that is not found shows `night`, and `bg_name` with no hour in the store answers as today; on a Sunday Night Megumi is in the Library, awake and "out" of his home, and on a Saturday he is at home; a story pin beats the table; lint refuses a stayer who is asleep that night, a Bond door, a home, a shut place and a yard; the thirty saves load.

**Harness lines it changes** (10.3; the rows are written there and in `docs/UI2_PINS.md` before the package starts: D43, K13). `u2_home.rpy:1163-1180`, "on each of the seven Nights everyone who lives on campus is in their own home", becomes "in their own home, or where `WORLD_LATE` puts them that Night"; the copy of the old schedule in `wp1_world.rpy` learns the table as it learned the away table. These two are the only lines that fail on a Sunday or a Wednesday Night; a dozen others cross those Nights and hold, two of them (`p2_hud.rpy`, `p2_contracts.rpy`) only because their roster has no Toge on that day. With the poses, not before: U3's pose check (`p2_view.rpy:714-740`) reads a row's picture from `TIME_VARIANT` or `WORLD_LIT`, and a `lit` row's hours from the Night cells of the schedule and the late table.

**Sentences elsewhere that this made false** were brought into line in one pass on 11 Oct, after the owner's answers (Method note 35).

**Left to others, and said here so it is not lost.** U1 lands D9 (Gojo is drawn into no place at Night); until then his draw can stand him in a room that has one Night picture, which is then shown as it is. Two lock lines say there is light in a shut office at Night (`sb_world.rpy`, Utahime's and Gojo's offices; U1's own harness pins the first), where the tables have her asleep at home and him off campus: proposed for U1, "Her door is shut and the room is dark." and "Gojo's office is locked. He left the television on."

**The owner's answers (O47, 11 Oct): yes to all three.** (1) The table of late stays, Megumi on Sundays and Toge on Wednesdays in the Library, which amends "at the latest hour everyone is in their own room". (2) Who lights a room: not me, and someone walking with me only in the room they would be in anyway. (3) My Room's five scenes are pointed at the lamp-on picture now, by U9.

### 5.8 The missing backgrounds (every stand-in)

No layout sits on any of these except the alley's lamp, so none needs a sitting. Each is a new picture made from words and the look, in two versions for the owner to choose from (D52).

**Nothing lends any more (D22).** Today flat files are borrowed by other pictures through tinted crops: the city at night lends to the alley, Shibuya, Shinjuku, the mall's front and the warehouse; the morning street to Kawasaki and Yotsuka; the town street to Harajuku; the ruined hall to the mall's inside and the annexe; `veil.png` to the mall's atrium and the ridge's veil; and the board's eight jobs play straight over `bg ruin`, `bg city`, `bg veil`, `bg alley dark` and `bg town_street` (`sb_board.rpy:35-44`). Painting a lender would change several stand-ins at once, and a rule that the lender waits for its borrowers deadlocks (a lender could never go in while one of its borrowers was unmade). So the scaffold (U0c) copies each of the five flat files as it is today to `game/images/bg/standin/<name>.png`, points every tinted stand-in in `bg_placeholders.rpy` at the copy, **defines the ten new image names that always exist, each with its stand-in** (the mall's three, the warehouse, the annexe and the five job sites; the club and the two district nights are defined only when their file exists, and the market is a file, not an image name: 3.1), and sets the board's job-to-picture column to the five `site_*` names. From then on each picture below replaces only itself, in any order, and leaving one unmade blocks nothing. (Two jobs change stand-in that day, because they share a kind with another: the Edogawa underpass and the Kita stairwell show the tinted hall; the checkpoint A note says so.)

| group | pictures | how each reaches the game |
|---|---|---|
| **Prologue** (9) | `alley` (lit), `alley_dark` (relit from it), `diner`, `home_living`, `home_bedroom_night`, `car` (seen from the back seat, the gap between the front seats in the middle, the driver's seat empty), and `street_morning`, `town_street`, `city_night` | `alley` and `alley_dark` drop in by name (`bg_placeholders.rpy`); the others replace a flat file of the same name. No scene line changes. |
| **The Sun Mall and the Nade sites** (5) | `mall_front`, `mall_hall`, `mall_atrium`, `warehouse`, `annexe` | new image names (U0c) with today's borrow as the stand-in; the `scene` lines that should show them are swapped by U9 (8.1), with the picture the fight inside the warehouse and the annexe plays on (an argument of its `call combat` line; without it the owner would buy the warehouse and then fight in a city street). **If U9's swaps are skipped (10.6), these ten renders are not made** |

30 pictures, 60 renders. With them, in batch 32, the Tokyo map (`map_tokyo`) is redone in place in the look, with the outdoor keep text: one more picture, 2 renders; its district markers stay where they are.

**The alley and the lamp.** `LAST_LIGHT_LAMP = (1718, 108)` is a `define` inside `ch1_alley.rpy`, and this plan does not open that file. The alley's prompt asks for "one lit street lamp high in the top right corner", and `install_redo.py --anchor 1718,108 --at <x,y>` shifts the approved render (padding by repeating its edge) so the bulb lands on the point; `wp9_prologue`'s screenshot of the Last Light moment checks the glow sits within 12 px of the painted lamp.

**Light.** Each new dark picture gets a measured row in `game/layout/light.json`, read by `light_for_bg` before `BG_LIGHT`, so people stop standing daylit in a dark alley (AUDIT B19); the rows that exist were tuned to the flat stand-ins.

### 5.9 The campus map and the area maps: made last

"Once we have all the mini locations. We can construct a accurate main map around them." (O20.) The seven maps are the last campus pictures made, after every place on them has its chosen picture (K4, K8), and the owner approves them at checkpoint C. Until then the area views and the campus map keep today's pictures, with the new places' pins placed clear on them (4.8); every pin still goes to the right place.

- **Six area maps,** composed as bird's-eye pictures from the south, with `map.txt` in front, not `stage.txt` (5.1) (north stays up, so left on the map is left in a north-facing yard). Each is built from the finished places: its references are the chosen picture of the area's yard, the crops of every building in the area (`tools/bgs/refs/`), and that area's piece of `campus_plan_ref.png`, the colour-block sketch `campus_plan.py` draws from the record's fixed facts; its words put the buildings at the pin targets of 4.8 with clear ground where the pins will sit. Two versions each. The Courtyard & Gate map is made first, alone (batch 21a): it is the test of whether the editor paints a map that agrees with finished places (5.2).
- **The campus map,** a painted bird's-eye picture of the whole school with the six chosen area maps as references, mountains across the whole top (their main mass behind the Onsen and the Riverside, where the Mountain's marker is: 4.8), the gate at the bottom, each other quarter where its marker already is. Two versions (batch 22). Installed with `map_campus.json`. Until it exists the flat drawing shows.
- **If the owner asks for a change on a map** at checkpoint C, the map is run again; a place's picture is not reopened for the map's sake (the map is built round the places, not the other way).

### 5.10 The main menu's picture and the menu ground

- The scaffold breaks two borrowings: `gui.main_menu_background = bg_placeholder("gui/title.png", "images/bg/gate.png")` and `gui.game_menu_background = bg_placeholder("gui/menu_ground.png", "images/bg/veil.png")` (`bg_placeholder` is defined at init −3, before `gui.rpy`'s −2), and it copies today's `veil.png` to `game/gui/menu_ground.png` and today's `gate.png` to `game/gui/title.png`. (Without the second copy the lookup falls back to `images/bg/gate.png`, and the main menu would change at batch 2, the second sitting, to a gate picture not composed for today's large name.) Until the owner chooses a mock-up the main menu shows exactly what it shows today, and the gate and the veil can be repainted at any time.
- **The main menu is a screen to design, not a picture to pick** (O25: "This was more aimed at our main menu screen."; 8.3 has the screen). It is late in the order (the owner's "isnt as important"). **At checkpoint C the owner chooses between three mock-ups.** Each is a real screenshot of the `main_menu` screen in one of three layouts, with the game's name set and the menu in place, over a picture cut free from the campus pictures that exist by then:
  1. *The gate.* The Main Gate's evening picture: the gatehouse over its garden water at blue hour, lanterns lit. The name set large in the dark left third, the menu in a column under it.
  2. *The gateway.* The Courtyard's night picture, the lanterns either side of the gateway lit. The name centred above the gateway's roof, the menu in one row along the bottom.
  3. *The veil.* The ridge's night picture, the small dark dome on its peak under the night sky. The name low on the right, the menu in a column on the right, the picture left open.
  The owner may take one, or the layout of one over the picture of another.
- **Who makes the mock-ups, and what shows meanwhile.** U6 builds the three layouts in wave 1 behind `gui.sys_menu_layout`, whose default, `"today"`, is today's layout with Continue: the main menu does not change before the owner chooses. The three pictures come later (the Courtyard's night in batch 7, the gate's evening and the ridge's night in batch 18), so the screenshots are taken by the session that prepares checkpoint C, with U6's harness `u2_menu_mock` (8.3). After the owner chooses, the chosen layout shows over its mock-up picture (L copies that picture to `game/gui/title.png`) until batch 34 replaces it.
- **Then its picture is painted** (batch 34, two versions): the chosen subject composed for the chosen layout, with the campus picture it was mocked up on as its reference and a quiet, dark enough area exactly where the name and the menu sit, so that nothing in the picture runs under the name. In the mock-ups the name is set as text in the game's display face; a drawn logo is a word away at checkpoint C.
- **The menu ground** is then made from that picture by `tools/gen_gui.py` (blurred, darkened to the ink colour): free.

### 5.12 Poses

70, in steps (7.2 has the list: 32, the 35 the owner said yes to on 7 Oct, O28, and the three that D61's late stays add, 5.7a), each in two versions for the owner to choose from. A pose is painted on the picture the player sees at that hour, so each waits for its room and its hour picture (`make_pose_workflow.py` reads the installed game file and refuses otherwise). The two that exist are painted on pictures being replaced, so both stop showing the day their room is installed (K7) and are remade. The pose prompt gains the person's standing height in pixels at that spot, so a seated person is no longer drawn large: the number comes from the room's `scale`, read from its `view_` file first and, when the room has not had its layout sitting yet, from the same triple in its keep list (5.4; U0d writes it for the Common Room, so batch 5's prompt has it before wave 1). With neither, the tool refuses in one line. It refuses a `POSES` row at the Bathhouse, the Sauna or the Pool (K16), and its sentence that keeps the person's face, hair, clothes and colours as their sprite's is frozen. Utahime's pose (7.2 row 2, awake at her desk) carries her sprite and, in plain words, "long dark hair, a ribbon, a scar across the face" (AUDIT P12). The prompt also asks for "the same clean flat anime cel shading as the reference figure, a soft contact shadow only". **These changes, the order check of K8, and the manifest row and `pose_<place>.json` box that `install_pose.py` writes are U0d's, in wave 0,** so the first pose test (batch 5) is made with the tooling it is testing and shows the day it is installed: without a manifest row a pose on a revised picture counts as stale (5.5) and would not show. U3 only re-picks `POSES`. **Two people at one piece of furniture** (7.2 rows 12 and 13, at the Library's table) are each given a named, separate seat in `POSES`; the second is made after the first is installed, and `install_pose.py` refuses a box that overlaps another installed pose box of the same room and hour picture **that can show at the same time**, naming both. (It reads `SCHEDULE`, `WEEKEND`, `WORLD_ASLEEP` and `WORLD_AWAY` from the dump, the last for the three district rows, and the late table too once U0n has taught the tools it, 5.7a, so Nobara's and Maki's boxes in Harajuku are held to the same rule: Megumi reading on his bed and Megumi asleep in it never show together and may share the bed; Toge at the shogi board and Panda asleep beside it do, on weekday Nights, and may not overlap.) The check exists because a pose reaches the game as an opaque box cut from a whole-room render, so an overlap would paint empty room over the other person. **A row whose `state` is `asleep`** (rows 27 to 32) is not a figure at all: the tool writes one fixed sentence for it, "in the bed, a sleeper under a blanket, turned to the wall; only the top of the hair shows; no face, no arm, no shoulder", with the person's sprite as the reference for hair colour only (for Panda: "a very large mound under a blanket"). That sentence is frozen with the clothes sentence (K16), and such a patch is drawn as part of the room, never as a person: it cannot be clicked. (No teacher has an asleep row: Utahime and Ijichi asleep are behind their doors and are never painted, 7.2.)

**The 35 of O28** (7.2 rows 33 to 67; batches 41 to 46) are made by the same tool under the same rules, and four things are said for them.

- **People who share a room.** With every cell painted, a room can hold several painted people at once (the cafeteria at weekday Noon holds five; the Common Room in the Evening three; the training hall, the riverside, the Courtyard, the lounge and Harajuku two each). The rule above is kept and widened by one clause: **every pose that can show with another in the same room and hour picture has a named place of its own in `POSES`, and is made in a later sitting than the one it shares the room with,** so the earlier pose is installed, and its box is known to `install_pose.py`, before the later one is written. That is why batches 41 to 46 are grouped by person (a person is never in a room twice) and are run in order. Two at one piece of furniture still follow the rule of rows 12 and 13: Panda and Toge at the cafeteria's back table (rows 57 and 63) and at their shogi board by day (rows 59 and 66) sit at named ends, Toge made after Panda is installed.
- **A pose keeps the room usable.** `install_pose.py` also refuses a box that covers an exit point of its room, naming the point (it reads the points from the dump, as it reads the other boxes); the spot is then moved and the pose run again from the allowance. A box that covers a hotspot point is refused only when that hotspot is not the object the row is painted at: a `POSES` row may name one hotspot id in `at` (the reading table, Shoko's desk, the shogi board, the workbench, the photograph, the railing, the straw bundles, the weapon case), and a box over that one is accepted. For a moved action the tile returns to the column while the pose shows, as it already does when a standing person is in front of the object (`hot_covers`, 7.3); a look's point is set clear of the seat at the room's layout sitting. So no pose of 7.2 that sits at the thing its caption names is refused for sitting there, the pose test (row 3, at the Library's reading table) among them. With one or two poses a room the spots were chosen by hand to be clear; with up to five, and yards of five to seven exits, the tool checks. These refusals, and `at`, are U0d's, with the overlap refusal.
- **A spot names what the chosen picture has.** The spots of rows 33 to 67 are written from today's pictures and the plan's descriptions. When a room's chosen picture does not have the thing a spot names (a table by the window, a rock, a far sofa), the row is re-pointed, one line, at that room's layout sitting, before its batch's file is written; the caption does not change.
- **A pose in Tokyo** (rows 60, 61, 67). A district is not a campus place: it has no stage, and friends are shown there by `tky_people` (7.6). So: `make_pose_workflow.py` accepts a `POSES` row whose place is a district that `WORLD_AWAY` sends that person to, and applies the order check of K8 to the district's picture (batch 32); the height it puts in the prompt comes from a `scale` triple L measures on the district's picture the day it is installed and writes to that picture's keep list (the same key the Common Room's has); the pose is painted where the person's sprite stands today (`pos_cl`, `pos_c`, `pos_cr`), so a painted friend and a standing one beside them never overlap; and the patch is drawn by the `tky_meet` screen, never by a `show` statement, so no pose file's path is ever in a save (7.6; U5; K9). `view_pose` is unchanged: a district has no hour pictures, so it looks for the day pose, and its stale gate (5.5) hides a pose painted on a district picture that has since been replaced.

Megumi in the forest (row 40) is painted with one Divine Dog, because his caption there says so. The dog is part of his picture, inside his box, and is not a second person; it has no reference sprite, so the prompt describes it in words ("a large white wolf-like dog with a dark mark on its forehead"). If neither version draws it well, the row is painted without the dog and the caption becomes "on the trail, hands in his pockets, listening" (one `AMBIENT` line, U4's).

### 5.13 Every batch

**The order is the owner's** (O20, D53): the main locations first, working outwards from the Courtyard; then the rooms off each; then the new places; the maps last. **Every picture is made in two versions** (O21, D52), so a sitting of twelve renders is six pictures to choose.

One page of numbered steps each (`~/Desktop/JJK Game images/ui2/art_<n>.md`), **twelve renders or fewer**, and no single run over ten paid boxes (the record: a rate-limit failure aborts a run), so a sitting of twelve is always two files. **"a then b" is two files and two short runs in one sitting, with a pause between them:** the owner runs a, says in a session which version of each picture they choose (a picture with a new window line or an addition always comes back as a LOOK), the session installs them (about ten minutes for rooms redone in place; about fifty minutes a picture for a new place, whose layout is done then, so b may be left for another day), and only then can b's file be written, because b is made from a's installed pictures (K7, K8). That is batches 3, 4, 6 to 13, 15 to 19, 21, 24 and 28: eighteen. Batches 14, 23, 25, 27, 32, 33, 41, 42, 43, 45, 46 and 47 are two files with no pause (their halves do not depend on each other): twelve. Batch 44 is one file of ten. Batches 35 and 36 are five short files each, one per person. Batches 37 to 40 are the four people (5.11). `tools/art/batches.json` holds this table; `tools/art_batch.py <n>` writes the workflows and the note. All prices are Nano Banana Pro at 2K, $0.16 a render. "E, N" is a place's evening and its night.

**Totals:** 440 background, map, pose, icon and face renders ($70.40: 40 in batches 1 to 5, 178 in batches 6 to 22, 140 in batches 23 to 36, 70 in batches 41 to 46, 12 in batch 47), six curse renders ($0.96) and the four people (about $5, about 25 paid repair boxes): **about 446 renders and the four people, about $76,** in **forty-seven sittings** after the free batch 0 (eighteen are two files with a pause, twelve two files with no pause, two are five short files each, four are the people, and the other eleven are single short sittings). Installed: 35 place pictures, 72 hour pictures (the 64 of the hour table and the eight `lit` pictures of 5.7a; two of the eight, the Library's and My Room's, are renders already made in 7b and 3b) and `ridge_veil`, 8 maps (the Tokyo map among them), 30 stand-in replacements, the main menu's picture, 70 poses (the 67 of these sittings and the three of 5.7a, below), 18 blink-and-talk pictures, 7 sprite and curse files (about 240), and 26 icons, plus free foreground cuts and the free menu ground. Allow one render in five run again: **$92.** The three look tests already spent about $2.57 (sixteen renders). The fallbacks of 5.2, the third try for a scene-named relight (5.7), the office or my room installed without its addition (5.3) and the canary of 5.2 come out of the allowance.

**Outside those counts** (D57): batches 0b and 0c, seven renders in two short sittings, about $1.12, and the look tests the owner ran before the build (6 and 7 Oct). **Also outside them, until they have a sitting:** the three poses that D61's late stays add (5.7a: Megumi and Toge in the lit Library, Panda asleep in the dark shared room), two versions each, 6 renders, about $0.96. No pose sitting has room for all three (25 and 27 are at twelve renders, 26 and 44 at ten), so where they are run is not settled; it is named when their rows are written (7.2).

| the Tokyo map in the look | +1 | +2 | |

Nothing was removed to pay for any of it, and nothing is on a list to be cut for cost.

**Compromises found and left, each with what it would add** (none is a matter of money now; each is the owner's time, so each is said, not taken; one has since been taken by the owner's ruling, and its row says so):

| left as it is | what the better way would add |
|---|---|
| blink and talk on the calm face only (8.1's known cost) | about 110 renders, about $17.60, ten sittings; a later pass (section 11) |
| one version of each blink-and-talk frame | 18 renders, two sittings, and nothing to choose between: left on purpose (D52) |
| five kinds of job site shared by the board's eight jobs | 3 pictures, 6 renders, about $1, one sitting, and three more image names in the contracts |
| the seven campus maps tinted by the hour (4.8), not painted for the evening and the night | 14 pictures, 28 renders, about $4.50, three sittings, after batch 22 |
| one Night picture for each friend's room, one small lamp lit, shared by a friend who is up and one who is asleep (4.1). **No longer left: taken on 11 Oct** (O42, D61, O47) | 5 more pictures (a dark room for the asleep), 10 renders, about $1.60: they are batch 47, with the dojo's `lit`, and the second Night image name for each room is `bg <place> lit` (5.7a) |
| one face each for Mom, Dad, Kurose and Nade | not cost: second faces wait for the scenes that use them (5.11) |

**Order within the table.** Batches 1 and 2 come first and in that order (everything is composed outwards from them). After that a batch can be run whenever its "needs first" is met; in practice batch 3 follows at once (the canary's baseline is made in it, and three wordings are tested). Batches 16 and 19 can be brought forward of checkpoint B if the owner would rather judge the pool and the onsen as themselves there; run that early, the pool's evening (in 16b, an outdoor relight) waits for 7a to be judged, and its day picture does not. Batches 21 and 22 are last among the campus pictures by rule (K4). Everything after batch 22 can stop at any point with the game whole, and can be run in any order its "needs first" allows. **Batches 41 to 46 are the one run with an order of their own:** 42 after 41, 43 after 42, 45 after 43 and 44, 46 after 45 (each keeps its people clear of poses the earlier ones installed); 44 needs none of the other five. They are numbered last so that no earlier number moves, not because they must be run last: 41 to 44 can be run any time after batch 27, and 45 and 46 any time after batch 32, if the owner would rather have the campus's people finished before the pictures off campus. Batch 47 is numbered last for the same reason and is a campus sitting: it can be run as soon as batches 9 to 11 and 15 are chosen and U0n has landed, and batch 27 waits for it.

## 6. Blending: people who stand in the room

The owner's sentence was read as: a shadow under them, colours matched to the room's light, a believable size and floor line, furniture in front of them where it belongs; and, after the rooms are redone, painted poses (section 7). Today the game has a simple form of each: one soft shadow blob, the same shape everywhere; one hand-set tint per room or per hour; one sole line for every room; three rooms with a foreground; eight rooms with fitted standing rows; and nothing that measures any of it.

**The one constraint everything here is built round (K9).** A sprite on screen during a save is saved as the displayable `lit_sprite` built. Anything added to that tree is in every save made mid-scene, for good. So nothing is added to it. Blending is done in three places that are safe: **data** the existing `_sprite_light` and `_shadow_light` already read; **`view_sprite` and the stage screens**, which build their displayables in the screen's `add` line on every draw and never reach a save; and **sidecars and polygons**.

### 6.1 The room's data

`game/layout/view_<place>.json`, hand-authored at the place's layout sitting (L); every key is optional and has today's behaviour as its default.

| key | holds | how it is got | read by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `slots` | where 1, 2, 3, 4 people stand: `[x centre, y offset, zoom, far]` | proposed by `tools/stage_preview.py --propose` from `floor` and `scale`, corrected by eye on the preview | `view_slots` (exists; the sidecar already wins over code) |
| `poses` | pose boxes; from this overhaul they are written to their own file, `game/layout/pose_<place>.json`, so an install never touches a `view_` file a package has open (K15) | `install_pose.py` (exists) | `view_pose` (the `pose_` file first, then this key) |
| `floor` | one or two polygons of floor or ground a person may stand on, and optional polygons they may not (the pond, the railing, a table top) | traced on the picture | the preview and `check_layout.py` only (never the game, except `view_floor` for the review tools) |
| `scale` | `[x, y top, y bottom]` of one door or post beside where people stand, taken as 200 cm | read off the picture | the preview and `check_layout.py` only |
| `slot_light` | per hour and per slot, a small `[tint, brightness]` on top of the room's row, for a slot in a sun patch or in shade | the sampler, at each slot's own rectangle; left out where the difference is small | `view_sprite` |
| `cast` | outdoors, per hour: `[direction, length, darkness]` of a long shadow on the ground | judged from the picture's own shadows | `view_stage` |
| `talk` | the foreground stays up in dialogue | by eye, on the dialogue preview | `view_enter` |

`game/layout/light_<place>.json`, written by the installer: `{"": row, "evening": row, "night": row}` (and `"lit": row` where the room has a lit picture, 5.7a), a row being `[tint, brightness, saturation, shadow opacity]` or the same with `[..., shadow lean in px, shadow stretch]`.

All 35 places get a `floor`, a `scale` and `slots` (today 16 of 24 stand people on default spots: the pond, the railing and the sweets table are among them). Rooms redone in place have theirs written on today's picture, because nothing moves, in L's in-place series beside wave 1 (10.2), **together with the room's hotspot points and its moved exit**: 6.3's rules (no slot row hides a hotspot) and 4.6's (what a person's rectangle pushes into the column) tie where people stand to where things are, so one hand places both. Composed pictures and new places get theirs at their own layout sitting.

### 6.2 What is drawn, and where

| # | effect | drawn by | data | reaches | in a save? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Light matched to the picture, by measurement,** for every place and every hour picture | the existing `_sprite_light`; `light_for_place` reads the sidecar row first, then `PLACE_LIGHT`, then the default | `light_<place>.json` | everyone lit, wherever they are drawn: the stage, the person view, dialogue, story scenes, the board | `scene_light`, the tuple that is saved today |
| 2 | **Light tied to the tint where the hour is only a tint.** At Night in a yard the picture is washed `#6f86d8`, −0.34 and people only `#a8b6e8`, −0.10, so they glow. The sprite's light for a tinted hour becomes the day row moved 60% of the way to the background's own grade, so the two tables can no longer drift apart | `light_for_place` | one constant, `LIGHT_FOLLOW = 0.6`, and the grade tables that exist | everyone lit, in every place whose hour picture is not made yet (once every hour is painted, 5.7, this is the fallback only) | no |
| 3 | **A shadow that follows the room's light.** The blob under the feet leans away from the light and stretches when the light is low; it is fainter in flat light and stronger under a lamp | the existing `_shadow_light` (same name, same signature): besides `alpha` it sets `xoffset` and `xzoom` on the shadow's own Transform | two more values at the end of a light row; a four-value row means (0, 1.0). `light_matrix` reads the first three values of a row of either length | everyone lit by `lit_sprite`. In dialogue it is behind the text box, so it pays off on the stage and the person view | old saves hold four values and the code accepts four or six |
| 4 | **Size and floor line per room** | `view_slots` | `slots` | the stage, and where the person view's ease starts | no |
| 5 | **A different light per standing spot** | `view_sprite`: the per-slot `matrixcolor` it already sets for hover and distance also takes the slot's `[tint, brightness]` | `slot_light` | the stage only | no |
| 6 | **Furniture in front of people,** in more rooms (the table below), and **kept in front while talking** where the room's sidecar says `talk` | `view_fg` and `view_enter`, as today | polygons in `tools/fg/`; `talk` | the stage; dialogue in rooms marked `talk` | the foreground's file path, as the cafeteria's is today: those files are never renamed or deleted (K9) |
| 7 | **The others stay in the room** while I look at one person, dimmed and one blur step back, not clickable. In the conversation itself they step out, as now | `screen view_others(loc, who)` in `view_stage.rpy`, used from `sb_char` by one line the scaffold places | – | the person view | no |
| 8 | **A painted person stays painted** while their card is open (D31) | `view_focus` keeps the pose patch in place; the sprite eases in only when a scene starts | – | the person view | no (screen only; if the scaffold's test shows otherwise, this row is dropped and today's fade stays) |
| 9 | **A long cast shadow outdoors,** by day and evening | `view_stage`: one more `add` under each standing person, the shadow picture squashed, skewed and dimmed; a fixed transform, no function | `cast` | the stage only | no |
| 10 | **Panda fits:** slot choice and the foreground test use each person's own canvas width (880 for Panda), so he is no longer sliced by the cafeteria table's layer | `view_pick_slots` | – | the stage | no |
| 11 | **The stand-in people stop being flat:** Mom, Dad, Kurose and Nade become `lit_sprite` sprites (8.2) and get the shadow and the room's light with them | – | – | story scenes | by image name |
| 12 | **Someone asleep is not on the stage** (D48). No sprite, no shadow, no name tag, nothing to click. Once their blanket shape is painted (7.2 rows 27 to 32) it is drawn as part of the room, under the hotspots, and takes no focus | `stage_people` leaves them out (one line in `sb_screens.rpy`, laid by U0b); `view_asleep(loc)` in `view_stage.rpy` returns the fresh asleep patches of the room's sleepers, and the stage adds them before the people | `pose_<place>.json`, rows whose `state` is `asleep` | the stage | no |

**Foregrounds after this overhaul** (free, cut from the room's own pixels by `tools/make_fg.py`, recut by the installer on every install of the room or one of its hours):

| room | object | hours |
|---|---|---|
| `dorm_common` | the low table | day, evening, night (recut) |
| `cafeteria` | the near table | day (recut), evening and night (new; the installer copies the day polygon to each hour's key, 5.4) |
| `utahime_office` | the desk | day (recut), evening (new) |
| `office` | the new desk, Yaga behind it (only if the desk holds, 5.3); its polygon is drawn by L at batch 12's sitting, the day the desk exists | day, evening (new) |
| `gojo_office` | the sweets table (his feet are in it today) | day, evening (new) |
| `teachers_lounge` | the sofa's back | day, evening, night (new) |

Not given one: the Library and the infirmary (their tables and desks are filled by poses), the classroom (the desks are too thin to hide anyone behind; Megumi is painted at one instead), the yards, the friends' rooms.

**Not done, and why:** a gradient or rim light with direction over each sprite (a new layer inside `lit_sprite`, in every dialogue save for good, for an effect the text box mostly hides); a second foreground plane; a per-room size in dialogue and story scenes (it means redefining `pos_c` or `view_enter_ease`, and what an old save holding them does afterwards has never been tested); the others staying on screen during the conversation itself. All are on the "later" list (section 11).

**Motion budget (R9):** no new function transform anywhere. The eight a place view may hold stay eight; `p2_view`'s call-count check passes unchanged.

### 6.3 Rules for where people stand

Written into every layout sitting and checked by `check_layout.py` and the preview:

- every near slot's feet are inside a floor polygon and outside every no-stand polygon; a far slot's feet are inside the floor and behind the foreground polygon;
- a person's drawn height at a slot (the near line draws a 173 cm person 596 px tall, about 3.45 px a centimetre) is within 12% of what the room's `scale` says a person that tall measures at that depth;
- name tags stay inside x 179 to 1700, people inside x 40 to 1700 (the existing validator);
- **no slot row for one or two people hides any hotspot of the room,** and no row for one to four people hides the room's only things to click (Megumi no longer stands in front of the television and the card table: AUDIT F5, R12);
- in a yard, the four-person row leaves clear the number of exits 4.6 asks for;
- the Courtyard's vending machine, which is never hidden, stays clear of every slot row.

In a shaped place (D60) these rules are read exactly as written, on the square at each hotspot's point; the shape takes no part in them, and the sitting puts the point on the part of the shape people cover least.

### 6.4 What it costs when drawn

Per standing person: one sprite, one shadow quad, outdoors at most one cast-shadow quad; two 0.25 s light functions (as today), two 0.05 s face functions where blink and talk exist (as today), one breathing function (as today). Per room: one background, at most one foreground layer. On the person view: up to three dimmed sprites more, with static blur. The shadow's lean and stretch are set inside a function every sprite already carries, on its existing tick. Nothing is added per frame.

### 6.5 How it is measured and tested

**Outside the game, on the pictures** (ComfyUI's Python; `tools/test_art_tools.sh` runs all of it and prints PASS, FAIL or one SKIPPED line):

- **`tools/sample_light.py <place> [variant]`** reads the standing rows, takes the band of floor under the soles and the band of wall behind the heads, and proposes the row: tint (the band's mean colour, pulled halfway to white: people are lit by the room, not painted with it), brightness (the floor's luminance against a mid-lit floor, clamped), saturation, shadow opacity (higher under a lamp), lean and stretch (from which side is brighter at head height and how low the light is). The installer writes it; a person may tune it. `--check` fails a hand-edited row that has drifted more than 0.12 in brightness or 0.15 in any tint channel from the sample unless the file says why (`"note"`), so "matched to the room" stays a measured claim and a redone room cannot keep the old room's light.
- **`tools/stage_preview.py <place> [hour] [--who a,b,c] [--bg <render>] [--dialogue]`** draws the stage as the game draws it: the picture under its hour's tint and the ink wash, each person's sprite at their slot's zoom and floor line, the light matrix, the shaped shadow and the cast shadow, the far steps, the foreground on top, every hotspot and exit ring (for a shaped place, D60, each shape's outline, and a second frame with every shape lit and named), the name-tag line. One sheet per place (`shots/stage/<place>.jpg`: one to four people by day, and a frame for each hour picture). It is what a room is judged on **before** its picture is installed (`--bg` takes a render straight from ComfyUI's output folder), what goes in each art note ("here is the room with the people who use it"), and what a reviewer looks at. `--dialogue` draws the scene case (people 23% larger, feet on the bottom edge), because a room that looks right on the stage can look wrong in a scene.
- `check_layout.py` on every `view_` file: 6.3's rules by number.

**In the game** (`tools/smoke/u2_blend.rpy`, with test sidecars under `tools/smoke/assets/u2_blend/`; not pointer-dependent):

- a sidecar's light row, `slot_light` and `cast` are read, win over the tables, and fall back key by key; a stale row is not used;
- with a four-value `scene_light` set by hand (an old save's), `_shadow_light` and `_sprite_light` run without error and leave the lean at 0 and the stretch at 1;
- after Wait, after walking, in a story scene, on a board job and on a Tokyo trip, `scene_light` equals what the picture shown gives (the five set points);
- at Night in a yard with no painted hour the sprite's brightness is within 0.10 of the background's;
- **the saved sprite is unchanged (K9):** a text dump of `lit_sprite(...)` for Yuji (each node's class, its function's name, its curried argument names, its file path) equals `tools/smoke/baseline_sprite_tree.txt`, written by U0a from the untouched tree, and the pickle of what `lit_sprite` returns is byte for byte what it was;
- R10's save test (`p2_view`: save and load on the first line after `stage_person`, in a room with a foreground kept in dialogue, after a rollback, with blink and talk) passes unedited, and again with a pre-overhaul save in which a sprite is showing;
- the count of function transforms on the place view is unchanged; with Motion off, zero;
- the person view with four people on stage: three dimmed, none focusable;
- **the asleep:** with Megumi asleep in his room (a weekday Night), `who_is_here` holds him and `stage_people` does not; no sprite, shadow or name tag is drawn for him; with a test asleep patch in `pose_megumi_room.json` the patch is drawn, takes no focus, and is not drawn on a weekend Night, when his awake pose is;
- **slots, by what exists at the time.** In U3's worktree, on its test sidecars: a place with a sidecar returns its slots for one to four people, and a place without returns the code row or the defaults, key by key. At the trial merge: each of the sixteen in-place rooms and every composed picture already installed returns slots from its sidecar; a new place still on its stand-in and a yard still at revision 1 are listed as "waits for its layout sitting", not failed (the eleven new places are laid out at batches 9 to 11, 13, 16 and 19). At U10: all 35 that have their picture. No slot row is written into `VIEW_SLOTS` to make this pass (K2).

## 7. People doing things, and things to do

### 7.1 Captions: every cell says something

Every (person, place) pair the routine can produce gets its own caption in `AMBIENT` (today 21 of 43 show a filler). A caption never claims a prop or clothing the standing sprite contradicts ("in a yukata" is out; "fanning herself on the bench" is in), and at the baths and the pool it never puts anyone in the water (K16: the sprite is in uniform, on the deck or in the rest hall).
