The 21 fillers are written the same way by the package that owns the table (U4) and shown to the owner at checkpoint B as one list. Captions that can never show are fixed or removed: Megumi's riverside line and Nobara's rooftop line go (their routines never go there); Panda's "sparring with Toge" becomes "stretching, slowly, like a building settling"; Utahime stops being "buried in paperwork" in the cafeteria. A person I asked to the card table reads "here because I asked".

**Seeing them without hovering.** The owner's ruling "pictures without words" stands as the default. Added: a "Show names" row in Preferences (`persistent.show_names`) and a held key (N; not Tab, which is Ren'Py's skip toggle) that, while down, labels every person with their caption, every exit with where it goes and every icon with its name.

### 7.2 Poses: the policy and the list

- One person, one picture. No group pictures (they are fixed to who is there, and the schedule moves). People who share a table are painted at the same table, **each in a named seat of their own** (5.12): Panda at the left end of the Library table and Toge at the right end. The second of a pair is made after the first is installed, and the tool refuses boxes that overlap and can show together. At the shogi board in their room Panda and Toge are never painted together: by 4.3 one of them is asleep whenever the other is up at Night, so each is painted at the board alone, and each one's blanket shape (rows 29, 32) is kept clear of the other's seat. By day they are both at the board on weekend Mornings, and are painted there as rows 59 and 66: each at a named side, Toge made after Panda is installed, exactly as at the Library's table.
- A pose is made for a (person, place, hour picture) the routine really produces; the harness checks every row against the schedule, the asleep table, for a district row `WORLD_AWAY` and, for a row painted on a `lit` picture, `WORLD_LIT` and the late table (5.7a; the ledger row for `p2_view.rpy:714-740`, 10.3), and counts the rows from the tool's table, never from a literal (`p2_view.rpy:712`'s "exactly 14" becomes that rule).
- A pose is made only after its room **and** the hour picture it is seen on are installed (K8), and shows only with the picture it was painted on (K7).
- **A pose steps aside when its caption is not the one showing:** when an `AMBIENT_STORY` row has taken over (after the Sun Mall Nobara is "re-taping the grip of her hammer" while her pose holds a phone), the standing sprite shows, unless the pose's row lists that condition (AUDIT P11).
- A painted person stays painted while I look at them (6.2 row 8). A companion never takes a pose.
- **An asleep pose is not a person** (K16, 5.12): a shape under a blanket, turned to the wall, drawn as part of the room while `world_asleep` is true for its owner. It cannot be clicked; the bed's "Asleep" tag sits on it (7.3). Until it is painted the tag stands alone.
- Every pose is made in two versions and the owner chooses.

The three have no row in the tables above yet: their spots (in the Library, clear of rows 3, 12 and 13) and their sitting are written before they are bought (5.13). By person the 35 are Yuji 5, Megumi 4, Nobara 5, Maki 5, Panda 4, Toge 5, Shoko 2, Yaga 4, Utahime 1 (the three in Tokyo are counted with their people). **None of the 35 is at the Bathhouse, the Sauna or the Pool, so K16 removes none of them and the 67 the owner was told stands.**

**Still never painted, by rule:** anyone at the Bathhouse, the Sauna or the Pool (K16; the pose tool refuses such a row). That is six cells of the week, which keep their sprites, in uniform: Nobara, Maki and Utahime in the rest hall on weekend Evenings; Yuji, Nobara and Panda on the pool's deck on weekend Afternoons. Utahime and Ijichi asleep are behind their bedroom doors and are never painted. Gojo's wandering is drawn by lot and has no row. A companion and a person asked to the card table never take a pose (they are not where their routine puts them).

Of the twelve phase 2 planned, nine are kept and three are re-pointed at where those people now spend their time (Maki from the training hall to the dojo; Toge and Panda from the cafeteria and the Courtyard to the Library). If the test fails, poses stop at three, none of the other sixty-four is bought (the owner's yes of O28 was to poses that match), and captions plus "Show names" carry "people doing things"; the asleep shapes, which are not figures beside sprites, are then judged on their own.

### 7.3 Dead things come alive

**Every place has at least one thing in the picture besides its exits** (a rule in `u2_things`). Looks are free and work at Night. Each is a row in `room_spots.rpy` and a point in the place's `hot_` sidecar; points for a room redone in place are measured on today's picture at its in-place layout sitting (L, beside wave 1), and points for a composed picture at its own layout sitting. **The ids are frozen first** (`SPOT_PLANNED`, 3.2, in the scaffold), so a layout sitting that happens before wave 1 (batches 0, 1 and 2 do) places every look and moved action once, and lint accepts a point whose hotspot is planned but not yet registered.

**Exits sit on doors, indoors too** (AUDIT M4; L's in-place layout sittings, which write the `hot_` files and the door boxes of the rooms redone in place). Each in-place room's exit is put on its painted door, on today's picture; where the room shows no door, it goes to the bottom of the picture as the way back (5.2's rule). Known today: the infirmary's (at (84, 500), on a window), the ridge's, and the girls' corridor's (at the far end; the stairs are behind me). `check_layout.py` holds the rule: an indoor exit is inside a door box named in the room's keep list (`doors`, 5.4), or in the bottom band. **When it is a failure:** today's rooms break the rule on purpose until their sitting, so the rule is checked only for a room whose keep list holds a door box or whose `hot_` file holds an exit written at a layout sitting; until then the room is listed as "waits for its layout sitting", not failed. From wave 1's trial merge it is a hard failure for all sixteen in-place rooms. One negative test: a test sidecar with an indoor exit on a window must fail.

**Actions that move onto the object they belong to** (no new action id; the column tile disappears while the object is visible, as `hot_covers` already does, and comes back when a person stands in front of it): Study on the classroom's front desk and on my own desk; "Sit a while" on the rooftop railing; Train on the training hall's straw bundles, the dojo's weapon rack and the Training Yard's posts (a new place for it); Practice on the Courtyard's straw posts; Swim on the river's deep pool. The card table's icon moves from the floor cushion onto the low table, and stays, dimmed, once the day's limit is reached (AUDIT R13, L3). The dimming is drawn by `hot_layer`, which is final in the scaffold: U0c gives it a generic "spent" state (a registered hotspot may name a `dim` function; today it dims only for the night rule or a lock, `sb_hotspots.rpy:312`), and U5 supplies the function.

**The picture is what is clicked** (O34, D60). In a place that has its new picture no button sits on the picture. Each exit and each thing to do there is a **shape**: the building, door, flight of steps, path or object itself, traced on the picture at the place's layout sitting as one to three polygons and written to `hot_<place>.json` under `shapes`, beside the point it already has under `spots`.

- **What I see.** Nothing, until the pointer is on a shape. Then the shape lights and its name opens against the square at its point, on the side `tips` gives, which is where a button's name opens today. A click does what the button did. A locked door, an action the night rule closes and the card table once the day's limit is reached light at under half strength and say why, as their buttons are dimmed and say why today (a hotspot's `dim` function returns the reason as a string, `""` when it is not dim). **While names are shown** (the setting, or N held) every shape of the place is lit at reduced strength, each with its name drawn against the square at its point on its `tips` side, where its hover label opens; `check_layout.py` refuses a place where two of those name rectangles meet. A house's exit shows up to three faces of the people inside beside its name, under the pointer and while names are shown (`path_faces`, U2; drawn by `hover_label`, which knows where the name landed). **A thing that is used up for the day** (the vending machine after one can, the infirmary cot, the racks) is not a hotspot until tomorrow: its button disappears today, and in a shaped place it stops lighting.
- **The light** is drawn from the polygon at that moment: no picture file is made for it and nothing moves. It is a soft warm-white wash and an edge of two lines, a light one over a dark one, so that it shows on a white wall (the storehouse is a white kura) as it does on a night picture. It is drawn on a canvas a blur's width larger than the shape on every side and is added to the picture by an outer transform, never under the blur (in this engine a blur stops at its displayable's border, and an additive picture shows only when drawn straight onto an opaque one). Its colour and strengths (under the pointer, the edge, dimmed, while names are shown) are `HOT_SHAPE`'s defaults unless `game/layout/hot_look.json` gives others: that file is how the owner's "stronger" or "weaker" is answered once `sb_hotspots.rpy` is final.
- **What is traced.** A building with one way in is traced whole, as much of it as the picture shows. Where one building holds several ways in (the staff building's offices), each is traced as its own door, porch or wing. A path or a flight of steps is traced as the ground it covers, to where it leaves the picture. A way out that is behind me (5.2's rule) is a low band of floor along the bottom edge. A thing to do is traced as its object (the row of striking posts, the vending machine, the low table); a small object is traced with what it stands on, or with room round it, so that no shape is smaller than a button (5,184 px²).
- **The button leaves the picture and stays in the screen's structure.** Each hotspot keeps its 72 px square at its point, with the same id (`hot_<id>`), the same action, the same hover label under the same key, and today's alpha wrapper (1.0, or 0.45 when it is locked, shut by the night or spent), drawn with nothing in it. **In a shaped place the square takes no pointer** (its focus mask refuses every point): it is there for the keyboard, for `renpy.set_focus` and for the rules. The shape is a second focus area over the same action (`hotshape_<id>`), which the keyboard skips; it carries the same text under a tooltip key of its own, `hotshape_<id>`, because every tooltip key on the place view is unique (`p2_hud.rpy:621-624`), and `hot_tip_rect` and the side lookup answer for it exactly as for `hot_<id>`. **The shape is lit whenever the tooltip in force is its hotspot's,** under either key, so it lights from the keyboard too; the light is never the shape button's own hover state. So every rule about a hotspot's point or square is read as it is written today: `HOT_BOUNDS` and the lanes, the 110 and 120 px spacings, 4.6's count, 6.3's rows, the side its name opens on. `hot_spots_for` returns the rows it returns today, with the same eleven keys (`p2_contracts.rpy:439-442`), and `hot_layer` keeps its two parameters. **Hidden means what it means today:** while a person's rectangle covers the square at a hotspot's point, the hotspot is not shown and its shape takes no click; a way out or a thing to do is then in the column, and a thing to look at waits until they move, since a look has no row in the column today either (`sb_hotspots.rpy:135-140`). The sitting therefore puts each point on the part of its shape that people cover least: beside the standing rows, and above them only left of x 364 (4.6).
- **Layers.** A shape is drawn, lit and clicked in its hotspot's layer. A back shape is under the people (a click on a standing person's own pixels is the person's, and beside them the wall's), under a painted pose's whole box (a pose is a button over its box, `view_stage.rpy:776-784`) and under the room's foreground cut-out. So a thing that is its room's foreground object (the Common Room's low table, a desk) is registered `front=True`, and its shape is in front; a standalone's is in front too, as its button is today (the Courtyard's vending machine, which is never hidden). `check_layout.py` asks that a front shape lie inside the foreground outline of `tools/fg/<place>.json` or be traced tight to its object, because whatever it covers of a person is its click and not theirs. Where two shapes of one layer overlap, the smaller is on top (the striking posts in front of the storehouse); of two of the same area, the one whose id sorts first.
- **The rules** (`hot_validate` in the game's lint, and the same numbers in `check_layout.py`; the limits are one table, `HOT_SHAPE`, which the dump carries). A shape is one to three polygons of 3 to 60 points each, inside the picture; a polygon does not cross itself and a shape's polygons do not overlap; its area (the shoelace sum) is at least 5,184 px², a button's, and at most two fifths of the picture; its hotspot's point lies inside it and under no smaller shape, so that a click at the point is its own; no shape has more than half its area under smaller shapes; its id is a registered or planned hotspot or exit of that place. "Under smaller shapes" and "two fifths" are counted on a 4 px grid, by cell centres, by one function whose text is the same in `sb_hotspots.rpy` and in `check_layout.py`, and a test gives both the same three shapes. Two hotspots registered at one point and never offered together (the storehouse's `racks` and `armory`, `room_spots.rpy:35-36`) share one shape: both ids carry the same polygons, and the point and burial rules are not read between two hotspots whose points are equal. **A place is shaped all at once:** if its sidecar holds any shape, every exit and every hotspot the game itself registers or plans for the place has one, or is named under `keep_tile` with its reason; an id that begins `zz` is a harness's and is left out of the rule (as `p2_rooms.rpy:564` and `p2_paths.rpy:234` leave such ids out). **In a shaped place any visible hotspot that has no shape is drawn as its button, exactly as today, above the shapes:** a `keep_tile` row, and a harness's own test hotspot. The known `keep_tile` case is a bed tag until its blanket is painted (batch 27): an empty bed that says "Asleep" under the pointer tells nobody that someone is in it, so until then the tag keeps its button; at batch 27's sitting the six beds are traced and their rows removed. `check_layout.py` prints how many keep a button in each place, and why, for the sitting's note.
- **When a place is shaped.** Its `hot_` file holds `shapes`, the file is not stale (`for_rev`), and its picture is at revision 2 or later (5.5): `art_rev` of the file stem of `LOCATIONS[loc]["bg"]`, which is the place's key everywhere but My Room (`your_room` for `dorm`, K1). A stand-in, an old picture and a yard still at revision 1 draw their buttons exactly as today, and K12 is untouched: until a yard is repainted an exit to a new building is a button at a clear point on the old picture. The shapes of a room redone in place are traced at its in-place layout sitting (L) on the picture it shows that day and are read from the day its new picture is in; the installer's proof that nothing moved gains a 12 px band along each shape's outline (5.4). The Courtyard and the Library are the first two shaped places (batch 0), and the owner judges the light, and whether anything is hard to find, on those two before the other thirty-three are traced. It is judged in a played game: in explore mode only the ways out and the things to look at answer (`sb_hooks.rpy:91-92`).
- **Not done, and why.** A cut-out picture for each light (a file for every shape, for an edge the soft light does not need; it is the fallback, behind the same sidecar key, if the drawn light looks rough at batch 0). A thing staying clickable while someone stands on its point (its name would need a second place to open, and 4.6's count and 6.3's rows would have to be rewritten; the owner is asked at checkpoint B whether it is missed). A shimmer over everything on walking in (on the owner's word, as an eased fade and never a function transform, and never with Motion off). Either would be U10's, or a small package of its own, because `sb_hotspots.rpy` has no owner between wave 0 and the integration. Shapes on the maps.

Each is registered with `when=` a function that is true only while that person is asleep in the room (`world_asleep`), so it shows on the Nights its owner sleeps and at no other time; its label is "Asleep" and its one line says so and nothing more ("Megumi is asleep. I leave him be."). They use the look icon, so the icon count does not change. It is also the model for a later quest's hotspot: a registration with a `when` that reads a story flag (D49).

With the moved actions and the new places' own actions that is about 70 things to click in the pictures against 15 today. The lines are caption text, not story; they are proposed by U4 and read by the owner at checkpoint B. Not built (each is a feature, not a look; section 11): watching the television with someone, the console, opening the locked cabinet or the chest, the wardrobe as outfits.

### 7.4 The card-table invite

"Like invite toge or panda etc to come play."

- **Where.** A second tile beside the card table's, **"Ask someone to the table"** (`cards_invite`, its own icon: the existing invite icon already means "Ask them along"). **When it shows, exactly:** it is not Night, not explore mode, I hold the smallest stake, the day's limit is not reached, and fewer than four people are in the Common Room (the room stands four). **It does not use `cards_ok()`:** that function is False whenever nobody is in the room (`room_cards.rpy:212-229`), which is the very case the invite exists for; built on it, the tile would show only when someone was already there.
- **Who.** A small picker, `screen cards_invite_pick`, shows the faces of every student I have met who is on campus this hour (their routine puts them somewhere, at home included), is not already in the room, is not pinned somewhere by the story, and whom I have not already asked today. No Bond level. Each tile says where they are now ("in the Library").
- **What happens.** One line as they come in ("Toge looks at his phone, then at the door. He comes."; one per person) and they are **in the Common Room for the rest of this hour**: standing on the stage, on the map, in the journal, and at the table when I sit down, because `cards_opponents()` already takes whoever is in the room. I can ask again while the room has space; the table seats three friends with me (`cards_opponents` takes three), and **someone I asked sits first**: `cards_opponents` puts invited people ahead of the others (a plain function in `room_cards.rpy`, U5's; no protected statement). So on a weekday Evening, when the three first years already fill the table, I can still ask Toge or Panda over, and he plays in place of one of them. Next hour they are back in their routine.
- **How, with no new saved state.** The game already has a way to put a person somewhere for one hour: `pin(who, loc)`, which writes `schedule_overrides[(who, day, period)]` (`sb_world.rpy`; story scenes use it). The invite calls `pin(who, "dorm_common")` for the current hour and adds `"cards_inv:<who>"` to `actions_today`.
- **Cost.** Free: it takes no hour. The game at the table costs what it costs today. Nothing in `sb_do_cards_play` changes, and none of its protected statements. The picker is one new `call screen` pair, protected from the commit it lands in.
- **What it does not do:** invite by phone or from a dormitory door; invite teachers; carry over to the next hour. Each is a word away.

### 7.5 What teaches a new player

- **Six one-time hints** (`game/ui_hints.rpy`): a single line in the objective banner's lane the first time its situation happens, gone on the first click, never again (`hint_seen`; the function that dismisses a hint adds its id and calls `renpy.retain_after_load()`, as the armory's buttons do at `sb_items.rpy:357-410`, because a save loads to the start of the `call screen` it was made on and would otherwise bring the hint back). (1) the first place view: "Things in the picture can be clicked: people, objects, and the doors and paths that lead somewhere else. Rest the pointer on one and it lights up." (worded for a place with buttons and for a shaped place alike, D60); (2) after it: "Rest the pointer on anything to see its name. Hold N to see them all."; (3) the first time the map opens: "Pick an area, then a place. Walking is free."; (4) the first time a person stands in a place: "Click someone to talk or to do something together."; (5) the first time an hour is spent: "Each thing with a pip takes an hour. Wait lets an hour pass."; (6) the first Night: "At Night nothing that takes an hour can be started. I can still walk, talk, look and visit. Go to bed ends the day." The first five show only before day 3, so an old save is not lectured and no migration is needed; the sixth can show once to anyone. Never shown in a replay, in explore mode or while skipping.
- **"How to play"** in the pause and title menus: the orphaned `screen help`, rewritten as one page in the game's words (the place view, the map, the journal, the hours and the night, walking, the keys M, J, N, Esc and Space, and Tab to skip, as in any Ren'Py game).
- **A small card when the day turns** ("Day 9 · Tuesday", two seconds), shown from the new-day hook the way the objective banner is; it takes no required argument, so a save holding it loads.
- No scripted walkthrough: that would be a new story scene.

### 7.6 Friends in Tokyo at weekends

"We should find some of them off campus aswell in tokyo shopping." (O23.)

**How a trip works today** (`sb_tokyo.rpy`, frozen): from the gate, or from the map's Tokyo tab, Morning to Afternoon, Ijichi drives me to a district. `label sb_trip` plays his line in the car, shows the district's picture, says its two or three arrival lines, then **calls `label district_<key>` if such a label exists** (`:180-181`; none does today), then drives back: half a day gone, and I am at the gate. A district has no people, no stage and nothing to click.

**The smallest way a district can show people** is that one existing call. Nothing in `sb_tokyo.rpy` or the loop is edited.

- **Who is there** is data (D51): `WORLD_AWAY` in `sb_world.rpy` (3.2), read by `char_location`, which answers "off campus" for them as it does for anyone away today. Proposed, each one table entry: **Nobara and Maki in Harajuku, Yuji in Shibuya, on Sunday Afternoons.** Nobara in Harajuku comes first, as the owner's example; Maki carries the bags; Yuji is at the arcade. Megumi, Toge and Panda stay on campus (4.3). Shinjuku asks Grade 2 and Kawasaki waits for the story, so nobody is sent there.
- **The journal and the map say which district.** `person_now`'s `name` reads "In Harajuku" and `doing` their caption (4.2); the People card and the person's page print it. On the map's Tokyo tab the district's marker, and the district's own view beside "Ride with Ijichi", show up to three faces of **the friends who are there this hour** (`map_district_people(key)`: those `world_away` puts in the district now, so faces show in the Afternoon only), and the hover line names them ("Nobara and Maki are here this afternoon"). On a Sunday Morning and at Noon the line reads "Nobara and Maki will be here this afternoon", with no faces: at Noon they are still on campus by their routine (Nobara is in the cafeteria, and the journal says so), and nobody is shown in two places at once. A ride begun in the Morning is back before they arrive; a ride begun at Noon finds them, because it takes the afternoon (I arrive after lunch, as they do).
- **Riding in finds them.** `game/room_tokyo.rpy` (new; U5) defines `label district_harajuku` and `label district_shibuya`, each one line, `call tky_people("<key>")`. With nobody there it returns at once and the trip plays exactly as today; under the harness's autoplay (`smoke_autoplay`) it also returns at once, as `sb_trip`'s own menu is skipped there, so the thirty-day drivers never stop on its screen. With someone there: each friend is shown standing in the district's picture (their usual sprite, at the scene positions `pos_cl`, `pos_c`, `pos_cr`, lit by the picture's own light row, with a contact shadow) and their caption is said as one first-person line ("Nobara is outside a shop window with three bags, deciding on a fourth."; no caption says "already", since a Noon ride and an Afternoon ride say the same line). Then one small screen, `tky_meet(key)`: a tile for each friend, and "Head back". `tky_people` hides the friends' sprites before it returns, so nobody is left standing over `sb_trip`'s next line ("Ijichi is waiting where he said he'd be"). **`tky_here(key)`** is the friends whose `WORLD_AWAY` cell for that district is this hour or, at Noon, the next; it leaves out anyone not on the roster and anyone a story pin or a flag schedule holds for the Afternoon.
- **Nobody is waved off at the gate and then met in town.** The frozen trip lets go of whoever is walking with me before the district's label runs (`sb_tokyo.rpy:142-145`), and nothing new is saved, so `room_tokyo.rpy` cannot know a friend was beside me a line earlier. It is closed from the other end, in two places that are not frozen. `advance_period` (`sb_world.rpy:390-401`) lets a companion go, with one toast ("Nobara heads off to catch the train into town"), when the hour that begins is their `WORLD_AWAY` hour or the one before it (U0c1, with the table). And "Ask them along" is not offered with a friend in those two hours (one test in `activity_state`, laid by U0c2 beside the test that keeps the romance kinds out of the baths). So from Sunday Noon until they are back, a friend in the table is never walking with me, and `world_away` never has to answer for a companion.
- **To talk to and spend time with, as on campus.** A friend's tile offers **Talk** (the campus small talk, `label sb_smalltalk`, free, as often as on campus) and **"Spend the afternoon together"**: four or five first-person lines written for that person in that district, Bond +4 with them (`TKY_BOND`, the figure a night out already gives a companion), once a day each; they are added to `spent_today`, so it counts as today's time with them exactly as time spent on campus does. It costs nothing beyond the half day the trip already takes. "Head back" returns to `sb_trip`, which drives me home as it does today.
- **They are back by the Evening.** The table sends them only for the Afternoon; their Evening cells are on campus (the baths, the pond), and at Night all three are in their own rooms (4.3). A ride begun in the Afternoon brings me back at Night, as today.
- **What a save can hold:** a save made on `tky_meet` rests on its `call screen` in `room_tokyo.rpy`, with `sb_trip`'s unchanged return site under it. The three labels and the `call screen` pair are protected from the commit they land in (2.3). Nothing new is saved.
- **Decency (K16).** A caption says what a friend is doing in the street, dressed as their sprite. The romance kinds are not offered on `tky_meet`: it has two choices and they are the two above.
- **Painted there too (O28).** Once batches 45 and 46 are in, each of the three is painted into the district's picture (7.2 rows 60, 61 and 67), standing where their sprite stood. **How it is drawn:** by the `tky_meet` screen and by nothing else. The screen asks `view_pose(who, key)` for each friend of `tky_here(key)` and, when it answers, adds the patch over the district's picture at its box (an `add`, as the stage adds a pose); a pose is never shown by a `show` statement. On campus a pose is drawn by the stage screen and never reaches a save; a district has no stage, and a save can rest on `tky_meet`'s `call screen`, so a patch shown as an image there would put a pose file's path in that save, where K9 allows none and the stale gate of 5.5 could not hide it. A screen is rebuilt on load and asks `view_pose` again. **The order on screen:** the caption line is said before the screen opens, as today, so for that one line a friend stands as their sprite; `tky_people` hides the sprite of each friend whose `view_pose` answers just before it calls the screen, and the screen shows them painted while I choose. When Talk or "Spend the afternoon together" is chosen the screen has closed and that friend is shown as their sprite for the lines, as a painted person on campus is when talked to; back on the screen they are painted again. Nothing else changes: `tky_meet` and its two choices are the same, and with no pose file (or a stale one, 5.5) the sprite stays up as before. No new name or label, and no pose path in any save (U5; `u2_rooms` checks it with a test pose file: on `tky_meet` the patch shows, no sprite is drawn for that friend and the friend beside them still stands; a save made there holds no path beginning `images/place/pose_`, and loads with the pose file removed, showing the sprite).
- **On stand-ins.** Until batch 32 the districts are today's flat pictures; the friends stand on them all the same. Harajuku's and Shibuya's prompts ask for open pavement across the lower middle, where they stand (5.8).
- **Adding someone or somewhere later** is a `WORLD_AWAY` entry, a caption, the lines, and, for a district that has no label yet, one two-line label; `tky_validate` (lint) refuses an entry whose district has no label, no open state without a grade or story lock, or no lines.

**What this does not do** (section 11): no shop to buy from, no building to walk into, no district stage with exits or hotspots, nobody riding in the car with me, no shopping trip I can propose, no scene of the day out. The districts' own scenes are the story phase's, and when it writes `district_harajuku` properly it replaces the body of these labels and keeps their names.

---

## 8. Scenes and screens

### 8.1 Story files: twelve scene lines, then faces (U9, alone)

**Why it is safe.** A save resumes at a statement name. When a changed `.rpy` is compiled beside the old `.rpyc`, names are carried over by matching each statement's identity (Ren'Py 8.5.3, `script.py:556-572`). A say statement's identity is its speaker and its words, not its attributes; a menu's has no text at all, and it keeps its name while a neighbour keeps its own; a call's is its label, not its arguments (`ast.py:1616-1617`); a `scene` whose image changes gets a new name, and no save rests on a `scene`. **What the owner's saves do and do not prove:** today their menu-parked saves rest in `ch1b_the_offer.rpy` and `ch2_tour.rpy`, and their return stacks hold sites in `script.rpy`, `ch2_tour.rpy`, `sb_loop.rpy` and `explore.rpy`. U9 opens one of those files, `ch2_tour.rpy`, and changes one `scene` line in it (`:381`, below; O47), on which no save rests (5.7a); so "the owner's saves load the same" says next to nothing about U9's edits. What covers U9 is the saves U0a makes inside the files it edits (10.2) and the names check below.

**Commit 1: the scene lines that change picture.** Each new image name is defined in `bg_placeholders.rpy` by the scaffold (U0c) with today's picture as its stand-in, so the swap can merge before the pictures exist and shows what it shows today. **My Room's five lines** (the last four rows of scene lines below; decided by the owner on 11 Oct, O47; 5.7a) name no image: each asks `world_lit_bg("dorm")`, a function U0n lays in wave 0, which answers My Room's `lit` picture once it is installed and fresh and `graded_bg("dorm", "Night")` until then, so these five also show what they show today the day they land.

| file | today | becomes |
|---|---|---|
| `ch4_mission.rpy:22` | `scene bg city` | `scene bg mall_front` |
| `ch4_mission.rpy:79`, `:337` | `scene bg ruin` | `scene bg mall_hall` |
| `ch4_mission.rpy:291` | `scene bg veil` | `scene bg mall_atrium` |
| `missions_arc1.rpy:43` | `scene bg city with fade_hard` | `scene bg warehouse with fade_hard` |
| `missions_arc1.rpy:113` | `scene bg ruin with fade_curse` | `scene bg annexe with fade_curse` |
| `story_arc1.rpy:335` | `scene bg courtyard with fade_slow` (Maki's roster, "the board outside the classroom building") | `scene bg school_yard with fade_slow` (AUDIT G9) |
| `ch2_tour.rpy:381` | `scene bg your_room night with fade_slow` (my first night) | `scene expression world_lit_bg("dorm") with fade_slow` (O47, 5.7a: the room with its lamp on, where its chosen `night` has the lights off) |
| `events_arc1.rpy:381` | `scene bg your_room night` (Panda's and Toge's visit; its `with fade_slow` is a line of its own, `:382`, and is not touched) | `scene expression world_lit_bg("dorm")` |
| `story_kurose.rpy:119` | `scene bg your_room night with fade_slow` (the envelope under the door) | `scene expression world_lit_bg("dorm") with fade_slow` |
| `story_oda.rpy:23`, `:95` | `scene bg your_room night with fade_slow` (two letters) | `scene expression world_lit_bg("dorm") with fade_slow` |
| `missions_arc1.rpy:67` | `call combat("warehouse", _p, True, "bg city")` | the fourth argument becomes `"bg warehouse"`: the fight draws the picture it is handed (`sb_combat.rpy:296`), so without this the warehouse scene would cut to a city street for the fight |
| `missions_arc1.rpy:132` | `call combat("nade_curse", None, True, "bg ruin")` | the fourth argument becomes `"bg annexe"` |
| `sb_night.rpy`, the club branch of `label night_out` | no picture of its own: it plays on over `scene expression night_bg(key)`, the district's night | one new line, `scene expression night_club_bg(key)`, after `$ add_yen(-NIGHT_OUT["cover"])` and before the first say line; away from the three protected menus and both `call market_open` pairs, in a label marked for the story rewrite. Not `scene bg club`: a fixed stand-in is one picture, and the club in Shinjuku would show a Shibuya street until the art exists. `night_club_bg` (U0c) returns `"bg club"` once that file exists and the district's own night until then, so the line shows exactly today's picture the day it lands |

None of the twelve `scene` lines is the statement after a `call`; none sits directly before a menu whose first inner line also changes; only the image name changes on each of the first seven, and on My Room's five the image name gives way to an expression and each line keeps its own `with`. (Read at today's lines.) **The two call lines are the one exception to "never touch a call"** (K10; PHASE2's D43). They are name-safe: the Call and the Pass node after it (which is what a save made inside the fight has on its return stack) keep their names because an argument is not part of a call's identity, and a save made inside the fight keeps the picture name it was handed, which stays defined. The names check must show both names surviving, in a scratch tree, before the commit is made; if it does not, the two arguments are left alone and those two fights keep the general pictures, and the checkpoint D note says so. The four memory tiles that show the first seven's scenes are data rows and follow them (U7, 3.1); the tiles of My Room's scenes keep their name, `bg your_room night`, which is the room at night with its lights off (5.7a).

**Commit 2: faces.** Scope: the 26 `ev_*` character scenes in `events_arc1.rpy` and the `act_*` scenes in `events_activities.rpy`, `events_intimate.rpy`, `events_romance.rpy` and `events_stand.rpy`: about 1,150 lines of script in which no face changes today. Chapters 4 and 5, the Nade missions, Kurose, Oda and `story_arc1.rpy` wait for the story phase, which rewrites them.

- A face is added **only as a say attribute** on a line that person already speaks: `yuji serious "…"`, or `yuji @ surprised "…"` for one line. The words between the quotes stay byte-identical.
- **No `show` line is added, no line is reworded, no "you" becomes "I",** no menu, call, flag or reward line is touched. A face that would need a narration line to change on is left for the story phase.
- Each scene's first spoken line carries an explicit face, so the greeting face no longer runs through a grave scene (Yuji grinning through his third scene). Then about one change every four or five spoken lines, on the line where the mood turns.
- Only the eleven campus people and Kaito's portrait, only the six faces.

**Proof.** `tools/smoke/u2_names.rpy` compares, in a tree whose `.rpyc` are copies of the owner's, **every node** of the edited files (not only the kinds a save can rest on: the Pass after each call is what a return stack holds, and a save can rest on a Python statement such as `$ renpy.pause`) against `tools/smoke/baseline_story_names.json`: every baseline name still exists with an identical `diff_info()`, except exactly the twelve listed Scene nodes, each of which is replaced by one node with a new name (a `scene` whose image or expression changes is a new statement); apart from those twelve replacements exactly one name is new (the club's line); nothing else differs. Seven of the twelve name an image the scaffold defines, which lint checks; My Room's five name none (`world_lit_bg` is a function, not an image name), and no made save and no harness rests on any of them, so the names check alone covers those five (5.7a). **Who writes the baseline:** U0a, before any story file is touched, for the frozen story files and for U9's files except `sb_night.rpy`. `sb_night.rpy` is the one file of U9's that an earlier package legitimately edits: U0c2 changes its init blocks in wave 0 (`NIGHT_REST_BLOCKS`, two `NIGHT_CARRY_LINES` rows, `night_club_bg`), and a Python node's identity is its source (`renpy/ast.py:1182-1183`), so each edited block takes a new name and a baseline taken before U0c2 would report a harmless change as a fault. So U0a dumps `sb_night.rpy`'s names to a file of their own (`baseline_night_names_u0a.json`), U0c2 proves against that dump that every node outside the init blocks kept its name (10.2), and **U0c2 writes `sb_night.rpy`'s entry of `baseline_story_names.json` as its last step,** after its own edits, in a tree whose `.rpyc` are copies of the owner's. U7 and U9 compare `sb_night.rpy` against U0c2's entry. Then `tools/load_all_saves.sh` must show every row of the save set SAME **and `dead_return_sites: none`** (or exactly what the wave-0 report shows: the tool calls a save SAME when it lands on its statement even if a return site below it is gone, so SAME alone would not catch a lost return site). The save set includes U0a's saves made inside these files: on a spoken line in an `ev_*` scene with the sprite up; on a line inside an `act_*` scene; on the menu at `ch4_mission.rpy:117` (its return stack holds the Pass after `events_arc1.rpy:1051`); inside the warehouse fight; on the menu at `story_arc1.rpy:353`; on the night out's arrival menu and its after-club menu. Then lint (it rejects an attribute with no picture), `wp9_prologue`, `wp10_chapters`, `wp5_memories`, `phase1_all`, `phase2_all`, `p2_saves`, `p2_night`.

**Known cost:** the mouth moves on the calm face only, so more faces means the mouth shows less often. Accepted for now; a talking mouth per used face is on the "later" list.

**Why it is last, alone, and first on the cut list.** It is the only step that edits story files, where a save made mid-scene rests; it needs no art, and nothing depends on it. If the owner would rather faces wait for the story rewrite, commit 2 is skipped; if commit 1 is skipped too, the mall, the warehouse and the annexe keep playing on the painted general pictures, and My Room's five scenes show the room with its lights off from the day its chosen night is installed (5.7a).

### 8.2 The stand-in people and the curses (U7)

- **The five stand-in lines** at `jjk_defs.rpy:96-100` become one guarded block, at `init 1 python` (after `jjk_sprites.rpy`'s init-0 `image` statements), that defines `mother`, `father`, `oda`, `kurose`, `nade` as today's tinted figures **for each key where `not renpy.has_image(key, exact=True)`** (an `image` statement cannot be conditional, so the block calls `renpy.image`). The guard is on the image, not on the file: when `mother_neutral.png` has landed but `tools/gen_sprite_defs.py` has not been run yet, nothing else defines `mother`, and a guard on the file would leave `show mother` (`ch1b_the_offer.rpy:121`) with no image. When the generator is run, its `image <key> = lit_sprite(...)` takes over. No scene line changes: `show mother at pos_cl` shows the new sprite, with a contact shadow and the room's light, in old saves too. The four old flat files stay until all four people have landed, and `oda` keeps using one.
- No Character gains an `image=` tag in this overhaul and no attribute is given to Mom, Dad, Kurose or Nade (an attribute on a speaker with no pictures is a lint error and a crash). That, and their second faces, arrive with the story phase.
- **The curses** drop in by file name (5.11). The Sun Mall's curse is a third file: `image curse big` becomes a switch on `has_flag("settled_in")` (the alley's before the campus opens, the mall's after), as `image mc` already switches outfits; with no third file both show the first. The fight screen itself is the combat phase's.
- *Harness (`u2_offcampus.rpy`):* all three states: with no files the five names resolve to today's figures; with a test picture overlaid but the generator not run, still today's figures and no error; with the generator's lines overlaid as well, `lit_sprite` sprites with a shadow; `--dup-globals` is clean both ways; a save made with `mother` on screen loads and shows whichever is installed.

### 8.3 The main menu, the menus, the engine screens (U6; restyled, never rewritten)

Screen names, parameters, ids and what buttons do stay exactly as they are.

| screen | change |
|---|---|
| `main_menu` | **Designed as a screen** (O25, D56; 5.10 has the mock-ups and the picture). Today the name is set at 132 px in a box 1,100 px wide on the left, straight over the gate picture, with the two-kanji watermark on the right and the menu at the foot of the same column (`screens.rpy:486-538`): it runs across the picture. The design sets three things together: **its own picture; the game's name set properly** (two lines, "Jujutsu Kaisen" small above "Cursed Bonds" large, the fan-work line and the seal with it, on a quiet part of the picture made for it); **the menu laid out cleanly** (Continue, Start, Load, Preferences, How to play, About, Quit, as one group with one alignment). **Continue** shows above Start when a save exists and loads the newest slot. This is the one engine screen whose layout is designed afresh; its name, its lack of parameters, what each button does, `gui.show_name` (which still hides the name) and the developer-only "Explore the campus" stay. U6 builds the three layouts of 5.10 behind one token, `gui.sys_menu_layout`, so that checkpoint C's mock-ups are screenshots of the real screen. The token takes `"today"` (the default: today's layout with Continue added, so nothing changes on the main menu before the owner chooses), `"gate"`, `"gateway"` or `"veil"`. **The mock-ups are made by a harness U6 writes, `u2_menu_mock`:** for each of the three layouts it sets the token and the background to the named installed picture (the gate's evening, the Courtyard's night, the ridge's night), writes `shots/menu_mock_<n>.png`, and refuses in one line if a picture is not installed in the new look. It is run by the session that prepares checkpoint C, once batches 7 and 18 are in (10.5), not by U6: those pictures do not exist in wave 1. After the owner chooses, that session sets the token to the chosen layout and L copies its mock-up picture to `game/gui/title.png` (5.10); U10 deletes the layouts the owner did not choose, `"today"` among them |

| `game_menu` | the menu ground (5.10) |
| `file_slots` | each new slot reads "Day 9 · Tue · Evening · Cafeteria" from `FileJson`, written by one `config.save_json_callbacks` entry. **The place is written only when it is true:** when `settled_in` is set and a sandbox screen (`sb_place`, `sb_map`, `sb_char`) is showing. Before `settled_in` the line reads "Prologue" (`here` is still its default there, so a new player's first autosaves, in the alley, the diner and at home, would otherwise all read "Day 1 · Mon · Morning · My Room"); in the middle of a scene, a mission, a Tokyo trip or a night out it reads the day and hour with no place. An old save, which has no such keys, shows its clock time as today. A small delete button on each slot. Still six slots a page. |

| `choice` | the list moves to the right-hand third of the screen (a style change only), so the face at centre stays visible. Captions that would wrap past three lines at the new width are listed by the harness. |

(Not "shown while `config.developer` is on": the owner plays from the Ren'Py launcher, where developer mode is always on, `00library.rpy:353-360`, so they would still see every one of them and conclude it had not been built; and the harnesses run that way too.) U6, in `ui_journal.rpy`: the "n more chapters in a later build" rows (`:661-662`, `:1192`) and `JR_FX_NOTE` (`:95`, "Takes effect in combat from the combat update"). U2, in `ui_map.rpy`: the fifteen Tokyo pins. U8, in its screen blocks (wave 2): `sb_items.rpy:930`, `sb_market.rpy:354`, `sb_board.rpy:1091`, with the two harness lines that assert those notes are present (`p2_night.rpy:1732`, `wp4_board.rpy:434`; ledger, 10.3). The lines stay in the source as comments, for whoever builds combat. **These stay on screen, on purpose,** because they mark where the build really stops and saying nothing there would be worse: "Written in a later build" on a memory tile or a journal row for a scene that is not written (`sb_memories.rpy:87`, `sb_missions.rpy:525`, `ui_journal.rpy:1131, 2907`), the objective line "The story continues in a later build" (`sb_missions.rpy:868`), the two closing chapter cards (`sb_missions.rpy:1161`, `story_arc1.rpy:466`) and one item's source line (`sb_items.rpy:96`); three of those files are frozen (2.1). `tools/later_ok.txt` (U0a) lists exactly the lines that stay, and `check_content.py --later-notes` greps player-visible text for "later build" and "combat update" against it: a report during waves 1 and 2, a failure at integration. A new harness, `u2_screens.rpy`, takes the first screenshots that exist of the main menu (each of its three layouts), save, load, preferences, help and choice screens.

### 8.4 The phase-1 screens (U8: board, armory, market, card tables, grade card, toasts)

One rule: **the red seal stays as the header's mark; every word button becomes an icon tile with a hover label (Take, Buy, Deal, Hit, Stand); one Close, top right, the same everywhere** (it appears three ways today), on the computer, fishing and the invite picker too. The card games get a one-line legend under the table (what Hit, Stand, Bet and Fold do). The Black Market's screen draws its own painted ground when the file exists. The three "later build" and "combat update" lines in these screens (`sb_items.rpy:930`, `sb_market.rpy:354`, `sb_board.rpy:1091`) are hidden (8.3). Screen names, tags, return tuples and every `call screen` / `$ _x = _return` pair are protected and untouched; `chapter_card` and `grade_card` can be inside a save and keep their parameters exactly. This is the first package to wait if a wave runs long: the screens work today.

### 8.5 What does not change in scenes (and waits for the story phase)

Where people stand and how big in a written scene; the hour picture a scene shows (with one stated exception: My Room's five scenes, which U9 points at the lit room, 8.1, O47); a scene offered away from where it is set; people who speak without being drawn; a motion kit; one-off painted moments; the paper and phone views; the "you" narration; reward lines over the Last Light lamp. Each needs scene lines reworded or re-staged, and the story phase is about to rewrite those same lines. The rooms those scenes name are redone, so every one of them improves without a line changing.

## 9. Contracts (what is frozen first)

`docs/UI2_CONTRACTS.md`, started by U0a and final after U0c but for the one addition U0n makes at the end of wave 0 (5.7a: rows are gained, nothing changes shape), with `tools/smoke/u2_contracts.rpy` asserting all of it (each symbol with an example call and its return shape; each function's owning file by `__code__.co_filename`). `u2_contracts.rpy` is not edited after wave 0, and it is in every package's standard set, so **every symbol it asserts exists, at least as a stub, when wave 0 ends** (9.3 lists the stubs).

### 9.1 In the order of what it would cost to get wrong

1. **Home:** `dorm`, `bg your_room`, `default here = "dorm"`, `sb_do_path_dorm` (K1).
2. **The eleven place keys** (all eleven: the owner said all five rooms) **and their image and file names:** `bg <key>`, `bg <key> night|evening`, and `bg <key> lit` for the five friends' rooms (U0n, 5.7a), `images/bg/<key>[_<variant>].png`. A key that reaches a save can never be removed; a room dropped later is a shut door, and a room added later is an appended key on a spare door (4.1, D50).
3. **Action ids and labels:** `path_<key>` ×11, `bath_soak`, `bath_sauna`, `pool_swim`, `cards_invite`; the loop calls `"sb_do_" + id`. The activity id `swim_pool` and its label `act_swim_pool`. **The hotspot ids of `SPOT_PLANNED`** (id, place, kind), the six bed tags among them, so layouts can be made before wave 1. A hotspot's widget id and tooltip key `hot_<id>` (unchanged, in a shaped place too) and its shape's, `hotshape_<id>` for both (D60). The labels `district_harajuku`, `district_shibuya` and `tky_people`, and the screen `tky_meet` with its three return tuples.
4. **The saved sprite** (K9): the text dump of `lit_sprite`'s tree for one person, recorded.
5. **The light row:** four values, or six with `(shadow lean, shadow stretch)` last; `light_for_place(loc, per)` and `light_for_bg(name)` keep their signatures.
6. **Sidecar shapes** (3.2), `pose_<place>.json`, `faces.json`, who may carry `for_rev` (composed pictures and new places only), the `spare` key of the three houses, the `shapes` and `keep_tile` keys of a `hot_` file with the limits table `HOT_SHAPE`, and the keys of `hot_look.json` (D60), the manifest's fields (`rev1_commit`, `from_commit` and `record` among them), the keep list's keys, and **the shape of `tools/art/game_layout.json`** (5.4), the one file the art tools read the game through.
7. **The 26 icon names** and which cell of sheet 4 each is in. **The new image names of 5.8:** ten always defined with a stand-in (`bg mall_front`, `mall_hall`, `mall_atrium`, `warehouse`, `annexe`, `site_tunnel`, `site_ward`, `site_platform`, `site_pool`, `site_karaoke`); three defined only when their file is loadable (`bg club`, `bg shibuya night`, `bg shinjuku night`); the market's file name, `images/bg/market.png`, which is not an image name; and the stand-in copies under `images/bg/standin/`.
8. **`tools/bgs/look.txt`, `keep.txt`, `keep_light.txt`** (frozen after batch 3), **`stage.txt`** (after batch 1, the first pictures it is used for), **`map.txt`** (after batch 21a), **`keep_outdoor.txt`** (after batch 8), the pose prompt's two frozen sentences (a person's clothes as their sprite's; the asleep shape: K16) and **the shape of `tools/bgs/campus.json`**. Its rows are never frozen as a whole (O20): a fixed row changes only when the owner chooses that picture again, a proposed row whenever a chosen picture says otherwise.
9. **`hint_seen`** (changed only through a function that calls `renpy.retain_after_load()`), the six hint ids, `persistent.show_names`, and the key held to show names (N; `u2_contracts` asserts it is in no `config.keymap` list, and if N turns out to be taken the scaffold picks the next free letter and the help page names it).
10. **The raised budgets** (K11) and **the ledger** (10.3).
11. **The door table, the asleep table, the away table and the three hooks** (D48 to D51): the shapes of `WORLD_DOOR_BOND`, `WORLD_ASLEEP` and `WORLD_AWAY`; the flag names `door_<place>` and `welcome_<room>`; the three answers of `world_home_state` (`"awake"`, `"asleep"`, `"out"`); and that a hotspot's `when` is how a quest shows one. **K17's check** (`--baths-optional`).
12. **The tools' command lines:** `make_redo_workflow.py`, `make_scene_workflow.py`, `install_redo.py`, `check_layout.py`, `check_campus.py`, `art_status.py`, `art_batch.py`, `stage_preview.py`, `sample_light.py`, `campus_plan.py`.

### 9.2 Symbols

| symbol | file | returns |
|---|---|---|
| `world_inside(loc)` | `sb_world.rpy` | the house's key or None |
| `world_pinned(loc)` | | bool |
| `world_home(who)` | | a place key or None |
| `world_door_bond(loc)`, `world_door_owners(loc)` | | an int, or None for a place with no Bond door; a list of person keys |
| `world_asleep(who, per=None)` | | bool (False at every period but Night, for a companion, and for anyone a story pin or flag schedule has placed) |
| `world_home_state(who, per=None)`, `world_room_state(loc)` | | `"awake"`, `"asleep"` or `"out"`; `{owner: one of those}` (`{}` for a place that is nobody's home) |
| `world_away(who, per=None)` | | a district key or None |
| `tky_here(key)` | `room_tokyo.rpy` (a stub from U0c: an empty list) | list of person keys: who a ride begun now finds in that district |
| `tky_caption(who, key)` | `room_tokyo.rpy` (a stub from U0c: `""`) | a caption string; `""` when that person has none in that district |
| `map_district_people(key)` | `ui_map.rpy` (a stub from U0c: an empty list) | list of person keys, three at most: who is in the district this hour (not who a ride would find: at Noon it is empty and `tky_here` is not) |
| `view_asleep(loc)` | `view_stage.rpy` (a stub from U0c: an empty list) | list of (who, patch) for the room's sleepers whose asleep patch is installed and fresh |
| `person_now(who)` | (unchanged) | the same dict, **exactly the six keys it has today** |
| `place_access(loc)` | (unchanged signature) | `(bool, reason, short tag)`; a place whose flag `door_<place>` is set is open; a house is always open from one of its own rooms; a room inside a house asks its house first (not when I stand in that house or in another of its rooms, and not when its own flag is set); a friend's room asks `world_door_bond` of one of `world_door_owners` |
| `night_club_bg(key)` | `sb_night.rpy` | an image name (str) |
| `bg_variant(loc, per)` | (unchanged signature) | the attribute or None; None for a stale hour picture |
| `art_rev(stem)`, `art_fresh(stem)` | `art_manifest.rpy` | int (1 when unknown); bool |
| `rule_layout(name)` | `sb_rules.rpy` (unchanged signature) | dict; `{}` for a file whose `for_rev` does not match (a file with no `for_rev` is always read) |
| `path_keep_clear(loc)` | `room_paths.rpy` | list of (x, y) |
| `hot_shapes_for(loc)` | `sb_hotspots.rpy` | `{hotspot id: [polygon, ...]}`, a polygon being a list of `[x, y]`; `{}` for a place that is not shaped (D60) |
| `hot_tip_rect(key)` | | `(x, y, w, h)`, the square at a shaped hotspot's point, for either of its tooltip keys, `hot_<id>` and `hotshape_<id>`; None for any other key |
| `path_hub`, `path_exits`, `path_neighbours`, `view_slots`, `view_slot_rects`, `view_person_rects`, `view_pose`, `view_motion`, `stage_person`, `char_focus`, `register_hotspot`, `register_place_action` | | unchanged |
| `light_row(loc, variant)` | `lighting.rpy` | a 4- or 6-tuple or None (the measured row when fresh) |
| `view_floor(loc)` | `view_stage.rpy` | list of polygons or None |
| `bath_ok()`, `pool_ok()` | room files | bool |
| `cards_invitable()` | `room_cards.rpy` (a stub from U0c: an empty list) | list of person keys |
| `view_face_layers(path)` | `view_stage.rpy` (unchanged signature and layers) | as today; which faces carry the blink comes from `faces.json` first, then `VIEW_BLINK_ALSO` |
| `hint_show(id)` | `ui_hints.rpy` | None (on `tools/none_ok.txt`: U0c adds the row in the same commit as the stub, since `p2_contracts` runs every example in that file and would fail in U0a and U0b with a row for a function that does not exist yet) |
| screens | | `cards_invite_pick()` returns `("ask", who)` or `("close",)`; `view_others(loc, who)`; `hint_layer()`; `hud_day_card(n=None, weekday=None)` (`cards_invite_pick` and `hud_day_card` are stubs from U0c, appended to `room_cards.rpy` and `ui_hud.rpy`); `tky_meet(key)` returns `("talk", who)`, `("with", who)` or `("back",)` (a stub from U0c, in `room_tokyo.rpy`); `main_menu`, `choice`, `say`, `file_slots`, `preferences`, `quick_menu`, `journal`, `sb_map`, `sb_place`, `sb_char` keep name and parameters |

### 9.3 Hooks the scaffold places in files other packages will own

So that no wave package has to touch a file it does not own. Each is behaviour-neutral until its package fills it.

| in | the hook | filled by |
|---|---|---|
| `sb_screens.rpy` | `use hint_layer` in `sb_place`; `use view_others(here, who)` in `sb_char`; and, laid earlier by U0b with the table they read, the three asleep lines: `stage_people` leaves out anyone `world_asleep`, `ambient_of` gives them the caption "asleep" as its first statement, and `rail_missions` leaves out a mission whose giver is asleep | U4 (hints), U3 (the others); the asleep lines are final in U0b |
| `ui_map.rpy` | `use hint_layer` in `sb_map` (laid by U0c2, with the stub it names); `map_district_people(key)` returning `[]`; and, laid earlier by U0b, the sleeper test in `map_has_news`, `map_waiting` and the mission rows of `screen map_area` (3.1) | U4; U2; the sleeper test is final in U0b |
| `ui_icons.rpy` | `icon_words_shown` also reads `persistent.show_names` and the held N key; `hover_label` opens against `hot_tip_rect(key)` when that answers, and draws the faces `path_faces(to)` returns beside the name of a shaped exit (D60) | final in U0c |
| `sb_hotspots.rpy` | while names are shown, an exit wears where it leads and a hotspot its name; an exit tile draws the faces `path_faces(to)` returns (a stub in `room_paths.rpy`: none); `hot_validate` refuses a sidecar point for a hotspot or exit that is neither registered nor in `SPOT_PLANNED`; `SPOT_PLANNED` itself; a registered hotspot may name a `dim` function and `hot_layer` draws it at the dimmed strength while that returns a reason (a string, which the dimmed button or shape says; `""` when it is not dim); **D60, all of it:** `hot_shapes_for`, and in `hot_layer`, for a shaped hotspot, the square drawn empty, the shape's focus area and its light, the dimmed and the names-shown strengths, a house's faces beside its exit's name, and the shape rules in `hot_validate` | final in U0c; U2 fills `path_faces`; U4 registers the planned ids; U5 supplies the card table's `dim`; L writes every shape |
| `view_stage.rpy` | the sidecar keys of 6.1 read with today's behaviour as default; `view_pose` reads `pose_<place>.json` first; the gates of 5.5 in `view_fg_path` and `view_pose`; `view_face_layers` reads `faces.json` before `VIEW_BLINK_ALSO` (the same layers, chosen by data, so K9 is untouched); `view_asleep(loc)` returning `[]`, and the stage's one `add` loop over it | U3 builds on them; `install_face.py` writes `faces.json` (L, batches 35 and 36) |
| `lighting.rpy` | four- or six-value rows; `light_row`; the `light.json` lookup in `light_for_bg` | U3 |
| `sb_world.rpy` | the gate in `bg_variant`; `WORLD_VARIANTS` and its registration; the door, asleep and away tables with their functions (U0b, U0c1: final in wave 0, since doors and the Night work from checkpoint A) | U1 (the toasts, the lines, `person_now`'s names) |
| `sb_night.rpy` | `pool_swim` and `swim_pool` in `NIGHT_REST_BLOCKS`; `night_club_bg`; the two girls' rooms in `NIGHT_CARRY_LINES` | final in U0c (U9 adds the one line that calls it); U0c2 writes this file's names baseline after its edits (8.1) |
| `sb_activities.rpy` | the `swim_pool` row, appended, hidden by `when="pool_ok"`; the one test in `activity_state` that keeps the romance kinds out of the Bathhouse, the Sauna and the Pool, and the one that keeps "Ask them along" from a friend in their Tokyo hour or the hour before it (7.6) | U4 (the choice order, `TRAIN_TOGETHER`, the `loc` lists); U5 opens `pool_ok` |
| `room_bath.rpy`, `room_pool.rpy` | the three actions registered with `when="bath_ok"` / `"pool_ok"`, both returning False, and their labels (`sb_do_bath_soak`, `sb_do_bath_sauna`, `sb_do_pool_swim`, `act_swim_pool`) as `return`: the place-action ids exist for `night_validate`, and the activity's label exists for the `swim_pool` row U0c appends | U5 |
| `room_cards.rpy`, `ui_hud.rpy` | appended at the end of each: `cards_invitable()` returning `[]`, an empty `screen cards_invite_pick`, an empty `screen hud_day_card(n=None, weekday=None)` | U5; U4 |
| `room_tokyo.rpy` | `tky_here(key)` returning `[]`; `tky_caption(who, key)` returning `""`; `label district_harajuku`, `label district_shibuya` and `label tky_people(key)` as `return`; an empty `screen tky_meet(key)`. With the stubs a Sunday ride plays exactly as today; the three friends are already off campus on Sunday afternoons by U0c1's table, and until U1 names the district in wave 1 the journal says "Off campus" for them (the checkpoint A note says so) | U5 |
| `bg_placeholders.rpy`, `sb_board.rpy` | the ten always-defined image names of 5.8 with their stand-ins, the three defined only when their file exists, the stand-in copies, the board's job-to-picture column | final in U0c |
| `gui.rpy` | the two picture lookups (5.10); the `gui.sys_` tokens | U6 |
| `jjk_defs.rpy` | the guarded stand-in block | U7 |
| the new-day hook registry | nothing is placed: `world_welcome_check` and the day card register themselves from their own files | U1, U4 |

No hook is needed in a frozen file: every new action is a registered place action reached through the loop's existing `call expression "sb_do_" + _r[1]`.

---

## 10. Work packages

```
Before wave 0: nothing is waited for. Every question asked with the plan is answered (O15 to O32).
               The last (a painted pose for every cell) was answered "Yes" on 7 Oct: batches 41 to 46.
               One run may be pressed at any time, before or beside wave 0: batch 0b, Yuji's five other faces (D57).
Wave 0  (in turn, in the owner's folder on ui2)
        U0a the safety net  →  U0b my room moves  →  U0c1 the places  →  U0c2 the scaffold
        beside U0b and U0c (no game file touched): U0d1 the record and the prompts · U0d2 the installer and its proof · U0k the cut-out's edge
        after U0c2 has merged: U0d3 the layout tools and batch 0's two layouts
        after U0k, U0c2 and U0d2: batch 0c (Yuji's blink and mouth), then L installs Yuji's whole set in one step (D57)
        last, after U0d3: U0n rooms lit at Night (D61, 5.7a)
                                                                              → checkpoint A (no drawing to approve)
        art: batch 0 (free), then batches 1 to 5, beside wave 1 (the main locations first, working outwards)
        L:   the in-place layout sittings (the sixteen rooms redone in place), beside wave 1, in the owner's folder
Wave 1  (six parallel worktrees, ~/Desktop/JJK_worktrees/u2_<n>, branched from ui2 after checkpoint A)
        U1 world and people · U2 the map and the doors · U3 stage and blending
        U4 things to do · U5 baths, pool, invite · U6 menus and system screens
                                                                              → trial merge → checkpoint B
        art: batches 6 to 22, and 47 once 9 to 11 and 15 are chosen, beside wave 2 and after it (the rooms off each location, the new places, the maps last)
Wave 2  (two parallel, then one alone)
        U7 the people as drawn · U8 the older screens
        then U9 story files (alone)                                           → checkpoint C (when batch 22 is in)
        art: batches 23 to 46
Wave 3  U10 integration                                                       → checkpoint D
```

Nineteen packages (the scaffold is built as two, U0c1 and U0c2, the art tools as three, U0d1 to U0d3, the cut-out's edge, U0k, added on 7 Oct by D58, and the rooms lit at Night, U0n, added on 11 Oct by D61, each with its own acceptance: PHASE2's rule that a wave-0 package of thirty files is split, `PHASE2_DESIGN.md:1107-1121`), and one standing art-install role (L). (The second draft had a step, U0e, for when the owner moved something on a drawing at checkpoint A; there is no drawing now, and a chosen picture changes sidecars and the record, never code: O20.) Elsewhere in this plan "U0c" and "U0d" mean the pair and the three together. The split is by risk, not only by size: U0b is small and alone because it moves the bedroom; U9 is alone because it edits story files. **What may run beside what in wave 0:** U0d1 and U0d2 touch no game file and need nothing U0b or U0c builds, so they run beside them; while U0b or U0c is mid-edit the committed dump may be out of date, so U0d1 and U0d2 run their tools on a local dump with `--layout-dump` (5.4). U0d3 does not: its layouts place every hotspot of `SPOT_PLANNED`, and `check_layout.py` reads the numbers of the tree U0c changes, so it starts after U0c2 has merged.

**Standard acceptance for every package** (PHASE2 2.4, with this overhaul's additions): (a) lint clean, no `VALIDATE:` line; (b) `check_content.py --all` shows no new finding; (c) its own harness passes through `tools/smoke.sh`; (d) `u2_contracts`, `p2_contracts`, `core_flow`, `phase1_all` and `phase2_all` pass, and every harness in its row of 10.1, apart from ledger lines handed off by name (K13); (e) **the save set loads the same:** the owner's eleven saves and the nineteen made saves, by `tools/load_all_saves.sh --from <snapshot>`, each row SAME **and its `dead_return_sites` line reading `none`** (or exactly what the wave-0 report shows for that row); (f) `tools/check_campus.py` and `tools/check_layout.py` pass once U0d has landed, by the rules of 5.2 and 7.3: the record and every test sidecar in full; a yard or map still at revision 1 is listed as waiting for its batch, not failed; an in-place room that has not had its layout sitting is listed as waiting for it, not failed on the indoor-exit rule (a hard failure for all sixteen from wave 1's trial merge); **in a worktree these tools are run on a local dump:** `tools/smoke.sh u2_dump --out shots/art/game_layout.json`, then each tool with `--layout-dump shots/art/game_layout.json`, and `tools/art/game_layout.json` is never committed from a worktree (5.4); (g) a `zz_worst` screenshot of each full screen it owns; (h) a five-line "what changed, what to look at" note for the owner. Worktrees get copies of the owner's `.rpyc` and never copy back.

### 10.2 The packages

#### U0a · The safety net (no game file changes)

1. Back up `game/*.rpyc`, `game/saves/` and `~/Library/RenPy/JJKgamemd-1788928056/` to `~/Desktop/JJK_backup_ui2_<date>/`. **Copy the owner's eleven saves into a snapshot folder there:** the owner keeps playing, and their autosaves are the only saves that rest on story menus.
2. Run `load_all_saves.sh` on the untouched tree; commit the report.
3. **Make nineteen more saves from the untouched tree,** in a scratch copy with the owner's `.rpyc`, by harness (the drivers `p2_saves.rpy` already has). Each is named for what it holds; no acceptance line anywhere depends on what a slot of the owner's contains, because autosaves rotate. *Where my room moves and the Night changes:* standing in my room; in the garden one step after walking out of my room; on the map with the Dormitories area open; **on the map opened from my room;** **on `sb_place` in the garden on a weekday Night with Yuji and Panda on stage; on `call screen sb_char("panda")` there; on `night_carry_home`'s line with the day turned in the garden; on `sb_place` in the girls' corridor in the Evening (walked in during the Afternoon); on `sb_char("yuji")` at the riverside on a weekend Afternoon** (those are the cells 4.3 moves). *Where the stage and the people change:* on the first line of dialogue in the Common Room with the foreground and a sprite up; in the prologue with `mother` on screen; on a chapter card. *Inside the story files U9 edits* (8.1): **on a spoken line in an `ev_*` scene of `events_arc1.rpy` with the sprite up; on a line inside an `act_*` scene reached through an activity; on the menu at `ch4_mission.rpy:117`; inside the warehouse fight (`call combat`, `missions_arc1.rpy:67`); on the menu at `story_arc1.rpy:353`; on the night out's arrival menu; on its after-club menu.** They join the snapshot and are committed under `tools/smoke/assets/ui2_saves/` (they hold no personal play), so every package's acceptance (e) can be rerun from the repository; the snapshot folder's path and a checksum for each of the owner's eleven go into `docs/load_report_ui2_wave0.txt`. They are the saves this overhaul is most likely to hurt, and the owner does not happen to have them: on the day this was checked all eleven of the owner's were on day 1, none at Night, none in a file U9 opens.
4. The baselines, from today's tree: place actions for every place and period; who is where for every place, period, weekday and weekend; the exit graph; every statement name of the story files (`baseline_story_names.json`: the frozen story files and U9's files except `sb_night.rpy`, whose names go to `baseline_night_names_u0a.json` for U0c2 to check itself against; U0c2 writes that file's entry of the baseline after its own edits, 8.1); the text dump of `lit_sprite`'s tree; screenshots of the place view, the person view, the map's two levels and the main menu.
5. `tools/prefixes.json`: rows for `art_manifest.rpy` (`art_`), `room_bath.rpy` (`bath_`), `room_pool.rpy` (`pool_`), `room_tokyo.rpy` (`tky_`, with an exception for its `district_` labels, which the frozen `sb_trip` calls by name), `ui_hints.rpy` (`hint_`), and new prefixes for `jjk_defs.rpy` (`standin_`), `bg_placeholders.rpy` (`standin_`: today its row allows only `bg area_` and `bg library`, which no Python name can carry, and the scaffold adds about twenty-five stand-in definitions there), `screens.rpy` (`sys_`), `gui.rpy` (`gui.sys_`); exceptions for the label `act_swim_pool` in `room_pool.rpy` (the loop calls an activity by `"act_" + id`), for `SPOT_PLANNED` in `sb_hotspots.rpy` (whose prefixes are `hot_` and `mg_`), and for `persistent.show_names` in the file that holds its default. Then frozen again. (`tools/none_ok.txt` is not touched here: its one new row is U0c2's, with the stub it names.)
6. **K3, once:** `p2_paths.rpy:631` (the copy of `PATH_KEEP_CLEAR`: equals the live hotspot points), `:642` (the Courtyard's exit point: clear of the machine and inside the frame), `:316, :483, :1089` (the garden's "exactly 7": at most 7 and equal to its table), `:393` and `p2_map.rpy:350` (24: `len(LOC_ORDER)`), `:717` (30 yard exit labels: counted from the table), `:866-888` (the Mountain box: from the file); `p2_map.rpy:336-341` (press `row_<loc>` for every place: for every pinned place), `:351-352` (the budgets of K11), `:875` (`2 * (len(LOCATIONS) - 5)` pin hovers: counted from the pinned places); `p2_view.rpy:510, 587, 769, 775` (slots compared with whatever `view_slots` reads), `:707-712` (14 pose rows: every row passes the schedule check, read from `TIME_VARIANT` and not through the gated `bg_variant`, which is None for a stale hour picture, exactly the Common Room's state between batch 0 and batch 3), `:726-740` (the foreground file lists: "every polygon file has its cut for every fresh hour picture of its room"), and `:521-551`, `:603-615` and `:736-740` (the pose and foreground checks made with the harness's own test pictures laid over the Common Room) run on **a fixture of their own**: the `p2_view` overlay (`tools/smoke/assets/p2_view/game/`) gains `layout/pictures.json` (`{"pictures": {}}`, so in the scratch game every picture is revision 1 and the test poses and cuts are fresh whatever the real manifest says) and `layout/pose_dorm_common.json` holding the two test boxes (`view_pose` reads that file first, 3.2, so the real one batch 5 installs cannot stand in front of them; the overlay's `view_dorm_common.json` keeps its boxes, which `:524` reads); `p2_contracts.rpy:1052` (88: the count the sheets hold); `p2_rooms.rpy:83` (the fifteen are present; more are allowed), `:513` (`len(SPOT_LINES) == 3`: counted from the table), `:515-528` ("every sidecar point equals the code's default, and there are 15": becomes "every sidecar id is registered or planned for its place and its point passes `hot_point_findings`", no count; batch 0 installs `hot_courtyard.json` and `hot_library.json` with moved points, and the art role may not edit a harness), `:779-790` (the vending machine's point: the applied one from `hot_spots_for`, not the code row in `HOT_SPOTS`).
7. `check_content.py --layout-files` (allowed keys and shapes of every `game/layout/*.json`).
8. `docs/UI2_PINS.md`, the ledger (10.3): each line found by grep, then confirmed by running its harness in a scratch tree with a stub of the change.
9. `tools/review_pack.sh <package>`.
10. **The pointer lock** in `tools/smoke.sh` (K13): a harness on the pointer list waits for a machine-wide lock, and refuses with one line while a window of this game is open. With six worktrees in wave 1 and `p2_contracts` in every package's standard set, collisions are otherwise certain and each looks like a real hover failure.
11. `tools/later_ok.txt` and `check_content.py --later-notes` (8.3).
12. `tools/smoke/u2_dump.rpy` (not pointer-dependent; `--out <file>` writes the dump somewhere other than the committed path, which is what a worktree uses, 5.4) and the first `tools/art/game_layout.json` (5.4, D47), its hash taken over the `.rpy` files and `game/layout/*.json`; its shape goes into the contracts. Keys the tree does not have yet (`SPOT_PLANNED`) are written empty and filled when U0c regenerates it.
13. `check_content.py --decency` (K16), a report now and a failure for U4 and U5.
14. `check_content.py --baths-optional` (K17), with K17's exact list of allowed places and its one negative test: clean on the untouched tree by construction, and a failure for any later package that reads a bath anywhere else.

*Acceptance:* the tree is byte-identical under `game/`; every harness in `tools/README.md` gives the verdict it gave before; each rewritten check fails when its sidecar is deliberately broken; the thirty saves load the same. **K3 as a whole, the moved-points half:** with a test overlay that moves every exit, hotspot, slot and pin of the Courtyard and of one area map at least 50 px to other legal points, `p2_paths`, `p2_rooms`, `p2_view`, `p2_hud`, `p2_map`, `p2_contracts` and `wp1_world` still pass. (The stale half is in U0c2's acceptance: the manifest reader and the gates that make a picture stale do not exist until then, so a test manifest would prove nothing here.) **The ledger's scratch-tree confirmation** (step 8) stubs the whole Night column of 4.3, the starred weekend cells, the two weekend Evening cells and the Sunday afternoons in Tokyo, not only my room's move, and runs every harness in `tools/README.md` against it, so a line nobody listed turns up here and not in the package that happens to meet it.

- the thirty saves load the same; for the "one step after walking out of my room" save, rolling back lands in my room; **the made save "on the map opened from my room" and the made save "standing in my room" play on to the next morning** (`p2_oldsave`, run, not read);
- after loading the garden-at-Night save and the person-view save made there: no exception, the person view opens and closes, the garden shows nobody, and Yuji and Panda are in their rooms: Yuji awake, and Panda asleep (a weekday), in `who_is_here("senpai_room")`, absent from its stage, offering nothing; **with a mission whose giver is Panda made available (a character mission of his, or a test one), `rail_missions("senpai_room")` is empty on that weekday Night and the map shows no job mark and no gold badge for him, and on a weekend Night, when he is awake, the mission is offered;** Maki, Utahime and Ijichi, who have no home row until U0c1, are on no asleep list yet and are never asleep; lint is clean on the new `_validate_world` rules, and a test name on an asleep list with no home row fails it;
