---

## Method: where the judges disagreed, and what was decided

The three judges each named a different design as the best (the owner's words: the living campus; the code and the saves: the smallest safe steps; the art and its proof: pictures first). They agree on more than that suggests: each asked for the other two's strongest part as a graft. This plan is those grafts, taken together. Where they pulled apart, the code was read again:

1. **The dormitory's shape.** A seventh map area (steps), a house table with my room pinless and a Home chip (campus), or a house table with my room keeping its pin (pictures). Read: `room_paths.rpy:178, 214-238, 296-333, 349-364`; `ui_map.rpy:811-812`; `p2_map.rpy:273, 336-352`; `p2_saves.rpy:467-476`. Decided: the house table with the pin kept. It needs only validator lines (the code judge confirmed the steps design overstated its cost), keeps six markers (the owner's judge), and avoids both the crash and the level-1 check the campus version ran into.
2. **Where exits live.** In code with layout sittings that edit `.rpy` (steps) or in sidecars (pictures, campus). Read: the sidecar reader exists (`room_paths.rpy:226-231`); `path_validate`'s spacing rules read code rows only (`:381-390`). Decided: sidecars, with the validator's spacing moved onto applied points and `check_layout.py` before install, as the code judge's graft 3 asks.
3. **The shadow's shape.** Read `lighting.rpy:108-143` and the five set points: the six-value light row (steps) is the only one right by construction.
4. **`person_now`.** Read `p2_contracts.rpy:431-436`: exactly six keys. The shape stays (steps).
5. **The area view's word budget.** Counted from `sb_world.rpy`'s names with `_lib.rpy:392-403`'s rule: Dormitories 17, Staff 17. Raised on purpose to 18; all three designs had it wrong.
6. **The Common Room at Night.** Read `wp1_world.rpy:329-334`: it must stay empty by routine. The campus design's Nobara cell was moved to her room.
7. **How far "all the backgrounds" goes, against how many story lines are touched.** The owner's judge wanted every stand-in replaced with the scene swaps; the code judge wanted the fewest edits where saves rest. Read: the seven `scene` lines (none follows a call or fronts a menu); `sb_night.rpy:520-595`; `sb_market.rpy`'s screen. Decided: all the pictures; the swaps in one package, alone, with the names check; the market's ground drawn by its screen (no statement); one new line for the club; no `show mother sad`; the alley fitted to the lamp so `ch1_alley.rpy` is never opened.
8. **Faces.** On by default (the owner's judge), but alone, last and first on the cut list (the code judge).
9. **The baths and the pool.** On the Training Yard (pictures) the column check fails by arithmetic (seven exits and two fixed tiles are nine); on the Mountain Path and the Training Yard separately (campus, steps) no yard passes seven. The sauna as its own room and "nobody is drawn in the bath" are the owner's judge's grafts.
10. **The teachers' door.** The steps design's open sitting room, as the owner's judge proposed, inside the campus design's one house, which costs the staff steps one exit instead of two.
11. **The proof for a redo.** Pictures' guard windows and keep lists, with the campus design's floor measure and the steps design's escalation; the sharpness threshold set from the third test (the art judge re-measured: 3.0 and 7.5 times), since all three designs' "three times" would have refused the approved look.
12. **The window views.** The art judge found the two third-test rooms show a generic town roof. I looked at the pictures: the Common Room's left window shows a house that is not on the campus, so it and the Cafeteria are run once more in batch 1; the chosen Library's grey roofs are kept and the plan puts a roof there (first the Cafeteria's, which the second verification found is behind the camera; now an archive wing behind the Library, item 16).
13. **Batches.** The art judge's count of renders, not pictures, and no sitting over twelve; the free batch 0 first; the Common Room's evenings and Nobara's pose in the very next sitting.
14. **Small corrections taken from the verdicts:** the konbini does stage one memory (`sb_memories.rpy:156`), so its row is re-pointed; `pool_swim` joins `NIGHT_REST_BLOCKS` in `sb_night.rpy` itself; `p2_saves.rpy:452-457` is in the ledger; no "intended losses" list is written for "Walk with them"; Ijichi lives on campus; there is no car at the gate because the gate has no road; rule names are K, never U.

15. **The adversarial review (three lenses, 45 findings, several of them the same fault seen from two sides).** Each was checked against the code and the pictures; all but the details noted here were right and are fixed. *The map:* the Ridge sat east of the Riverside on the plan and west of it on the Mountain map; it is now straight up the stairs from the Mountain Path (today's exit is at x 862 and today's pin at x 960), and 4.8's pins are printed from the plan by tool. The Training Yard's staff path is on the kura's right. The School Courtyard's camera faces north (it had its back to the schoolhouse it put on its right). The Common Room's window line was read off the installed picture (the pond is behind the camera). *Feasibility:* "Swim together" at the pool is its own activity (`act_swim` shows the river, `events_activities.rpy:102-103`); a house is always open from its own rooms (`path_walk` refuses a locked destination); the held key is N (Tab is `toggle_skip`); sidecars of in-place rooms carry no `for_rev`; the pose tooling, every new image name, the board's column and every contract stub move into wave 0; hotspot ids are frozen as `SPOT_PLANNED`; `check_campus.py` has a rule for pictures not yet made; the stand-in guard is on the image, after the generated sprites; the stubs register the new actions for `night_validate`; a yard keeps its area's icon until the sheet is cut; the club's line is `scene expression night_club_bg(key)`; the pointer lock; two more ledger lines; the six made saves in the repository. *Art:* sharpness is measured against revision 1; `KEEP` is split so a relight is not told to keep the time of day, and the three untried wordings each have a named test and a fallback; nothing lends any more, so "lenders last" and its deadlock with the cut order are gone and the whole prologue is one sitting; pairs at a table have seats and an overlap refusal; the fallback for batch 3 is a table; a canary guards against the editor changing; the curses are painted on green at 9:16; "water marks" left the look. *The owner's page:* three rulings are asked before building, the pose test comes before the checkpoint that judges it, and section 0 is corrected where it claimed more than the plan does. **Taken differently from the finding:** the three proposals for the Ridge's new position disagreed, and two of them kept a pin that would then have been reversed against the Bathhouse; (950, 110) with the pin at (960, 250) satisfies every relation. For the lenders, the stand-in copies were taken over the "warning the owner can accept", because they also remove the ordering. Not every "later build" line is hidden: the ones that tell the player where the story stops are kept and listed. No teacher poses were added; section 0 was corrected instead.

16. **Second verification** (five lenses: the first review's fixes, the saves, the build, the art, the owner's page; 59 findings, one of them a blocker, many the same fault seen twice). Each was checked against the code, the pictures and the owner's document; none was disputed, and where a finding offered two fixes the smaller or safer was taken. The owner's answers of 6 Oct were also taken in (all five rooms; the budget; the cloud plan is active), so section 0 now asks two new questions before building and one at checkpoint A. *The owner's page:* nobody is ever drawn or described in the bath, the sauna or the pool, no pose is made there and the romance options are not offered there (K16; the first draft's wording left a painted student at the pool one sentence away, and sending Maki and Nobara to the baths after dark would have put "Stay the night" there); friends' rooms were shut at every daytime hour, so each friend is home one weekend hour by day unless the owner says Night only; a girl's own door after dark asks Bond 30 with her; the card invite's tile no longer rests on `cards_ok()` (False in an empty room) and works in the Evening; where the new buildings hang is asked before it is built; a list of what will look unfinished between steps; the four people are a sitting each (twenty-eight sittings, the same money) and the cloud plan is named as outside the $36; notes are hidden for everyone, since the launcher always runs in developer mode; the title's subject is asked. *The pictures:* the chosen Courtyard shows two matching houses by the gateway that the bible did not have (now gate lodges, shown on the drawing); today's gate shows the old main hall behind the torii, so the gate is composed again (one more render); the Library's far windows face north, so the roof outside them is an archive wing and not the Cafeteria, which is behind the camera; the prompt check reads only per-picture lines (as written it failed the look itself); the rooftop moved to batch 8, so no outdoor wording is bought before its test; batch 1a reruns the Common Room and the Cafeteria from their revision-1 pictures, and that Common Room render is the canary's baseline (the canary had none); a look line is split so a vending machine is not asked for in the Training Yard; maps get their own framing text, not the eye-height one; the veil is in the sky, where the icon is; the yards' fallback has a last rung; scene-named hour pictures have their own fallback; the Mountain marker's box is said plainly; the manifest keeps two commits. *The build:* the `swim_pool` row is laid hidden in wave 0 with its icon row (wave 0 could not pass its own lint); the scaffold and the art tools are split into two and three packages, and the layout tools wait for the scaffold; the sixteen in-place rooms are laid out by one hand (L) beside wave 1, not by two packages apart; one committed, hashed dump feeds every art tool; three more harness pins become rules, with a whole-K3 test, so batch 0 can be installed without editing a harness; the prefixes and the `none_ok` row have owners; the blink's face table moves to a sidecar; six ledger rows. *The saves:* none of the owner's eleven saves is where the first draft said and none rests in a file U9 edits, so the net is defined by content: nineteen made saves, seven of them inside the story files, a names check over every node, and "SAME" only with no dead return site; the fights in the warehouse and the annexe take their new pictures by one call argument each (name-safe, the one stated exception to "never touch a call"), and four memory tiles follow; the slot line says "Prologue" before the campus opens; the journal's card says who is at home; a dismissed hint stays dismissed after a load; foreground files are never deleted; My Room's new evening replaces the static cell. **Counts after it:** 187 renders and the four people, still about $36; sixteen rooms in place, eight places composed again; thirty saves.

    **Confirming read.** One more reader went through the corrected plan and found ten things still wrong, four major and six minor, all about how the builders prove their work; none changes what the owner gets, what it costs or what they are asked. Each was checked against the code and none was disputed. (1) `p2_view`'s test poses and cuts over the Common Room would go stale at batch 0 and be hidden at batch 2: the harness gets a manifest and a pose file of its own, and the stale half of the whole-K3 test moves to U0c2, where the gates exist. (2) `sb_night.rpy`'s names baseline is written by U0c2 after its own edits, not by U0a before them. (3) A worktree runs the art tools on a local dump it never commits; the committed one is regenerated only in the owner's folder, and its hash covers the layout files too. (4) The slots check waits for a place's layout sitting, and section 0 says people in the eleven new places stand on general spots until batches 10 to 12. (5) Before checkpoint A prompts are drafted as text only; batch 1's files are written at batch 0's sitting. (6) U0c2 owns the one hook line in `ui_map.rpy`. (7) `stage.txt` is frozen after batch 3, its first use, not batch 1. (8) The weekend cafeteria drops to four in U0b, so `wp1_world.rpy:335` changes there as well as in U0c1. (9) The canary and its baseline are both measured against the revision-1 picture. (10) From a room with no pin, the house's pin is marked as where I am.

17. **The owner's rulings on the plan page** (6 and 7 Oct; SCOPE 3, every row from "Where the new buildings connect" down; owner rows O16 to O27). O16 and O17 were already in. This revision takes in the rest, and it has not had a second reader. **Items 12 to 16 above are a record of the plan as it stood at each review:** where they speak of a drawing at checkpoint A, a Bond of 30, "Night only", a cut order, batches by their old numbers, twenty-eight sittings or about $36, this item supersedes them. The code each ruling touches was read again (`sb_world.rpy`, `sb_night.rpy`, `sb_screens.rpy`, `sb_hotspots.rpy`, `room_spots.rpy`, `sb_tokyo.rpy`, `sb_loop.rpy`, `ui_journal.rpy`, `ui_map.rpy`, `screens.rpy`, and the harness lines that pin what changes) and today's girls' corridor picture was looked at; nothing was run.

    *Night (O18) and its correction (O19).* Everyone is in their own room at Night on all seven days; who is asleep is one table and one function; the filter sits in `stage_people`, because every screen takes its people from there, so `who_is_here`, the map and the journal still count a sleeper and nothing can be offered with one. The plan page first read "have them all accessible" as doors with no Bond level; the owner corrected it, so doors ask Bond 15 (one table entry) and then stay open at every hour, and what existed only so that a door followed its owner (the "out" lines and tag, the hours listed in the checkpoint notes, the special after-dark level on a girl's own door) is removed. For later quests there are three hooks and nothing else; the second needed no new contract, because a hotspot registration already takes a `when` function (`sb_hotspots.rpy:50, 96-101`). **Decided here:** Yuji's weekend pond and Maki's weekend baths move to their weekend Evenings (both fit); an asleep friend gets a painted shape under a blanket (six poses), because the awake pose for that bed already existed; the teachers asleep are never shown, since their bedrooms are doors; no new icon (the bed tag uses the look icon, so 114 stands).

    *Pictures first, the map last (O20).* The campus bible becomes a record with fixed facts and proposals; the plan drawing is no longer approved by anyone and survives only as a reviewer's sketch and, at the end, the maps' layout reference. K4 and K8 are rewritten; checkpoint A has no drawing and U0e is gone; "the editor follows a plan drawing" is no longer the first test, and "a yard composed from words and its chosen neighbour agrees with it" (batch 1) is. The pool is judged when its picture is chosen and again on the map. The fallback ladder keeps its rungs, but its last rung now reads "the record takes its facts from the picture", which is what the owner's ruling makes true everywhere.

    *Quality first (O21).* The cut order and every cost cut line are removed. Three compromises are taken back: a painted evening and night for every place that can be visited then (64 hour pictures where there were 28; only the sauna, a windowless box lit by its stove, keeps one picture); two versions of every picture, the rooms redone in place and every relight included; the teachers painted at home. One more was found and taken (the Tokyo map in the look), and three were found and left, each with its price (5.13). **Decided here:** two versions is read as literally as it was said, relights included, which is what makes it forty sittings and not thirty-three; painting an evening that used to be a tint takes Yaga's day pose off his weekday Evenings, so he gets an evening pose; a painted pose for every other cell (35 of them) is not added but asked, as instructed. Every batch was recounted: 364 renders, about $63, allow $76, forty sittings.

    *Room to grow (O22).* Spare doors in the three houses, their points measured and kept in a `spare` key, and a stated procedure and limits for adding a room. The girls' corridor is redone in place, so its spare doors are the far doors today's picture already has.

    *Friends in Tokyo (O23).* `sb_tokyo.rpy` is frozen, and did not need opening: `sb_trip` already calls `district_<key>` when that label exists. **Decided here:** Sunday afternoons only, so the Saturday pool and river keep their crowds; Nobara and Maki in Harajuku, Yuji in Shibuya (the two districts open without a grade); a ride begun at Noon or in the Afternoon finds them, a Morning ride does not; "spend time" is one choice worth the Bond a night out already gives a companion, inside the half day the trip already costs.

    *The baths are optional (O24).* K17, with a grep and a two-run play test.

    *The main menu (O25).* A screen designed from three mock-ups, which are real screenshots over campus pictures that exist by checkpoint C; its own picture is painted after the choice. "Restyled, never rewritten" still holds for its name, parameters and buttons; its layout is the one that is designed afresh.

    **Counts after it:** 35 places; 64 hour pictures; 32 poses; 8 maps; 364 renders and the four people, about $63, allow $76; forty sittings, eighteen of them two files with a pause; owner rows to O27; decisions to D56; rules to K17; thirty saves, unchanged.

Not taken, from any design: the Home chip, a seventh marker, a saved `light_shadow`, a new key on `person_now`, the cold-milk action, a test of the cheaper editor, a sidecar for the lamp's point, the second test's emptied Common Room, and the main-hall Courtyard.

18. **The check of item 17's revision (two lenses, eighteen findings: one blocker, six major, eleven minor).** Each was read against the code and the plan; all eighteen held, and none changes a count: the batch table was added up again and gives the totals of section 0 (40 + 178 + 140 renders, six curse renders, forty sittings, eighteen with a pause).

    *Code.* (1) **The blocker: a mission could be started with someone asleep.** Missions are offered from a place, by a frozen function that finds the giver in `who_is_here` (`sb_missions.rpy:689`), and a character mission passes the night rule; so "The Armory" would have been offered in Maki's room with Maki asleep. `rail_missions` and three readers in `ui_map.rpy` now make the same test, 4.3 lists every reader of `who_is_here` in place of the sentence "Nothing else in the game asks who is awake", and U0b, U1 and U10 test it. (2) U0b's asleep function read `WORLD_HOMES`, which was U0c1's: the table is now U0b's, each package adding its own people's home rows and asleep names together. (3) `tky_caption` joins the contracts with a stub, and the journal says "Off campus" for the three until U1 names the district in wave 1 (chosen over moving `person_now`'s Tokyo clause into wave 0: wave 0 stays tables and clauses, wave 1 words). (4) K17's two checks are rebuilt so that a correct build passes: the play test compares a bath against a Wait in the same hour, and the grep names its allowed places and has a negative test. (5) On a Sunday at Noon a friend was in the cafeteria and on the Harajuku marker at once: faces show in the Afternoon only. **Decided here:** a friend with a Tokyo hour is let go as a companion when Noon begins and is not offered "Ask them along" until they are back (one line in `advance_period`, one test in `activity_state`), because the frozen trip clears the companion before the district's label runs and nothing new is saved; the finding asked for the first half, and the second closes the Noon ride. (6) The fourteen-day schedule regression in `wp1_world` is in the ledger. (7) "The house first" is also skipped from another room of the same house. (8) The asleep caption comes before the story caption; lint rules for the new tables; a failing test for `spare`. (9) A stray sentence about Utahime's pose moved to the awake poses.

    *Art and the owner's page.* (1) K8 as written refused batches 3, 9, 13 and 19: a room now waits for its yard, and inside a house the order is the batch table's. (2) The ladder's last rung for the Training Yard installed a picture against a fixed fact, which K4 forbade: a `waived` row, written only there and only on the owner's word. (3) The main menu mock-ups had no maker: U6 writes `u2_menu_mock`, the session preparing checkpoint C runs it, the token's default is today's layout, and the chosen layout shows over its mock-up picture until batch 34. (4) Section 0 now says what the Bond doors cost in the first days. (5) The pool's evening waits for the first outdoor relight. (6) Two compromises are named with their price: the maps tinted by hour, and one Night picture for each friend's room. (7) Today's gate picture is copied to `gui/title.png`, or the main menu would have changed at batch 2. (8) The icon sheet is two versions, as the batch table has it. (9) Section 0 says where the rest of the rise comes from, and that fewer sittings is the owner's to ask for.

19. **The owner's "Yes" to the one question (7 Oct; O28).** The 35 unposed cells are painted: 7.2 rows 33 to 67, six sittings appended as batches 41 to 46 (appended, because inserting after batch 27 would renumber 28 to 40, named some thirty times; grouped by person and run in order, so no sitting needs a pause), none at the baths or the pool, so the 67 the owner was told stands; two small additions follow from it (`install_pose.py` refuses a box over an exit point, or over a hotspot point other than the one its row is painted at; the `tky_meet` screen draws a district pose in place of the sprite). **Counts after it:** 67 poses; about 230 installed pictures; 434 renders and the four people, about $74, allow $89; forty-six sittings, eighteen with a pause and eleven two files without; owner rows to O28; the batch table was added up again (40 + 178 + 140 + 70, six curse renders) and section 0 agrees with it. Items 17 and 18 give the counts as they stood before it.

20. **The check of item 19 (7 Oct), nine findings; eight are in the plan and one is outside it.** (1) The new refusal would have refused the plan's own poses, the pose test first: many sit at the object that carries the room's look or moved action. A row now names that object in `at` and is accepted over it (5.12, 3.2, U0d2, risk 28). (2) A Tokyo pose had no stated mechanism and could have put a pose file in a save: it is drawn by the `tky_meet` screen only, the sprite stands for the caption line, and `u2_rooms` proves no pose path is saved (7.6, 5.12, D54, U5). (3) The overlap rule and the harness's row check read `WORLD_AWAY` for the district rows. (4, 5) Section 0 named the people of each sitting wrongly and gave five people five tables; both corrected. (6) Batches 42 to 46 name the owner's yes and batch 42 names batches 3 and 5. (7) Row 47 sits clear of the schoolhouse door. (8) Section 11's Divine Dog is the figure of its own; row 40's pose is remade when it has a sprite. (9) The owner's page (`docs/plan_pages/ui2-plan.html`) still shows the question open and the old totals; it is republished from section 0 by the session that answers the comment. **No count moved:** 67 poses, 434 renders, about $74, allow $89, forty-six sittings; the batch table was added up again after the last edit.

21. **The characters, and the cut-out (7 Oct; O29 to O32, D57, D58).** The owner asked for the four characters that felt off to be looked at again before the build. Three look tests later (eighteen pictures, `docs/UI_OVERHAUL_2.md` 3e) the owner's word is: Maki, Shoko and Nobara stay as they are, and Yuji is redrawn in his school uniform with his red hood and red trainers (F). Because a new drawing moves his eyes and mouth and changes his clothes on every face, his whole set is installed in one step (L), from two small runs appended as batches 0b and 0c (seven renders, about $1.12, outside the counts above, which do not move). Two small additions make that step data only: U0c2 reads a person's eye and mouth boxes from `faces.json` before `VIEW_FACE`, and U0d2's installer cuts a close-up from a staged sprite, writes the boxes and installs a set whole. The cut-out tests of the same day (3d) are D58 and a package of their own, U0k: the edge step that measured about twenty-five times better than today's keying on exact pictures, used for new sprites only, with the anime-trained model as a checker. **No count moved:** 35 places, 67 poses, 434 renders, about $74, allow $89, forty-six sittings; eighteen packages where there were seventeen. Maki's lace loop is repaired in the game (517815d).

22. **One cloth for the school uniform (7 Oct; O33, D59).** Seeing Yuji's redraw beside the others, the owner said the clothing was not consistent. Measured on the six who wear the school uniform, it was not. A match that moves only the cloth's pixels onto one colour ramp was shown before and after, the owner said to apply it to all six, and it is applied to the five who are in the game in that uniform: thirty sprites and thirty side portraits, no pixel outside the cloth changed, and all eleven saved games load (ten land where they were saved; one autosave taken at a label runs on to its first line, as it must). Yuji's follows inside his install. `tools/cloth_match.py` is in the tree with its check. **No count moved.**

23. **The picture is what is clicked (7 Oct; O34, D60).** Shown the Training Yard as built so far, the owner said the buttons on the picture felt randomly placed and asked for the picture itself to be clickable once the new places are in: hover over the storehouse, it lights, click, walk there. Read in the code before anything was decided: a hotspot is an icon button on a ring at a point; the harness lines that find it by its id read its rectangle, its action or its label (and, as item 24 found, one group reads its icon); people are drawn over the back layer with a focus mask of their own pixels. **Decided:** the button keeps its place in the screen's structure and is drawn empty, and the traced shape is a second, larger focus area over the same action, so no rule about a point or a square and no harness line changes; the light is drawn from the polygon when it is needed, so no picture file is added and nothing new moves; a thing is hidden behind a person exactly when its button is today. A place is shaped only on its new picture (revision 2 or later), which is the owner's "after the new locations are added" taken place by place, and the Courtyard and the Library, at batch 0, are where the owner first sees it. The code is U0c2's, which had not been built and makes `sb_hotspots.rpy` final; the rules, the sheets and the first two layouts are U0d3's; every later shape is L's. **Not taken:** one button the size of the shape (every hotspot rectangle a harness reads would have become a building's bounding box); a cut-out picture for each light; a thing staying clickable while someone stands on its point; a shimmer on arrival (offered to the owner). **Counts after it:** owner rows to O34 (O35 the same evening, below), decisions to D60, risks to 50; no render, no sitting and no cost is added; a layout sitting is about ten minutes longer for each picture, and L's in-place series about two hours longer. **Also recorded here:** at the owner's choice, batch 1's file was written and run early, by the preview route, while the agent build waited for the weekly limit; how those renders are judged is in 10.2 (the art sittings) and risk 50. The six pictures came back the same evening and the owner chose (O35): the School Courtyard B, the Dormitory Garden A, the Training Yard B. All three read "yes" down their five-line checklists by eye, and each agrees with the chosen Courtyard, so the first test (5.2) looks passed; the rule itself is applied at the layout sitting, when `check_layout.py` exists. Two things were found. The School Courtyard's render had a black strip 131 px wide on its right edge: it is trimmed off the render (which is larger than the game's picture) and nothing is run again; a bar at an edge is now something to measure on every composed render. And the garden's version shows a second pagoda, which the owner keeps (5.2). Owner rows to O35.

24. **The check of item 23 (one reader, against the code and the engine's source; eighteen findings: four blockers, twelve major, two minor).** Each was read against the files it named; all held, and all are in the plan. *The blockers.* (1) "No harness line reads what a button draws" was false: `p2_paths.rpy:591-596` read a locked exit's lock icon and its alpha, in the Training Yard, which is shaped at batch 1, at a sitting where no harness may be edited. The line becomes a rule that holds in both kinds of place, in U0c2, and U0c2 now proves the standing set over an overlay that shapes all 35 places, not the Courtyard alone (10.3). (2) Two focus areas with one tooltip key would have failed `p2_hud`'s "every key is unique"; the shape has a key of its own. (3) "A place is shaped all at once" would have reported the harnesses' own test hotspots in real places and failed four lines of `p2_contracts`; ids that begin `zz` are left out, and a hotspot with no shape is drawn as its button. (4) The rule for whether the early pictures count could never say "keep": the installed Courtyard and the early reference are not the same bytes, and an install adds building crops that change two of the three prompts. The early pictures are now judged as any render is, with the differing sentences listed (10.2, risk 50). *The rest.* The storehouse's two hotspots at one point share a shape. A shape lives in its hotspot's layer: a back shape is under a painted pose's box and under a foreground cut-out, so a thing that is the foreground object is registered in front. The square takes no pointer and the shape lights from the tooltip in force, so the keyboard lights it too. My Room's picture stem is `your_room`, not `dorm`. What section 0 told the owner about a thing someone stands in front of was not what the code does: a look has no row in the column and simply waits, a painted person hides what they are painted at, and "above the heads" hardly exists. Section 0 now says so; the two changes offered at batch 0 that nobody could have built then (a shimmer, a thing staying clickable) are offered at checkpoint B, and the light's strengths moved to a sidecar L can write. The point and burial rules are stated so that both validators count alike. The faces at a house's exit, the names while names are shown, a `dim` reason, a thing used up for the day, the six beds at batch 27, U0d3's two more files, and how the light must be drawn to show on a white wall are each written where they belong. **Found by the same check, outside D60:** the four pictures of batch 0 are named in the record by 1920 px files that carry four black rows top and bottom, which the installer's fit lets through, so batch 0 installs from the 2752 px renders (5.13, U0d3). Batch 1's Courtyard reference was one of those files, which may be why one of its six renders came back with a black strip; batch 2's references were refitted without them. **Not disputed:** none. **Counts:** as item 23 left them, with owner rows to O35.

25. **The second ring, run early, and two rulings it brought (7 Oct; O36).** Batch 2 was written from a copy of the record in which the three chosen yards were fixed from first-guess layouts (10.2), and took three goes. The Staff Building Steps were chosen at the first. The Main Gate agreed with the Courtyard at once but read as a small shrine entrance; it was composed again as a gatehouse, and a third time from the garden path, with the gatehouse the owner liked given to the editor as a cut-out to copy. The Mountain Path's veil was first a grey wall and a patch of streaks (the record's words for it, "a faint dark curtain in the sky", gave the editor nothing to draw), then an enormous dome copied from the owner's reference picture, size and all, and at the third go, from words alone, the small dome the owner asked for. **What it taught, for every later composed picture:** the editor copies a landmark out of a reference picture unless it is told where that landmark is (the garden's second pagoda, O35; batch 2's prompts say where the tower and the pagodas are and none appeared); a reference picture carries its scale with it; a thing with no ordinary name needs its shape, material and size in plain words; and one named change on a chosen render keeps everything else. **Changed in the plan:** the gate's and the ridge's rows of 5.2 and 5.3, the gate's look (the garden pond) and Ijichi's caption, the main menu's first mock-up and its token, U1's words, section 11. The real record's rows for the gate and the ridge are proposals and are rewritten when the pictures are installed; `tools/art/staged/early/campus_for_batch2_third.json` holds the words they were painted from.

26. **The first rooms, run early, and what they taught (7 Oct; O37).** Batch 3a and 4a were written by the preview route as batches 1 and 2 had been. *The installer's check* (`install_redo.py --dry`) refused both pictures the owner chose in 3a and passed the two they did not; read by eye, each refusal was a false alarm of one of four kinds (a view changed on purpose behind a glass door; a floor drawn again with sunlight on it; the named addition sitting on a hotspot's own object; a pale thing on a wall that became lighter), which are recorded with the two renders as the tests the corrected tool must pass (`tools/art/staged/early/art_3a_early.json`); K6 stands, so the tool is corrected before they are installed. *The girls' corridor* took five goes. The owner's "doors that lead to nothing" meant doors in a wall with a window onto the outdoors, and was first misread as "no spare doors". Then the editor would not move a door: given any picture of the corridor, it copied that picture's door positions, twice, whatever the words said. The fifth go gave it one door as a swatch and composed the corridor from words, and the owner chose from it. **For every later picture:** to change a layout, do not hand the editor the old layout; and ask in plain words what a complaint about a picture points at before painting again.

27. **The first evenings and nights, run early, and the owner's ruling on a room at night (7 and 10 Oct; O38).** Batch 3b was written by the preview route from the two day pictures chosen in 3a, laid into a scratch root as the installed files would be. *The relight wording holds:* in none of the twelve pictures did anything move, and the installer's check, run without installing, passed them all (My Room's with a LOOK for the open laptop). *A relight still adds:* one lamplit night put a blanket over the sofa's arm, another a lit lamp on the desk, and one evening kept daylight in one of its two windows. The check caught none of the three, since for a relight it skips "nothing was added" by design (5.7), so a relight is always read by eye against its day picture for added things. *The owner's ruling:* of the lamplit nights the owner said the main light should be off. Both rooms were relit with an hour sentence of their own (every light off; moonlight through the windows; "dim, but everything in it can still be seen clearly"), all four pictures came back faithful, and the owner chose version A of each. That sentence is in the batch table as the two pictures' `hour_words`, so the sitting proper writes the prompt that was run. Measured, for the next dark room: the day pictures average 118 and 111 out of 255, the lamplit nights 84 to 102, and the chosen lights-off nights 69 each, with under 8% of the picture darker than 25.

28. **The Cafeteria and the corridor after dark, run early (10 Oct; O39).** Batch 4b was written by hand from the two day pictures chosen in 4a, one of them the corridor composed afresh. *Ten of ten keep their room:* nothing moved, and every corridor picture has six doors and no side window, each corridor sentence ending "Add no new window and no new light". *The installer's check refused three of the four Cafeteria pictures* because the window frames' shadow, as a grid of sunlight or moonlight, lies across the floor points where people stand; the floor had not moved (every other point agreed to 2 px). It is the seventh correction owed to the check before anything here is installed (`art_4b_early.json`). *A night sentence must say that what stands outside a window stays:* three of the four corridor nights replaced the trees outside the end window with plain starry sky; the chosen one kept them. *A corridor with one window* takes sentences of its own: the general evening sentence speaks of "the windows" and would invite one in a side wall. *One lamp of three left on* was offered as a night light and read to the owner as a lamp someone forgot; the dark, moonlit corridor was chosen. *An evening can be dramatic or correct:* the chosen corridor evening lights both side walls from one end window, which a single low sun cannot do; the owner was told and chose it for the hour it shows.

29. **The school's rooms and the first outdoor relight, run early (11 Oct; O40).** Batches 6a and 7a were written by hand as one file. *The outdoor relight holds:* in none of the Courtyard's six pictures did anything move, by the installer's fifteen points and by eye. *It adds street furniture:* two of the four nights put benches in the yard, one a notice board, and one strung lights on the pagoda although its sentence ends "add no new light"; a relight is not checked for additions (5.7), so an outdoor night is read by eye for benches, boards and lights before it is offered. *A room with no keep list is not checked for additions at all:* the classroom's version A hung school bags on four desks and stood two bookshelves by the teacher's desk, and the check, which had nothing to compare, said only LOOK for the window views. The keep list is written before a room redone in place is judged, as 5.4 has it; run early, these two were judged by eye. *Two ways at night, again:* the Courtyard was painted with every window dark and with the windows lit; both first versions were faithful, and the owner chose the dark one.

30. **The school's rooms after dark, run early (11 Oct; O41).** Batch 6b was written by hand from the two day pictures chosen in 6a. Nothing moved in any of the ten. *"What stands outside the windows is still there"* was written into both night sentences after batch 4b lost the trees outside a window, and all six nights kept theirs; the evening sentence did not say it, and one evening of four replaced the forest outside the infirmary with open sky. It belongs in every hour sentence. *Bookshelves again:* one classroom night grew two, as one classroom day had; the room's bare front wall is named in its keep list before it is installed. *A second night treatment with a reason:* the infirmary was painted dark and with someone working late (the monitor on, a ceiling light the room already has on low; one version of the two added ceiling lights and was set aside), because its night picture is the ground of two memories with Shoko at work. The owner chose the working-late one. *The owner on the window views:* "The scenery lines up outside the windows nicely" — the record's window lines, written from the chosen yards, do what 5.2 asks of them.

31. **Three rooms redone in place and refused (11 Oct; O45).** Sitting 15a and the storehouse of 16a were written by the tool's own in-place prompt and the owner refused all six: "Not just sharpened." *An in-place redo keeps what is wrong with a room:* its size, its camera and a layout made before its building had a picture. The dojo's window view was repainted from the record's line and came out as a lawn, because nothing in an in-place prompt shows the editor the yard. *So a room off a chosen yard is asked the owner's four questions before its sitting* (the map, the room, the story, the people: O45), and is composed again when today's layout fails one. *Composed from the yard:* the references are the chosen yard and a crop of the room's own building cut from it, never the room's old picture, which the editor would copy. *Sides are read from the record, not from the game:* the first draft had the two halls the wrong way round, because the comments beside the yard's exits in `room_paths.rpy` describe the old yard picture, whose sides the chosen one swapped (5.2). It was caught before the owner saw it, by reading the record's fixed rows and looking at the picture. *The story's lines are part of a room's brief:* a search of the script for each room's name found the hall "bigger inside than the building", the dojo's lamps and the bench at the back of the storehouse, none of which an in-place redo could have added.

32. **Rooms lit at Night: how D61 was designed (11 Oct; O42).** One worker read the scaffold on `ui2-u0c2` and the records, changing nothing, and answered seven questions with their lines; the design of 5.7a was then written from its report. What decided the design. *The second picture is one attribute token.* The hour's attribute is not only the picture's name: the foreground cut, the painted pose, the light row and a `view_` file's hour tables each build a file name or a key from it (`view_stage.rpy`, `lighting.rpy`), and a two-word image name would have handed the lit room the dark room's light. *`night` stays the empty room.* The frozen harness pins the Common Room's Night as `night` on a day nobody is in it (`u2_contracts.rpy:572-574`), six chosen `night` pictures are the dark ones, and the Library's lit picture was already filed apart; making `night` the lit name would have broken a line that is not edited after wave 0 and re-pointed every record. *The lit choice cannot always read the schedule.* Memory tiles call the reader at init, before any saved value exists (`sb_memories.rpy:272`), and a replay sets the place and the hour but takes the weekday from the save it is opened in; so a replay is lit by the memory's own cast and init answers the plain `night`. *Who counts.* `who_is_here` counts whoever walks with me and never counts me; had either counted, every room I walked into at Night would be lit and the lights-off pictures the owner chose would never be seen, My Room's among them. *Where a late stay can fall.* The harnesses fix a Tuesday, a Friday and a Saturday Night, and two lines loop all seven; the proposed stays keep to Sunday and Wednesday, so only the looping lines change, into the rule they stand for. *Two things found on the way,* written into 5.7a for U1: Gojo's draw still stands him in rooms at Night (D9 is not in the code), and two lock lines put a light in a shut office where the tables have nobody.

33. **The check of item 32 (one reader, against the code; seventeen findings: two blockers, seven major, eight minor).** Each was read against the lines it named; all held, and all are in 5.7a. *The blockers.* (1) The two helpers were named `night_up` and `lit_bg`, in a file whose new names must begin `world_` (`tools/prefixes.json`, which every package's acceptance runs). (2) "The light key gains the attribute" would have broken two frozen lines and was never needed: a painted picture is lit by its name, and only a tinted one carries the key. *What the design had wrong about the game.* A late Night for Maki in the dojo, taken from the script's own scene, would have opened the Grade 3 promotion fight, her first mission and "Stay the night" there, in a sentence that said nothing new was offered: her row is gone, and the dojo's lit picture is for the scene. Asking along the person who lights a room would have put its lamps out with both of us standing in it: whoever walks with me now counts in the room their own table has them in. Rows 22 to 24 of the poses are Shoko's, Gojo's and Yaga's and stay on `night`; Panda's asleep shape was missing from the dark shared room. *What it had wrong about the plan.* Five scene lines were added to U9 against 8.5, which leaves the hour a scene shows to the story phase, and against K10's and 8.1's count of seven; the design now re-points none, and puts My Room's five to the owner, because looking at the room's present night picture showed its lamps on, so the lights-off night would darken them. The contracts could gain rows only if section 9's own sentence said so, and two of the changes were not additions (the light key; two dump keys under a pinned list). The package was named U0e, a name the second draft had used and dropped. *Found on the way, and not this package's:* the pose tools read none of the scaffold's asleep, away and home tables, because the dump writes them under another key and shape (U0d3's file). *Counted exactly:* only the two looping lines fail on the proposed Nights; a dozen others cross them and hold.

34. **Three rooms composed from their yard (11 Oct; O45, O46).** The three rooms off the Training Yard were written from words as the inside of their own buildings, each given the chosen yard and a crop of its building, and came back as rooms the owner chose at the first sitting. *What the yard's picture did to the views.* In four of the six the editor painted what stands beside the building as what is seen from inside it: the storehouse beside the hall appeared through the hall's far screens, at the wrong end of the yard; the staff steps beside the storehouse appeared opposite its door. One version of the hall looked out on the hall itself. The dojo's version A was right in everything. **So a view from inside is named side by side in words** (what is on the right, what is on the left, what is at the far end), and the crop of a room's own building is cut tight to it. *One change, asked twice.* The hall's wrong view was put right by one named change in one of two tries; the second left it as it was. *A view wider than its door.* Asked to change only what is seen through the storehouse's narrow far door, to a view with a hall on each side, the editor widened the doorway to twice its width in both tries and dropped the door's leaves; the same sentence on the closer version of the room, whose door is larger in the frame, changed the view alone. **So a view through a narrow far door is asked for as a narrow view, or is placed by tool:** the two pictures shared one camera, so the part of the repainted view that fell inside the original doorway was put there by a small script, which changed no other pixel (`tools/art/staged/early/door_view_15s.py`). *Sides, again:* the first draft had the two halls the wrong way round (Method note 31).

35. **The rest of the plan brought into line with 5.7a (11 Oct; O47).** One pass over this document and no other file, after the owner's yes; 5.7a, O46, O47 and D61 were taken as true and not edited, apart from the one paragraph of 5.7a that listed this work. *Who is where.* "Everyone is in their own room at Night" now carries the two late stays wherever it is stated as the rule: section 0 (five places), O18's meaning column (the owner's words are untouched), 3.1, 4.3 (the second draft's Night cells, the Night column's heading and its rows for Megumi and Toge, and the count of who is up: Megumi in his own room on Saturday Nights only, Toge in the shared room on four weekday Nights, and a third person to talk to before any door is open on a Sunday or a Wednesday Night), 7.2 rows 16 and 19, 7.6, U1's harness words in 10.2, and 12.1 and 12.2. *Pictures.* 4.1's one shared Night picture is ended and its count is twenty-four hour pictures; 5.6's budget, 5.7's table (the friends' rooms, My Room, the dojo) and its count say 72 and point to 5.7a; D52, D25, 3.2 and 6.1 (the light key `lit`) and 9.1 follow. *The batch table.* One sitting is appended as batch 47 (the five dark rooms, ten renders, and the dojo's `lit`, two), so no earlier number moves; rows 9 to 11 say that the Night they make is the lamp-lit `lit`, and row 27 now waits for 47, because four of the asleep shapes are painted on a dark room. Added up again: 428 + 12 = 440 renders in the table and six curse renders, 446; $70.40 + $0.96 + about $5 is about $76; one in five more, $92; forty-seven sittings, twelve of them two files with no pause; 35 + 72 + 1 + 8 + 30 + 1 + 70 + 18 + 7 = 242 installed pictures, "about 240"; section 0 and "what moved the totals" agree (+90 pictures, +253 renders, +19 sittings), and the compromise of one Night picture a friend's room is marked as taken. *Poses.* 70 where the plan gave the total as it now stands (section 0's list, D32, 3.1, 5.12, 7.2, U3, 12.1, 12.2); the awake-at-home rows 16 to 21 are on `lit`, the asleep rows on `night` and Panda's and Toge's on `lit`. *Scenes.* U9's seven `scene` lines are twelve in D37, K10, 8.1 (its heading, its table as whole statements, its proof and what skipping commit 1 now costs), 8.5's stated exception, risk 14 and U9's acceptance; K7 says `scene bg your_room night` is written five times today and nowhere after U9, while the memory rows keep the name; 3.1 and 8.1 say the tiles of My Room's scenes do not follow them. *The package.* U0n is in the wave diagram, the count of packages (nineteen), 10.1, a short section of 10.2 that points to 5.7a, and three rows of the ledger; section 9's "final after U0c" names its one addition. **Found and not settled here.** (1) The three new poses have no row in 7.2, no spot and no sitting: no pose sitting has six renders free, so their 6 renders (about $0.96) stand outside 5.13's totals, said there and in section 0, and the sentences that count "the other sixty-four" poses after the test of three are left as they were. (2) 5.7a adds `story_kurose.rpy` and `story_oda.rpy` to U9's files; `ch2_tour.rpy`, which holds the first of the five lines, was not U9's either and is one of the two files the owner's menu-parked saves rest in, so it is added to U9's row and 8.1, D37 and risk 14 no longer say U9 never opens it. (3) Four of the five lines carry `with fade_slow` inline, not three (read in the files); `events_arc1.rpy:381` has it on the next line. (4) Which sitting makes which Night of a friend's room is read from 5.7a's "the dark rooms, as one sitting of their own": batches 9 to 11 make `lit`, batch 47 `night`. (5) The dojo's `lit` is written early with `art_15b.json` (row 15, O46) and counted in batch 47, where 5.7a prices it. (6) 5.7a does not say who rewords the friends' `night` rows in `tools/art/batches.json`; U0n's row in 10.1 lists only the files 5.7a names. (7) `docs/UI2_PINS.md` is not on this branch: its rows for the three ledger lines are owed after the merge, before U0n starts. (8) 10.4 and 10.5 are unchanged: no sentence in them is false, but neither has a line of its own for U0n or batch 47. (9) Left as records of their day: O28's and O38's rows, section 0's account of the yes of 7 Oct ("67 in all", "forty-six in all"), and Method notes 16 to 34.

36. **Three places off the Mountain Path, run early (11 Oct; O55).** The plan had the Forest Trail, the Riverside and the ridge redone in place; O45's four questions were asked of each first, and two failed. *A wild place is composed from a crop, not from its yard.* The Forest Trail and the ridge were each given one crop of the chosen Mountain Path (the path among the cedars; the stairs and the dome) and nothing else: not the place's own picture, whose layout the editor copies, and not the whole Mountain Path, whose house, gate and standing stone it would paint as the view (note 34). Both came back as the place the crop leads to, the ridge's stairs and dome matching the Mountain Path's. The opening sentence was the file's own: the tool's `open_yard` names halls and tiled roofs, which nothing wild should be told about. *A place whose layout is right can still be drawn again, from its own picture as the camera to keep.* The Riverside passed all four questions, but the two things clicked there were a waterfall fifty pixels wide and water that did not read as deep. Given today's picture with the tool's own sentence for a kept camera (`ref_old_camera`) and the whole place in words, one version kept to the pixel the half it was told nothing new about and painted only the half it was asked to change, so the game's fishing float and fishing spot lie where they did; the other painted everything afresh and moved the rock. Offered beside the two renders redone in place, which were today's picture, sharper, the kept-camera one was chosen. *Say what a thing is made of.* "One of its great roots rises out of the earth like a low seat" came back once as a root and once as a sawn plank between two roots. *A trail is judged by where it leads.* "Until it is lost among the trunks" gave one trail that runs on in sight and one that ends at the first bushes; the owner chose the first over the truer seat. For a path, say that it stays in sight to the far distance. *The installer's check* refused one of the two in-place renders for a flat slab of rock repainted lighter under two standing rows: one more case of the floor drawn again (note 26), handed to U0d3 with the others.

37. **The veil, the Onsen and a black band (11 Oct; O56 to O58).** *A picture the plan made "by the same edit, stronger" became a picture of its own.* `bg ridge_veil` was planned as the ridge with a stronger veil, when the ridge had none. With the dome in the chosen ridge, the same-view edit gave a dark patch above it that read as smoke; composed from the rope, in the script's own words ("I look up past the rope markers"), it is the picture both scenes stand in, and the owner chose it. *One thing repainted is placed by a mask grown from what changed.* The forest's seat was taken from its repaint as the largest connected patch that differs from the chosen render inside a window round the seat, grown by 22 px and feathered; the script asserts that every pixel outside the patch is the chosen render's own (`forest_seat_17b.py`). *A sunset reflects in a glossy black thing.* One evening of the ridge turned the dome bronze all over and the valley rust-coloured; the other kept the dome dark with the sunset on one side. "Stays as it is, black and glossy" is not enough for a relight: say what colour the thing must still be when the light is orange. *"Leave the right ninth free" was once painted as a black band.* One sauna came back with its right eighth black: in a small room with no window the editor had nothing to put there and took the sentence at its word. For such a room say what the right wall is (boards, plain), not that the ninth is free. The same render was lit like day: "lit only by its stove" held in one of two. *An in-place relight of a forest keeps the day's shadows.* Asked for shadows falling toward the camera at evening, both versions kept the day's and changed only the colour and the glow; the owner chose one, and it reads as evening.

38. **The dormitories' rooms, run early (11 Oct; O60 to O65).** *Rooms of one house.* Each friend's room was composed from words with its house cut from the chosen garden and one chosen room of the same house as "the same house inside": the five came back in their own house's plaster, timber and windows. *Lettering comes back unasked on posters.* "No lettering" in the words and "no text" in the look did not stop titles on Yuji's posters, one of them a famous film monster's name and likeness. One named change with a made-up subject for each poster cured it in one of two (the other drew a known film's walking machine), and only the posters were placed by tool. Book spines, shogi pieces and shopping bags, each said to carry nothing, came back plain. *A relight adds things.* Pot plants on a windowsill and boxes on a bookshelf, in two relit pictures of the boys' house, and the installer's check passed both: each version of a relight is read by eye against its day picture. *A lit room is lit by what the room has.* Each room was composed with one lamp of its own, off by day (Yuji's has its television), so its lit Night adds nothing. *Two rooms across a corridor do not share a view.* The record's proposals gave Nobara's and Maki's rooms the same window on the garden; their doors face each other, so one looks onto the garden and the other onto the staff buildings. *"More girly, or bland?"* Asked of the two girls' rooms, which had come back neat and nobody's. Each was drawn again from its own picture as the camera to keep, with "everything picture 1 has stays where it is" and a list of what is added: in four of four the room held still and only its owner's things arrived, and the fuller room of each was chosen. A person's room is written with that person's things in it at its first sitting.

39. **The layout tools and the first two traced places: what the build taught (11 and 12 Oct; U0d3, merged 12 Oct).** Built in a worktree in three sittings (the layout check; the installer and the stage preview; the Courtyard and the Library traced), read through two lenses twice, fixed twice, and the second round of fixes read once more by one reader, who found three more faults and mended them: fourteen commits, no file under `game/` changed, and `tools/test_art_tools.sh --strict` reads 439 lines where it read 186. *The layout check.* `tools/check_layout.py` takes every number from the dump and runs the game's own text for the shape rules (99 lines in its self-test, each refusal with a case that fails when its rule is weakened). Two rules the first draft could only report are refusals now, from numbers the game measures: a locked door's reason label (`lock_labels` in the dump) and the name tags' row (`frames.tag_edges`, `tag_gap`, `tag_w`); `u2_dump` reads 13 checks. Run on today's game it found two things for later sittings: with three people in the Common Room the row stands in front of the card table and the television (AUDIT F5; its layout sitting), and in the Boys' Dormitory's stand-in corridor the reason on Yuji's locked door would cover the door to My Room (that door is given a side under `tips` when the corridor is laid out). *The installer's check, as it stands.* It holds the wall under every traced shape's outline (9 px is refused, and the shape named once). Glass is left out of a guard window by `windows` rows of the keep list; a floor drawn again or crossed by light is a LOOK; the allow box wins over the automatic one; a pale thing on a wall that grew lighter is a LOOK. The three review rounds each took back a leniency that went too far: a standing point whose own window locks on an offset is refused; one that no longer lines up on a floor zone that is otherwise the same drawing is refused ("something stands there"); and on such a floor one 160 px part that no longer agrees with the game's is a flat thing set down (a bag, a mat) and is refused (0.75 and 0.55; measured margins beside the constants). Of a flat thing set at each standing point of the chosen Common Room and Cafeteria, 51 of 72 are refused where 22 were. **All 77 pictures chosen so far that are redone in place or relit read PASS (68) or LOOK (9), none REFUSED.** *What the check still cannot see, so it is read by eye:* anything a relight adds (5.7 never refuses one; a small `empty` row of the keep list that gained edge is shown, with thin margins: four real cases, the pot plants on Yuji's sill, the boxes on Megumi's shelf, the bag on the lounge's bar and its third lamp, pass unseen without such a row); a dark thing on dark wood; a thing that covers most of a floor (it reads as a floor drawn again); the Common Room's rows on the rug, whose zone is too small to be read in parts; and any room whose keep list has no floor zone. So before a room's in-place picture is installed its keep list gets a floor polygon under the standing points, `windows` rows where an exit or a hotspot sits on glass, and `empty` rows for sofa arms, desk tops and sills before its relights; `tools/bgs/keep/girls_dorm.json` still describes the old corridor, and `library.json`'s scale is half as large again as it should be (the staged `view_library.json` has the one to copy). *The tests stand in the tree as it was before the overhaul.* Each scratch copy the suite installs into is put back from git, picture by picture, by the manifest row's own `rev1_commit`, so the suite reads the same before and after batch 0, committed in one step or two (four lines fewer once the two staged layouts are the installed ones). It needs the checkout's whole history (a shallow one is a single FAIL line that says so) and never copies a link as a link. Each later sitting that chooses an in-place or relit picture adds its row to `EARLY_CHOSEN`; the first pose install will print a SKIPPED line naming its row, and `before_overhaul()` is then taught which file that kind of row governs. *Two checks had failed unseen.* `check_campus.py --selftest` since 7 Oct (its example was a way the game has since gained) and `check_content.py --all` since the scaffold's pictures (a test overlay's real image name): both are mended, and the first now runs in the suite. *One harness line became a rule* (`p2_view.rpy:798`, in the ledger); in a root that really holds batch 0, `u2_contracts` reads 235 with its overlay and 232 bare, and `p2_view`, `p2_rooms` and `p2_paths` pass. *The two layouts* are staged under `tools/art/staged/courtyard/` and `library/`, with batch 0's four pictures named by their 2752 px renders in `batch_0/art_0.json` (the four commands are there). The Courtyard has seven shapes (the schoolhouse, the way through the gateway, the training hall with its veranda steps, the flagstones along the bottom, the vending machine in front, and two planned: the gateway's roof as a look, the straw posts) and the Library four (the stacks, the locked cabinet, a band of floor for the way out, and the planned reading tables, both of them). No hotspot keeps a button and neither place has a foreground outline. The eye line each was laid out by is in its `check_<place>.json` (`horizon`: 748 and 554, read from crops, good to about 10 px), not in a sidecar the game reads. **For the owner at checkpoint A:** people are drawn a little smaller in the Courtyard (zoom 0.44 and 0.40) and stand further into the Library, where their name tags stay at the bottom edge about 135 px below them; the stacks' shape is the whole shelf wall and the Courtyard's buildings are traced whole; while names are shown the light is faint and the outlines are what one sees; and the tint the installer sampled for the Courtyard leans to the sky's blue. *Not built:* `stage_preview.py --propose` (6.1's slots proposed from `floor` and `scale`): the slots of these two were placed by hand and judged on the preview, and it is in no package's list. *For wave 1:* U4 registers `gateway`, `straw_posts` and `reading_table` (none in front) and runs `check_layout.py` on both places again, so that the planned name plates are measured; U3 teaches `stage_preview.py` the slot's own light and the cast shadow when the game draws them; and the map's package settles `for_rev` in an area file, which `map_validate` refuses today against 3.2.

40. **Rooms lit at Night, built: what the build settled that 5.7a left open (12 Oct; U0n, merged 12 Oct).** Two sittings (the game; the art tools), the first taken up once more because its harness would not have survived an art sitting, one review through two lenses that found no blocker and no major, and one round of fixes for its minors: six commits; under `game/` only `sb_world.rpy` and `art_manifest.rpy` changed. *As built.* `ART_HOURS` holds `lit`. `WORLD_LIT` names eight rooms (the Library, the five friends' rooms, My Room, the dojo) and `WORLD_LIT_ON` holds the rows whose file registered. `bg_variant` answers `lit` only at Night, for a registered room, when `world_lit_ready` (the file loadable and fresh against the day picture, with its own cut where the room has a foreground) and `world_night_up` both hold; otherwise it answers what it answered before. `world_lit_bg(place)` is the helper for written scenes. `WORLD_LATE` is read in `char_location` after the away table, through `world_late_cell`; `WORLD_SHUT_NIGHT` names the four places shut at Night; two validators run with lint. *Where it differs from 5.7a's letter, each for a reason:* `world_night_up(loc, per=None)` takes the hour asked, so a question about tonight put from another hour answers for tonight; the cache key's third part is `"lit"` or None, not the whole attribute, which keeps the cached path as cheap as it was; the dump's two keys are `lit_planned` and `late`; the contracts gained a section B16 beside the rows in A3, A4, B2, B3, B6 and B13; and lint also refuses a late row for someone with no routine of their own or for Gojo, and a `WORLD_LIT` row that is outdoors, shut at Night, misnamed or listed twice. *Nothing else changed,* by a scratch comparison of the commit before against the tree: 1,225 rows of `bg_variant`, `bg_name` and the graded picture are identical (35 places, five hours, seven days), and of 770 cells of who is where (eleven people, five hours, days 1 to 14) exactly four differ: Toge on the Nights of days 3 and 10, Megumi on those of days 7 and 14. The thirty saves load as before and nothing new is saved. *The harness pins rules, not today's answers (K3).* The first draft of `u2_lit` took it that the Library has no foreground cut, and failed 23 lines against an overlay that imitated batch 0; it was rewritten before the review and reads the same with the Library's pictures installed. A check on registration that could not fail in a tree where every file is fresh was given a line of its own that lays every lit room's day picture stale. *In the tools.* `lit` is a third hour wherever a tool lists the hours. `install_redo.py --relight <place> lit` writes `<stem>_lit.png`, its manifest row, its light row under `lit` and its cut where the room has an outline; it and the workflow maker both refuse `lit` for a place outside the game's table. `check_content.py --layout-files` knows the key, and refuses a `Night` key in a lit room's `slot_light` or `cast`. The sittings table says the Night pictures as data: 72 hour pictures (34 evenings, 30 nights, 8 lit) and 446 renders in batches 1 to 47, the Library's and My Room's lit rows in 7b and 3b at no render, and batch 47 marked `painted_early`, so that its page opens with "there is nothing to run". All eight chosen lit pictures pass the installer run dry (seven PASS, My Room's a LOOK, like its evening and its night). *The two halves were made to meet* in scratch roots with real pictures installed by the real tool: the Library lit on a Sunday Night with Megumi there and on a Wednesday with Toge, lit still when he is asked along, dark on a night nobody is there, dark with the lit file taken away, and its evening unchanged; My Room's place view dark on all seven Nights, and `world_lit_bg("dorm")` the lamp-on room. *Counts after the merge:* `u2_lit` 100, `u2_contracts` 245 with its overlay and 242 bare, `u2_dump` 14, the art tools' suite 466; `u2_baseline` reads its 47 lines and its six differences by design, two of them shorter by the two stays. *Open, and whose.* **For the owner, once the lit rooms are in:** on his late Night a stayer's own room stays dark even if I ask him along and walk him home, because his table has him in the Library that Night (one clause in `world_night_up` would light it; not written); and in the lit Library only the desk lamps glow, the room and whoever stands in it staying moonlit (its lit light row measures almost as the dark one's). Until the Library's pictures are installed the two stayers stand in the Library as it looks today. In a replay `world_night_up` does not ask which room (5.7a's wording; no memory sits in a two-picture room at Night today). `LIT_ROOMS` in `make_redo_workflow.py` and `check_content.py`'s reader are copies of the game's table, each held to it by a check: a room added to `WORLD_LIT` is added there too. *For L, the eight lit installs,* each after its room's day picture and with a fresh dump: the Library's `ui2_library_night_lamps` (a); My Room's `ui2_your_room_night` (a, of sitting 3b, not the lights-off pair); Yuji's `ui2_yuji_room_lit` (a); Megumi's `ui2_megumi_room_lit` (b); the shared room's `ui2_senpai_room_lit` (a); Nobara's `ui2_nobara_room_lit_b_00002`; Maki's `ui2_maki_room_lit` (a); the dojo's `ui2_dojo_lit2` (a). A room given a foreground outline after its lit picture went in has that picture hidden until the lit is installed again or its cut is made. In a lit room's `view_` file `slot_light` and `cast` are keyed by `night` and `lit`. *For U3:* the pose plan's check in `p2_view.rpy` (the ledger's Part 2c). *For U9:* My Room's five lines as `scene expression world_lit_bg("dorm")`. *For the story phase:* a mission written for Megumi or Toge is given hours, or it would open in the Library at Night.

**Still open, and where each is settled:** whether a yard composed from words and its chosen neighbour's picture agrees with it (batch 1), and whether the editor paints a map that agrees with finished places (batch 21a); whether a painted person matches the sprites in the new look (batches 5 and 20); whether a pose kept on the person view is screen-only (U0c's test); whether each yard, as painted, leaves the column at eight tiles (each layout sitting); whether the desk holds in Yaga's office (batch 12); whether a named addition and an indoor relight hold with their keep wording (batches 3a, 3b), an outdoor relight (7a) and an outdoor redo by day (8a); whether a named change holds (only if a yard fails twice); whether the two call lines keep their names when their argument changes (read in `ast.py`; proved by `u2_names` in scratch before U9 commits); which harness lines the five weekend daytime cells, the two weekend Evening cells and the Sunday afternoons touch (U0a's scratch run); whether the fixture's Bond at the harness lines that lock the girls' corridor is below 15 (U0c1's scratch run); how many of the girls' corridor's far doors can carry an exit (its layout sitting); whether `sb_smalltalk` plays as it should with the sprite already shown in a district (U5's harness); whether the owner reads the two houses by the gateway as lodges, which the record has fixed (they see it beside the garden's two versions, batch 1); whether the editor draws Megumi's Divine Dog well from words, with no reference (batch 42; if not, the row is painted without it); whether each of the 35 spots exists in the picture the owner chooses (each room's layout sitting); whether a friend who stands as a sprite for the caption line and is painted when `tky_meet` opens reads well in a district (the owner sees it at checkpoint D; the other way needs a second screen name); whether `advance_period` is the only way the hour changes on a Sunday (U0c1 reads the callers before it lays the companion's release there); whether N is free on the owner's keyboard layout (U0c's check); whether a place clicked on its picture, with nothing marked until the pointer finds it, reads easily enough, and whether the light drawn from the outline is good enough without a cut-out picture (batch 0, on the Courtyard and the Library). The saves were decoded and the pictures looked at for the second verification; line numbers were read, not run.
